## [Selective Prior Calibration Reduces Vision-Language Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29240) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Selective Prior Calibration (SPC), a method that subtracts candidate-level prior-preference estimates from image-conditioned scores with instance-dependent strength to mitigate commonsense-driven hallucinations in vision-language models. SPC improves accuracy on counterfactual images while largely preserving accuracy on matched commonsense images. This addresses a significant problem in vision-language models where commonsense priors override clear visual evidence, leading to hallucinations. The proposed method offers a training-free, selective approach that could improve reliability of multimodal AI systems in critical applications. SPC subtracts candidate-level prior-preference estimates from image-conditioned scores with an instance-dependent strength and revises the original prediction only when the resulting score pattern strongly supports an alternative. Experiments show gains generalize across CDH categories, candidate-answer permutations, and other conflict benchmarks, while rarely altering predictions on benchmarks without such conflicts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) often rely on commonsense priors, which can cause hallucinations when the visual input contradicts typical expectations, such as a hand with six fingers. Counterfactual images, which depict atypical states, are used to test whether models prioritize visual evidence over prior knowledge. Existing methods that suppress priors indiscriminately can harm performance on commonsense images where the prior is helpful.

## [CalibratedRubric: Adaptive Rubric Banks Improve LLM Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29252) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CalibratedRubric introduces a task-adaptive framework that combines Bayesian measurability filtering and item response theory (IRT)-based assembly to automatically generate compact rubric banks for open-ended LLM evaluation. On JudgmentBench, measurability filtering improves human-gold agreement from κ=0.604 to 0.743. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM evaluation by reducing reliance on costly expert-curated rubrics while improving agreement with human judgments. It offers an efficient, uncertainty-aware approach that could scale evaluation across diverse domains, benefiting researchers and practitioners who need reliable open-ended assessment. The framework uses a Beta-Bernoulli agreement posterior to estimate rubric measurability and a submodular information-coverage objective to construct compact banks. IRT-based greedy selection requires only 49 rubrics instead of 131 to reach target correlation on FinResearchBench, and task-label perturbations reduce system separation, confirming the importance of task-adaptive scoring.

**Background**: Open-ended LLM evaluation often relies on fine-grained rubrics, but expert curation is costly and hard to scale. Existing automated pipelines use strict judge unanimity and binary variance filters, which fail to distinguish measurable rubrics from informative ones. Item response theory (IRT) is a framework that separates latent ability from item properties, and submodular functions model diminishing returns, both of which are leveraged here.

The paper introduces TwT (Translation with Thought), a resource-rational framework for multi-domain machine translation that adapts reasoning effort based on input difficulty. Trained with supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning, TwT-7B and TwT-14B outperform larger state-of-the-art reasoning models while reducing token usage by 32–60%. This work addresses the inefficiency of uniform chain-of-thought reasoning in translation, offering a more cognitively aligned approach that improves both quality and efficiency. It demonstrates that smaller models can achieve state-of-the-art results with fewer resources, which is significant for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained environments. TwT is trained in two stages: first, supervised fine-tuning on difficulty-aware long chain-of-thought traces distilled from DeepSeek-R1 and rewritten by GPT-4o; second, reinforcement learning with a hybrid reward optimizing translation quality and reasoning efficiency. Evaluated on 15 benchmarks, including 3 seen and 59 unseen languages, with ablations across three backbone models.

## [Stable AR Speech Generation with Low-Frame-Rate Continuous Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29363) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Locodec, a locally encoded codec that creates low-frame-rate (8 Hz) high-dimensional (768-dim) continuous tokens, and MP-ELD, a single-token AR flow-matching framework with multi-path routing and residual classifier-free guidance. The co-designed system achieves stable long-form synthesis without external SSL/ASR models or post-training. This work addresses a core challenge in autoregressive speech generation: balancing sequence length, capacity, and stability. By showing that low-frame-rate high-dimensional continuous tokens can be stable when co-designed with the generation framework, it could enable more efficient and robust speech synthesis, impacting applications like TTS and voice assistants. The method uses 8-Hz, 768-dimensional tokens, which are much lower frame rate than typical discrete tokens (e.g., 25 Hz or higher). The Locodec shapes the representation space to improve interpolatability of a lower-dimensional core manifold and identifiability of high-dimensional coordinates, while MP-ELD uses multi-path information routing and residual classifier-free guidance to mitigate error accumulation. Experiments show competitive word error rate (WER) and stable long-form synthesis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive (AR) speech generation models predict tokens sequentially, but high frame rates or high capacity can cause distribution drift and error accumulation over long sequences. Traditional discrete tokens (e.g., from VQ-VAEs) are stable but may lose detail, while continuous tokens offer higher fidelity but are harder to model. This paper explores a middle ground: low-frame-rate high-dimensional continuous tokens, which reduce sequence length while preserving capacity, and co-designs the tokenizer and generator to ensure stability.

A new conditioned latent transport framework synthesizes DCE-MRI contrast enhancement in a single forward pass, outperforming existing models and offering a non-invasive alternative to gadolinium-based contrast agents. This innovation could reduce reliance on gadolinium-based contrast agents, addressing safety and environmental concerns, and potentially enabling contrast-free or contrast-reduced imaging workflows. It also improves downstream tumor segmentation, which is critical for breast cancer management. The method anchors the latent trajectory to pre-contrast anatomy and uses continuous time conditioning to synthesize contrast evolution at any acquisition time. In a reader study, synthesized images supported the same management decisions as real DCE-MRI in 70% of cases, and tumor segmentation Dice improved by 22.4% (0.60 vs. 0.49).

**Background**: DCE-MRI is essential for breast cancer management but relies on gadolinium-based contrast agents (GBCAs), which have safety and environmental toxicity concerns. Contrast synthesis aims to generate contrast-enhanced images without contrast agents, but existing methods struggle with temporal continuity and slow sampling. This work uses a conditioned latent transport framework to address these challenges.

## [ENTINEX: Entropic Information for Boundary Exploration in RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29419) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces ENTINEX, a novel reinforcement learning exploration method that assigns intrinsic rewards to state-space boundaries identified via entropic information, aiming to improve exploration in sparse and delayed reward environments. Experiments show ENTINEX consistently outperforms existing exploration methods. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in reinforcement learning: efficient exploration under sparse and delayed rewards. By leveraging entropic information to target boundaries, ENTINEX could enhance learning in complex real-world tasks where feedback is scarce, potentially benefiting fields like robotics and autonomous systems. ENTINEX uses entropic information to identify state-space boundaries and assigns intrinsic rewards to encourage agents to explore beyond them. The method is validated through extensive experiments in environments with sparse and delayed rewards, showing consistent improvements over baseline exploration methods.

## [AgenticRepair: Multi-Faceted Context Engineering for Vulnerability Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29422) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgenticRepair is a new framework that enhances automated vulnerability repair by engineering three types of program context—code-structure, runtime-execution, and commit-history—using specialized LLM subagents. On the SEC-Bench benchmark with 300 real-world instances, it achieves a 73% success rate, outperforming the strongest baseline by 29%. This work addresses a critical gap in automated vulnerability repair, which requires richer context than general bug repair. By demonstrating significant improvements over existing methods, it establishes multi-faceted context engineering as a promising direction for agentic security repair, potentially reducing the time and effort needed to patch security flaws. AgenticRepair orchestrates three specialized LLM subagents to engineer code-structure, runtime-execution, and commit-history contexts, which are then embedded into the memory of a dedicated repair subagent for context-conditioned patch synthesis. The ablation study confirms that the three context facets are mutually complementary, and that multi-agent scaffolding and base-model capacity each play an essential role.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Automated vulnerability repair aims to reduce the time and effort required to patch security flaws from a vulnerability triage report. Recent agentic AI approaches have shown promise in automated program repair, but vulnerability repair demands richer program context than general bug repair, including cross-file data flows, crash semantics, and commit history. AgenticRepair addresses these gaps by using specialized LLM subagents to engineer multi-faceted context, which is then used by a repair subagent to generate patches.

## [QR-STT: Stealthy Black-Box Thermal Attacks on Infrared VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29445) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces QR-Structured Thermal Triggers (QR-STT), a training-free, black-box framework that uses QR-pattern thermal perturbations to semantically attack infrared vision-language models (IR-VLMs). It optimizes module topology and rendering parameters via a three-stage gradient-free procedure, achieving targeted semantic steering while maintaining visual stealth. This work highlights a novel attack surface in the underexplored area of infrared vision-language models, which are increasingly used in security and surveillance. It demonstrates that structured thermal patterns can manipulate cross-modal semantic alignment, emphasizing the need for robustness evaluation against such attacks in real-world IR-VLM deployments. QR-STT preserves the functional regions of a QR pattern while optimizing internal modules assigned cold, neutral, or hot thermal states. Experiments on multiple CLIP-style encoders show that perturbations transfer to image captioning and VQA, causing target-consistent semantic drift in generated outputs.

## [ARCTIC: AI Code Critique System for Intent, Drift, and Spotlight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29516) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ARCTIC is an AI-powered code critique system that introduces three capabilities: intent prediction, drift detection, and code spotlight. It outperforms traditional AI review tools, achieving 0.86 F1 for intent prediction, near-perfect ordinal agreement (QWK=0.907) for drift detection, and 2.4x better quality estimation with 5x fewer tokens. This addresses a critical bottleneck in AI-assisted code review at scale, where AI agents generate code faster than human review can keep up. By focusing on correctness, security, and performance rather than style, ARCTIC could significantly improve software quality and developer productivity. The system is grounded in a six-theme taxonomy derived from 18,000 code reviews. In experimental rollout, drift scores reduced code misalignment by an additional 5.76 points (p=0.026), intent prediction received 90.2% approval, and zero defects have been attributed to self-reviewed diffs since launch.

**Background**: AI coding agents are generating code at volumes that exceed traditional peer review capacity, while existing AI review tools over-index on low-value suggestions like style and best practices. ARCTIC reframes code review around intent prediction, drift detection via backtranslation, and code spotlight to prioritize human scrutiny on critical regions.

## [TerraNova: A Foundation Model Coupling Earth and Society](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29527) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TerraNova is a foundation model that integrates 512 gridded Earth-system fields and 512 national societal indicators in their native geometries using cross-modal transformers and a hypernetwork. It introduces a novel approach to model the coupled human-Earth system without lossy averaging over political borders. This work addresses a fundamental geometric challenge in Earth system modeling, enabling a unified representation of physical and societal data. It could significantly advance AI for science, particularly in climate policy, sustainable development, and disaster response, by providing a more holistic view of the Anthropocene. The model uses dedicated encoders for location, country, time, and task, with cross-modal transformers fusing them into a shared spatiotemporal state. A hypernetwork generates a per-query decoder with an evidential head, and two contrastive objectives couple the representation: population-weighted alignment and alignment to pretrained geospatial embeddings. The frozen backbone can reconstruct dense fields from sparse observations and adapt to unseen variables in minutes on consumer hardware.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Earth-system foundation models typically operate on gridded data, ignoring political borders, while societal data is reported for administrative units. This geometric mismatch has required lossy averaging, losing fine-grained information. TerraNova overcomes this by processing both data types in their native geometries, using cross-modal transformers and a hypernetwork to generate task-specific decoders. This approach builds on recent advances in foundation models for Earth observation and hypernetworks for weight generation.

## [FriendBench: Benchmarking Familiarity Inference in Humans and MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29602) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FriendBench is a new benchmark that tests whether two people in a 20-second dyadic ice-breaker conversation are familiar or strangers, using text, audio, and video modalities. The study compares 26 models from seven companies against human panels across 96 balanced dyads, finding that the best model matches human accuracy but with different biases. This benchmark addresses a gap in evaluating social intelligence in multimodal LLMs, which is crucial for human-AI interaction and AI's ability to understand social cues. The findings highlight differences in decision biases and modality use, informing future model development and evaluation. The best model and human crowd are statistically indistinguishable on accuracy in every modality, but humans stay balanced while models lean toward 'stranger', indicating a difference in effective prior. Richer channels help both unequally, and only humans benefit from visible behavior on top of speech.

## [On-Policy Interaction Relaxes Representational Demands in Imitation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29617) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces OVI, an interactive on-policy imitation learning algorithm, and proves that expert interaction relaxes representational requirements, needing only the expert's value function rather than the full policy. It also provides a negative result showing interaction is necessary without stronger assumptions. These findings provide a theoretical foundation for why interactive and value-based methods often outperform behavior cloning in practice, potentially guiding the design of more efficient imitation learning and distillation algorithms. This is particularly relevant for large-scale applications like robotics and language model training. OVI is statistically efficient when the learner can represent the expert's value function and computationally efficient given a linear maximization oracle. Empirically, OVI outperforms BC, DAgger, and offline value-based methods, with the largest gains when the learner network is substantially less expressive than the expert's.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Imitation learning (IL) trains agents to mimic expert behavior from demonstrations, but standard methods like behavior cloning suffer from compounding errors and performance plateaus, especially when the learner cannot perfectly represent the expert's policy. Two common interventions are interactive querying of the expert and using value function estimation. This paper analyzes how these interventions affect representational requirements, showing that interaction can reduce the need to represent the full policy.

This paper introduces a new mathematical framework for prospective learning and embedded agency in multi-agent reinforcement learning, building on AIXI. It shows that self-prediction enables agents to reason about others and achieve infinite-order theory of mind. This work addresses fundamental limitations of standard reinforcement learning in multi-agent settings, where non-stationarity and embedded agency are critical. The framework extends AIXI theory to embedded agents starting from a Solomonoff prior, showing they can form consistent mutual predictions. It introduces new game-theoretic solution concepts and forms of cooperation unattainable by classical decoupled agents.

## [PAIR: Prefix-Aware Internal Reward Model for Multi-Turn Agent Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17877) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces PAIR, a two-stage reward model that combines a frozen hidden-state probe with a lightweight attention-based head to provide dense step-level rewards for GRPO training in multi-turn LLM agents. It addresses the problem of prefix contamination, where hidden-state probes degrade when conditioned on corrupted intermediate steps. PAIR offers a cost-effective and practical solution to credit assignment in multi-turn agent optimization, eliminating the need for external LLM judges, ground-truth answers at every step, or full-trajectory rollouts. This could significantly reduce training costs and improve the efficiency of RL fine-tuning for complex agentic tasks, benefiting the broader AI/ML community. The model uses a frozen hidden-state probe to estimate belief-consistency and a lightweight attention-based head to correct it toward grounded correctness, achieving the highest AUROC on contaminated trajectories at negligible inference cost. The approach is validated in multi-step settings, showing that attention-based features remain robust to contamination while hidden-state probes underperform on clean prefixes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning technique that optimizes policies by comparing groups of samples, but it relies on sparse outcome rewards, which limits credit assignment in multi-step tasks. Internal correctness probing involves training classifiers on LLM hidden states to predict properties like correctness, but existing methods assume clean inputs, which fails in multi-step agent settings where prefixes can be corrupted during exploration.

<a id="item-78"></a>
## [Role Relabeling Reveals Self-Correction Illusion in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05976) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces a training-free intervention called source-conditioned role relabeling, which changes only the message role of an erroneous claim in the chat template. Across 12 model-domain combinations, relabeling the assistant's error as an external role boosts explicit correction rates by 23 to 93 percentage points, significant in 10 of 12 settings. This finding challenges the assumption that LLMs have an inherent cognitive deficit in self-correction, suggesting instead that the failure is largely an artifact of role labeling in chat templates. It has broad implications for improving LLM self-correction capabilities and for understanding how instruction tuning and prompt design affect model behavior, potentially leading to more reliable AI systems. The intervention keeps the erroneous claim byte-identical and only varies its role among assistant, user, tool response, or system blocks. The most effective role label is domain-dependent: assistant role dominates in most math experiments, while a user message dominates in logical deduction. The study spans closed-weight APIs and open-weight models from 70B-class down to smaller families.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Chat templates structure conversations in LLMs using role indicators like system, user, and assistant, which are crucial for instruction-tuned models. Previous work showed that LLMs struggle to correct their own errors but can correct external ones, leading to the question of whether this is a capability deficit or an artifact of role labeling. This paper addresses that question by systematically varying the role label of the erroneous claim.

## [EvalSafetyGap: Unifying LLM Evaluation and Safety Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30219) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents a systematic survey and conceptual framework, EvalSafetyGap, that synthesizes 373 studies from 2018 to 2026 to unify benchmark validity and alignment failures as a shared proxy-target divergence problem under optimization pressure. It introduces a Goodhart-inspired Instability Decomposition and an Alignment Trilemma, along with an exploratory audit of ten models. This framework provides a shared vocabulary for researchers, developers, and auditors to address the measurement problem in LLM safety, potentially improving how safety is evaluated and reported. It highlights the risk of collapsing distinct evidence layers into a single safety score, which could mislead stakeholders and hinder progress in AI safety. The survey organizes evidence into an eight-stream taxonomy covering benchmark validity, contamination, dynamic evaluation, LLM-as-a-judge protocols, adversarial safety testing, reward and proxy optimization, mechanistic interpretability, and AI governance. The proposed framework formalizes the proxy-target divergence problem and suggests reporting capability, behavioral robustness, and governance disclosure as separate evidence layers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LLM evaluation and safety often rely on metrics like benchmark scores and safety tests, but these can improve without reflecting true capabilities or alignment. This is a measurement problem where proxies (e.g., benchmark scores) may diverge from the intended targets (e.g., actual safety). Goodhart's Law states that when a measure becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure, which is relevant to optimization pressure in AI training. The framework aims to address this by unifying research on benchmark validity and alignment failures.

## [LabGuard: Grounding Lab Rules into Runtime Guards for Embodied Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31045) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LabGuard introduces a language-to-execution safety suite that converts natural-language laboratory rules into executable specifications and deploys them as runtime guards for embodied scientific agents. It includes LabGuard-IR, LabGuard-Bench with 812 supervised annotations from 203 seed rules, and LabGuard-Grounder, achieving 79.4 task-scope F1 and reducing unsafe events from 39.5% to 23.8%. This work addresses a critical gap in embodied AI safety by bridging natural-language lab rules and machine-checkable runtime constraints, enabling safer autonomous laboratory agents. It has significant implications for laboratory automation and embodied AI research, potentially accelerating scientific discovery while reducing risks. LabGuard-IR defines a typed executable representation specifying fields such as constraint type, entity binding, action scope, state predicates, severity, and intervention mode. In LabUtopia, its runtime monitors integrate with ACT, keeping interventions below 0.5% while preserving task success.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Embodied AI for Science (EAI4S) involves physically situated agents that operate in laboratory environments, automating complex experimental workflows. Runtime guards are mechanisms that monitor and enforce safety constraints during execution, but translating natural-language rules into machine-checkable constraints has been a missing step.

## [Capability Convergence Hypothesis: Access Structure, Not Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14144) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Capability Convergence Hypothesis (CCH), arguing that under a fixed per-token inference budget, capability converges to an access-complete hybrid architecture rather than with scale. It identifies three resource walls—Shannon, horizon, and circuit—and reports pre-registered experiments showing a scissors gap in exact-retrieval error. This challenges the Platonic Representation Hypothesis by separating representational convergence from capability convergence, potentially reshaping how the field thinks about scaling laws and architecture design. It could influence future research directions in AI/ML, emphasizing access structure over raw scale for achieving certain capabilities. The hybrid architecture combines a compressive O(1)-state channel and a scalable verbatim-index channel, and is shown to cross all three walls under an explicit separability assumption. The paper separates proven results (access-completeness principle) from conjectures (field-level convergence trend), and reports one failed prediction with reversed direction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: The Platonic Representation Hypothesis (PRH) suggests that as models scale, representations converge to a shared model of reality. The Capability Convergence Hypothesis (CCH) extends this by arguing that capability does not follow the same convergence; instead, it depends on the architecture's access structure. The paper introduces resource walls based on information theory (Shannon), finite context (horizon), and computational complexity (circuit), and uses a witness task called the Newton's-apple problem in an infinite stream.

## [Multimodal Framework Verifies Examiner Claims in VR OSCEs with 99% Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19063) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Quality Action Assurance (QAA), a multimodal framework that verifies examiner claims in VR-based Objective Structured Clinical Examinations (OSCEs) by comparing them against a reference record built from video, VR logs, and actor annotations. QAA achieves 99.2% Actor F1 and 93.4% W@16 for temporal alignment, and detects examiner errors with 69.9% precision and 76.7% recall. This framework addresses a critical problem in medical education assessment by providing an automated way to verify examiner accuracy, potentially reducing subjectivity and bias in OSCE scoring. It could lead to fairer assessments and improved training for examiners, with implications for AI in education and medical assessment. QAA combines a constrained temporal action alignment model for action localization and actor source attribution with a large language model that extracts examiner claims and checks them against the record. In retrospective evaluation, correcting detected errors raised the share of factually correct transcripts from 39.2% to 79.2%.

## [Entropy-Scaled Trust Regions Stabilize Asynchronous RL for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22186) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Entropy-Scaled Trust Region (ESTR), a method that scales each token's off-policy deviation by its local entropy to stabilize asynchronous reinforcement learning for LLM post-training. ESTR achieves 37.34 avg@1 on BrowseComp-Plus and 95.69 on multi-turn GSM8K, matching synchronous GRPO while achieving a 2.6x speedup. This work addresses a critical instability in asynchronous RL for LLM post-training, which is increasingly important for scaling training efficiency. By revealing the entropy-dependent scaling of importance ratios, ESTR offers a novel and effective solution that could improve the stability and performance of large-scale RL training, benefiting both researchers and practitioners in AI/ML. ESTR requires no auxiliary forward passes or explicit version-switch detection, making it computationally efficient. The method consistently outperforms existing asynchronous methods across long-horizon agentic tasks and mathematical reasoning benchmarks, achieving the best train-inference consistency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Asynchronous reinforcement learning (RL) accelerates LLM post-training by overlapping rollout generation with policy optimization, but stale, off-policy data can destabilize optimization. Importance sampling ratios are used to correct the discrepancy between the sampling and target policies, but existing methods apply a uniform threshold based solely on ratio magnitude. This paper reveals that the natural scale of importance ratios varies with token entropy, leading to two distinct phenomena at low and high entropy, which magnitude-only correction fails to handle properly.

## [DynaResize: Runtime GPU Reallocation Boosts LLM Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22614) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DynaResize, a runtime GPU reallocation system, dynamically switches GPUs between rollout and training phases in disaggregated LLM post-training, improving end-to-end throughput by 66.5% and reducing execution time by 33% over the optimal static configuration. This addresses a critical bottleneck in RL-based LLM post-training, where static GPU partitioning leads to pipeline bubbles under long-tail latency. By enabling dynamic reallocation, it improves resource utilization and reduces training time, benefiting organizations that rely on large-scale RL post-training. DynaResize decomposes resizing into fine-grained operations and removes non-startup-critical work from the critical path using communicator reuse, bounded state staging, and hysteresis-based resizing. It hides 27% of role-switching overhead while preserving RL semantics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: RL-based LLM post-training often disaggregates rollout and training across separate GPU pools to improve efficiency, but static partitioning can cause pipeline bubbles when rollout latency varies. DynaResize dynamically reallocates GPUs between these stages to balance execution times, similar to phase-level multiplexing approaches like ROLLMUX, but focuses on runtime reallocation within a single job.

## [RLSVR: Extending RLVR to Open-Ended Tasks via Task Transformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23802) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Reinforcement Learning with Self-Verifiable Rewards (RLSVR), a training paradigm that transforms open-ended tasks into verifiable proxy environments, enabling self-verifiable rewards for LLM self-improvement. It instantiates RLSVR with SpyRL, a self-play method inspired by the social deduction game 'Who Is the Spy?', and demonstrates improvements on text summarization, creative writing, and mathematical reasoning. RLSVR addresses a key limitation of RLVR, which has been largely confined to math and coding due to the need for deterministic verification. By extending RLVR to open-ended tasks, it could enable scalable self-improvement for LLMs in broader domains, reducing reliance on biased human preferences or expensive reward models. The SpyRL method involves agents receiving asymmetric information, completing the same target task, and voting to identify a designated spy; since the spy identity is predetermined, voting outcomes provide fully verifiable rewards. Experiments show SpyRL outperforms existing self-improvement methods on non-verifiable tasks and yields consistent gains on verifiable reasoning tasks. Models and code are released on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method that uses automatic, rule-based checkers to provide rewards, enabling large-scale optimization for LLMs, particularly in reasoning tasks like math and coding. However, open-ended tasks often rely on human preferences or learned reward models, which can introduce bias and bottlenecks. Self-supervised learning (SSL) constructs pretext tasks to derive supervision from data itself, a principle that RLSVR adapts by transforming open-ended tasks into verifiable proxy environments.

## [Sparse Geometric MPNNs Prove Expressive for Generic Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02025) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proves that sparse geometric message-passing networks can separate generic non-isomorphic geometric graphs under certain connectivity conditions, specifically when the underlying graph is connected for rotation-equivariant features, and when it is generically globally rigid for invariant features. It also introduces a new architecture, EGENNET, that achieves these theoretical guarantees. This work advances the theoretical understanding of geometric MPNNs under sparse connectivity, which is common in real-world applications like chemistry and materials science. It provides rigorous guarantees that these models can be expressive even with limited local information, potentially guiding the design of more efficient and powerful architectures. The paper distinguishes between Equivariant Geometric Graph Neural Networks (E-GGNNs) using rotation-equivariant hidden features and Invariant Geometric Graph Neural Networks (I-GGNNs) using rotation-invariant features. For E-GGNNs, generic separation is guaranteed for any connected graph, while for I-GGNNs, it requires the graph to be generically globally rigid. The proposed EGENNET architecture is evaluated on synthetic and chemical benchmarks, showing favorable performance compared to alternatives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Geometric graphs are graphs whose nodes have 3D coordinates, common in chemistry and physics. Message-passing neural networks (MPNNs) are a popular framework for learning on graphs, but their expressive power—the ability to distinguish non-isomorphic graphs—is a fundamental question. Previous work showed that fully connected geometric MPNNs can separate generic non-isomorphic graphs, but real applications often use sparse graphs based on nearest neighbors. This paper extends those results to sparse connectivity conditions.

## [Revisiting Multi-Permutation Equivariance via Irreducible Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06665) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel methodology based on irreducible representations and Schur's lemma to characterize equivariant linear layers for permutation groups and related structures. It provides simpler derivations for existing models like DeepSets, 2-IGN, and Deep Weight Space networks, and extends to a full characterization of wreath product equivariant layers, revealing many non-Siamese layers previously overlooked. This work offers a unified theoretical framework that simplifies the derivation of equivariant layers and uncovers a richer class of layers for unaligned symmetric sets. The empirical improvements in graph anomaly detection, weight space alignment, and Wasserstein distance learning suggest practical benefits, potentially advancing geometric deep learning applications. The paper provides a complete characterization of wreath product equivariant layers, showing that many non-Siamese layers exist in certain settings, contrary to previous restrictive assumptions. The code is available on GitHub, and the methodology leverages Schur's lemma to derive results more simply than prior parameter-sharing approaches.

**Background**: In group theory, an irreducible representation is a representation that has no non-trivial invariant subspaces. Schur's lemma is a fundamental result stating that any linear map between irreducible representations that commutes with the group action is either zero or an isomorphism. These concepts are used to characterize equivariant linear layers, which are crucial in geometric deep learning for building models that respect symmetries in data.

RF-CRATE is a fully complex-valued transformer for deep wireless sensing, grounded in the complex sparse rate reduction principle and derived via the CR-Calculus framework. It demonstrates that white-box models can remain competitive with black-box counterparts, potentially influencing future RF sensing system design. The CR-Calculus framework extends Wirtinger calculus to complex-valued functions, enabling the derivation of interpretable complex-valued neural network modules.

## [Fast Feature Field (F3): Predictive Event Representation Achieves SOTA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25146) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Fast Feature Field (F3), a predictive representation for event-based camera data that learns to predict future events from past events, achieving state-of-the-art performance on optical flow, semantic segmentation, and monocular metric depth estimation at high speeds (120 Hz at HD, 440 Hz at VGA). 这项工作解决了高效表示稀疏、异步事件数据的挑战，使得机器人和计算机视觉中的实时下游任务成为可能。其在多个平台和条件下的最先进结果表明，在自主系统中具有实际部署的巨大潜力。 F3 exploits event sparsity and robustness to noise, using multi-resolution hash encoding and deep sets for efficient computation. It represents events within a contiguous spatiotemporal volume as a multi-channel image, and achieves 25-75 Hz at HD resolution for downstream task predictions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Event cameras, also known as neuromorphic cameras or dynamic vision sensors, respond to local brightness changes asynchronously per pixel, offering high temporal resolution and low latency compared to conventional frame-based cameras. Multi-resolution hash encoding is a technique that maps coordinates to learnable feature vectors via hierarchical hash tables, enabling efficient neural representations. Deep Sets is a neural architecture designed to operate on sets, ensuring permutation invariance, which is useful for processing unordered event data.

## [FetalMind: AI for Fetal Ultrasound with Salient Epistemic Disentanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12953) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FetalMind, a medical AI system for fetal ultrasound, introduces Salient Epistemic Disentanglement (SED) to improve report generation and diagnosis. It also curates FetalSigma-1M, the first large-scale fetal ultrasound report corpus with 20K reports from twelve medical centers. This work addresses the underperformance of existing medical vision-language models in fetal ultrasound, a challenging domain with multi-view reasoning and diverse diseases. By achieving +14% average gains and +61.2% higher accuracy on critical conditions, FetalMind could significantly enhance obstetric diagnostics and report automation. SED injects an expert-curated bipartite graph to decouple view-disease associations and uses reinforcement learning to steer preference selection along clinically faithful steps. The model is efficient, stable, and scalable, outperforming open- and closed-source baselines across all gestational stages.

**Background**: Medical vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual data for tasks like VQA and report generation, but most are adapted to structured adult imaging and struggle with fetal ultrasound's multi-view, heterogeneous, and disease-diverse nature. FetalMind leverages a bipartite knowledge graph and reinforcement learning to align with clinical workflow, addressing data scarcity via the FetalSigma-1M dataset.

## [AREA3D: Active Reconstruction Agent with Feed-Forward 3D Perception and Vision-Language Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05131) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AREA3D is a novel active reconstruction agent that integrates feed-forward 3D reconstruction models with vision-language guidance to autonomously select next-best views. It decouples view-uncertainty modeling from the reconstructor, enabling precise uncertainty estimation without expensive online optimization, and achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on scene-level and object-level benchmarks, especially in sparse-view settings. This work addresses limitations of heuristic-based active reconstruction methods, which often lead to redundant observations. By combining feed-forward perception with semantic guidance, AREA3D can improve reconstruction efficiency and quality, potentially influencing future research in active perception, robotics, and autonomous exploration. AREA3D unifies two complementary signals: feed-forward 3D perception for uncertainty estimation and vision-language guidance for high-level semantic cues. The framework is validated on both scene-level and object-level benchmarks, and code is planned to be released on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Active 3D reconstruction involves an agent autonomously selecting viewpoints to efficiently build accurate scene geometry, unlike passive methods that use pre-collected images. Traditional approaches often rely on hand-crafted geometric heuristics, which can be suboptimal. Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models, such as transformer-based architectures, can infer geometry in a single pass, while vision-language models provide semantic understanding that can guide view selection.

## [WebCoderBench: New Benchmark for Evaluating LLM-Generated Web Apps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02430) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WebCoderBench is a new benchmark introduced for evaluating LLM-generated web applications, comprising 1,572 real user requirements and 24 fine-grained metrics across 9 perspectives. It combines rule-based and LLM-as-a-judge methods for fully automated, objective, and general evaluation, and uses human-preference-aligned weights to produce interpretable overall scores. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM code generation evaluation by focusing on web applications with real-world requirements and interpretable metrics, which is highly relevant to AI/ML and software engineering. It provides a comprehensive and automated way to assess and compare LLMs, enabling developers to optimize models in targeted areas. The benchmark includes 1,572 real user requirements collected from an online LLM service of an industrial partner, covering diverse modalities and expression styles. Experiments across 12 representative LLMs and 2 LLM-based agents show that no single model dominates across all metrics, highlighting the need for targeted improvements.

## [Jasper: GPU-Native ANNS with Quantization and Fast Updates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07048) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Jasper, a GPU-native approximate nearest neighbor search (ANNS) system that achieves high query throughput and updatability. It presents a CUDA batch-parallel construction algorithm for lock-free streaming insertions, a GPU-efficient RaBitQ quantization implementation reducing memory footprint up to 8x, and an optimized greedy search kernel. Jasper addresses critical limitations in existing GPU-accelerated ANNS systems, such as slow batch updates, lack of efficient quantization, and poor compute-memory overlap. Its improvements could significantly benefit real-world applications that require fast, up-to-date similarity search on evolving datasets, potentially setting a new standard for GPU-based ANNS. Jasper builds on the Vamana graph index and achieves up to 1.84x higher query throughput than CAGRA, with up to 80% peak utilization per the roofline model. It constructs indices an average of 7x faster than CAGRA and provides updatability that CAGRA lacks, while delivering 10-74x faster queries than BANG, the previous fastest GPU Vamana implementation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Approximate nearest neighbor search (ANNS) is a fundamental problem in machine learning and information retrieval, where the goal is to find data points closest to a query in high-dimensional spaces. GPUs offer massive parallelism for distance computations, but existing GPU ANNS systems struggle with evolving datasets, memory bandwidth limitations, and data-dependent memory access patterns. Quantization techniques like RaBitQ reduce memory footprint but often introduce random memory accesses, and greedy search in graph-based indices makes compute-memory overlap challenging.

<a id="item-96"></a>
## [GeoRA: Geometry-Aware Low-Rank Adaptation for RLVR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09361) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GeoRA introduces a geometry-aware low-rank adaptation method specifically designed for Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR), exploiting the anisotropic and compressible structure of the RL update subspace via SVD to initialize low-rank adapters while freezing residual components. Experiments on Qwen and Llama models from 1.5B to 32B parameters show consistent improvements over strong low-rank baselines in mathematics, medicine, and coding tasks. This work addresses a critical gap in parameter-efficient fine-tuning for RLVR, which has distinct optimization dynamics compared to supervised fine-tuning. By preserving pre-trained geometric structures and enabling efficient dense computation, GeoRA could improve the efficiency and performance of large-scale reasoning models, benefiting the broader AI/ML community. GeoRA uses Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) to extract principal directions of the RL update subspace for initializing low-rank adapters, while freezing residual components as a structural anchor. The method is evaluated on models up to 32B parameters, showing stronger generalization and less forgetting on out-of-domain tasks compared to baselines like PiSSA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Low-rank adaptation (LoRA) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained weights and injects trainable low-rank matrices, reducing trainable parameters. RLVR is a paradigm for improving reasoning models using verifiable rewards, but existing low-rank methods like PiSSA are designed for supervised fine-tuning and may not suit RLVR's optimization dynamics. GeoRA leverages the geometric structure of the RL update subspace to address this gap.

## [Iterative RAG Outperforms Ideal Evidence in Scientific Multi-hop QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19827) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first controlled, mechanism-level diagnostic study showing that iterative retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) can surpass an idealized static upper bound (Gold Context) in scientific multi-hop question answering. Using a new chemistry-focused dataset, ChemKGMultiHopQA, the authors benchmark eleven state-of-the-art LLMs and report gains up to 25.6 percentage points for iterative RAG over Gold Context. This work provides crucial insights into when and why iterative retrieval-reasoning loops are beneficial, which can guide the design of more effective RAG systems for specialized scientific domains. The findings challenge the assumption that providing all relevant evidence at once is optimal, potentially impacting future RAG architectures and deployment strategies. The study evaluates three regimes: No Context, Gold Context, and Iterative RAG, and analyzes retrieval coverage gaps, anchor carry drop, query quality, composition fidelity, and control calibration. Failure modes include incomplete hop coverage, distractor latch trajectories, early stopping miscalibration, and high composition failure rates even with perfect retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) extends large language models (LLMs) by retrieving external information to augment their parametric knowledge. Multi-hop question answering requires synthesizing information from multiple sources across several reasoning steps. Iterative RAG alternates between retrieval and reasoning, potentially improving performance on complex queries. This study focuses on scientific domains where evidence is sparse and heterogeneous, using a new chemistry dataset to isolate genuine retrieval needs.

AIvilization v0 introduces a publicly deployed large-scale artificial society with tens of thousands of agents, featuring a unified LLM-agent architecture that includes hierarchical branch-thinking planning, adaptive agent profiles with dual-process memory, and a human-in-the-loop steering interface. The system demonstrates stable markets reproducing real economic stylized facts and coherent long-horizon agent portraits. This work addresses key challenges in long-horizon autonomy and adaptive behavior for LLM agents, offering a scalable framework for social simulation and multi-agent systems. Its design choices, such as hierarchical planning and dual-process memory, could influence future AI agent architectures and applications in economics, sociology, and interactive simulations. The environment integrates physiological survival costs, non-substitutable multi-tier production, an AMM-based price mechanism, and a gated education-occupation system. Controlled ablation experiments show robustness in multi-objective, long-horizon settings, and human steering is associated with measurably larger short-horizon profile updates.

The paper introduces DBench-Bio, a dynamic and fully automated benchmark for evaluating LLMs' biological knowledge discovery ability, using a three-stage pipeline to generate monthly-updated QA pairs from authoritative paper abstracts. It addresses data contamination and outdated static benchmarks by providing a living resource covering 12 biomedical sub-domains. This work provides the first dynamic, automatic framework for assessing AI's new knowledge discovery capabilities, which is crucial as static benchmarks become quickly outdated and contaminated. It could catalyze the development of knowledge discovery in AI research and offer a more reliable evaluation method for the community. DBench-Bio employs a three-stage pipeline: data acquisition of rigorous paper abstracts, QA extraction using LLMs to synthesize hypothesis questions and answers, and QA filtering for quality. The benchmark is instantiated to cover 12 biomedical sub-domains and updates monthly, with extensive evaluations of state-of-the-art models revealing current limitations.

## [Stem: Sparse Attention Module for Efficient Long-Context LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06274) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Stem introduces a plug-and-play sparse attention module that employs a Token Position-Decay strategy and an Output-Aware Metric to reduce quadratic complexity while preserving causal information flow. It achieves superior accuracy with reduced computation and pre-filling latency. This addresses a critical bottleneck in scaling LLMs to long contexts, especially during pre-filling. By aligning sparsity with causal information flow, Stem offers a practical efficiency gain that could enable longer context processing in real-world applications. The Token Position-Decay strategy applies position-dependent top-k selection to retain initial tokens, while the Output-Aware Metric prioritizes high-impact tokens based on approximate output magnitude. The method is training-free and designed as a drop-in module for existing models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Self-attention in transformers has quadratic computational complexity, which becomes a bottleneck for long sequences. Sparse attention methods reduce this by computing attention scores for only a subset of token pairs, but existing methods often ignore the cumulative dependency of initial tokens in causal architectures. Stem rethinks this by considering information flow in causal attention.

## [Step Grounding Rate Predicts OOD Generalization in Long-Horizon VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06828) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Step Grounding Rate (SGR), a metric that quantifies temporal visual grounding in long-horizon vision-language models (VLMs). Across eight models and three benchmarks, SGR predicts out-of-distribution retention with r=0.83 (p=0.003), revealing a behavioral law that grounded models generalize better. This finding provides a new evaluation axis for VLMs beyond final-answer accuracy, which can mislead when models guess correctly without visual grounding. It could influence model evaluation, safety, and design, especially for long-horizon reasoning tasks in robotics and autonomous systems. SGR varies by up to 10.8 percentage points among parameter-matched 7B models despite similar accuracy, indicating it captures an independent capability. Robustness checks show counterfactual traces drop SGR by 26–41 points, cross-architecture verifiers agree at ρ=0.96, and the predictor remains strong without explicit reasoning disclosure (r=0.78).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon vision-language models process sequences of visual inputs over time, requiring temporal grounding to maintain consistent beliefs. Standard benchmarks often only measure final accuracy, which can obscure whether intermediate reasoning is truly anchored to visual evidence. SGR measures the proportion of reasoning steps that are grounded in the current visual state, providing a behavioral faithfulness metric.

ELISA includes an automatic query classifier that routes inputs to gene marker scoring, semantic matching, or reciprocal rank fusion pipelines. <a id="item-103"></a>
## [CAP-TTA: Test-Time Adaptation for Efficient LLM Debiasing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13683) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CAP-TTA is a novel test-time adaptation framework that triggers context-aware LoRA updates only when a bias-risk score exceeds a threshold, using an offline precomputed diagonal preconditioner for fast and stable optimization. It effectively reduces toxicity/bias scores with lower latency than standard optimizers like AdamW or SGD, while improving narrative fluency and preventing catastrophic forgetting. This work addresses a critical limitation of debiased LLMs: their failure on out-of-distribution (OOD) and high-bias prompts. By enabling real-time, efficient correction at test time, CAP-TTA offers a practical solution for AI safety and deployment, potentially reducing the need for costly retraining and improving the reliability of LLMs in dynamic environments. CAP-TTA uses a bias-risk score to decide when to apply LoRA updates, and an offline precomputed diagonal preconditioner to accelerate optimization. It demonstrates strong empirical results across multiple benchmarks and human evaluations, showing reduced latency, improved fluency, and no compromise on debiasing performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time adaptation (TTA) is a technique that allows machine learning models to adjust themselves after deployment when encountering new data, addressing distribution shifts. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that freezes pre-trained weights and injects trainable low-rank matrices, reducing computational cost. A preconditioner in optimization improves the condition number of the problem, leading to faster convergence. This paper combines these concepts to debias LLMs efficiently at test time.

## [Video Reasoning Emerges via Chain-of-Steps, Not Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16870) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper challenges the prior Chain-of-Frames (CoF) assumption, showing that reasoning in video diffusion models primarily emerges along the denoising steps, termed Chain-of-Steps (CoS). It also identifies emergent behaviors like working memory, self-correction, and perception-before-action, and proposes a Training-Free Ensemble (TFE) method. This work provides the first systematic dissection of video reasoning mechanisms, offering a new framework that could guide future research in exploiting diffusion models as a substrate for intelligence. It has potential impact on AI interpretability and the development of more capable video generation and reasoning systems. The study uses qualitative analysis and probing experiments on Diffusion Transformer layers, finding that middle layers conduct key reasoning procedures. The proposed TFE method improves reasoning by ensembling latent trajectories from identical models with different random seeds, serving as a proof-of-concept.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Video diffusion models generate videos by iteratively denoising random noise, and recent work has shown they exhibit reasoning capabilities. Prior explanations assumed reasoning unfolds sequentially across frames (Chain-of-Frames), but this paper reveals it actually happens along the denoising steps, where models explore multiple solutions early and converge later.

## [llvm-harness: First Agentic Harness for LLVM Compiler Bug Fixing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20075) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces llvm-harness, the first agentic harness designed to assist LLM agents in understanding and fixing compiler bugs, along with llvm-bench, a benchmark of 334 reproducible LLVM middle-end bugs, and a specialized mini agent llvm-autofix-mini. Evaluation shows that with llvm-harness, frontier models improve their bug-fixing performance by 62%, and llvm-autofix-mini outperforms the enhanced state-of-the-art by 22%. This work addresses the challenging problem of automated compiler bug fixing, which is critical for software reliability. By providing a specialized harness and benchmark, it enables LLM agents to tackle complex compiler bugs more effectively, potentially accelerating compiler development and reducing manual debugging effort. The harness includes agent-friendly LLVM tools and a tailored mini agent, llvm-autofix-mini, for automatic fixing of LLVM middle-end bugs. The benchmark focuses on the middle end of LLVM, which is one of the most widely used compiler infrastructures, and the paper also highlights open challenges in applying LLMs to compiler engineering tasks.

LLVM is a widely used compiler infrastructure with a middle-end that performs optimizations on LLVM IR. An agentic harness is the software layer around an LLM that enables it to act as an agent by managing tools, memory, and feedback loops, which is essential for multi-step tasks like bug fixing.

## [FB-MEBE: Entropy-Based Exploration Boosts Zero-Shot Sim2Real Quadruped Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25464) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces FB-MEBE, an online zero-shot reinforcement learning algorithm that combines unsupervised behavior exploration with a regularization critic to improve sim2real transfer for quadrupedal control. It enhances data diversity by maximizing the entropy of the achieved behavior distribution, leading to better downstream task performance and natural policies deployable to hardware without fine-tuning. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in zero-shot RL for real-world robotics: the quality of pretraining data. By improving exploration in the online setting, FB-MEBE enables more practical deployment of quadrupedal robots, potentially accelerating the adoption of RL in physical systems. FB-MEBE builds on the Forward-Backward (FB) algorithm and introduces a regularization critic to shape policies toward natural behaviors. The method is validated in simulated downstream tasks and demonstrated on real hardware, showing improved performance over other exploration strategies.

Sim2real transfer refers to training policies in simulation and deploying them on physical robots, which is challenging due to the reality gap. This paper tackles the issue of low-diversity data from undirected exploration in online zero-shot RL.

## [Alibaba Field Experiment Reveals Nuanced Effects of Generative AI in Customer Service](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29888) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A large-scale field experiment at Alibaba found that generative AI assistance improves service speed and subjective quality (customer ratings) but has no significant effect on objective quality (customer retrials). The study also shows that lower-performing agents benefit most, while top performers experience declines in quality. This provides causal evidence on generative AI's real-world impact in customer service, highlighting that benefits are not uniform and can even be negative for top performers. It underscores the need for careful deployment and performance evaluation tailored to agent skill levels. The AI assistant drafts issue diagnoses and solution proposals only in the opening stage, and agents can adopt, modify, or disregard them. The study measures both access and usage effects, and finds that top performers' decline is consistent with workflow disruption, including increased shift-away time and response delays.

## [ActionParty: Multi-Subject Action Control in Generative Video Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02330) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ActionParty introduces a multi-subject action-controllable world model for generative video games, using subject state tokens and a spatial biasing mechanism to enable simultaneous control of up to seven players across 46 diverse environments in the Melting Pot benchmark. This work addresses a critical limitation in video diffusion world models, which previously could only control a single agent, by enabling multi-subject action binding. It has significant implications for interactive AI, generative gaming, and multi-agent simulation, potentially advancing the development of more dynamic and controllable virtual environments. The model introduces subject state tokens, latent variables that persistently capture each subject's state, and jointly models them with video latents using a spatial biasing mechanism to disentangle global frame rendering from individual action-controlled updates. Evaluations on the Melting Pot benchmark show significant improvements in action-following accuracy and identity consistency, with robust autoregressive tracking of subjects through complex interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Video diffusion models are generative models that create videos by iteratively denoising random noise, often conditioned on text or other inputs. World models in AI build internal representations of environments to predict future states based on actions, enabling agents to plan and reason. However, existing video diffusion world models have been limited to single-agent control, struggling with action binding—associating specific actions with the correct subjects in multi-agent scenes. ActionParty tackles this by using subject state tokens and spatial biasing to enable multi-subject control.

## [Compiled AI: Deterministic Code Generation for LLM Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05150) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'compiled AI', a paradigm where LLMs generate executable code during a compilation phase, after which workflows run deterministically without further model calls. It presents a system architecture, a four-stage validation pipeline, and an evaluation framework, demonstrating significant token savings and high reliability on function-calling and document intelligence tasks. This approach addresses critical concerns for high-stakes enterprise and healthcare applications, where reliability, auditability, and cost efficiency are paramount. By shifting from runtime inference to compiled code, it could enable more predictable and secure LLM-based automation, potentially accelerating adoption in regulated industries. On function-calling (BFCL, n=400), compiled AI achieves 96% task completion with zero execution tokens, breaking even at ~17 transactions and reducing token consumption by 57x at 1,000 transactions. On document intelligence (DocILE, n=5,680), the Code Factory variant matches Direct LLM on key field extraction (KILE: 80.0%) and achieves the highest line item recognition accuracy (LIR: 80.4%). Security evaluation across 135 test cases shows 96.7% accuracy on prompt injection detection and 87.5% on static code safety analysis with zero false positives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based workflow automation typically relies on runtime inference, which can be costly, non-deterministic, and hard to audit. Prior work like DSPy optimizes declarative LM pipelines, while LLM+P combines neural and symbolic planning. Compiled AI builds on these ideas by constraining generation to narrow business-logic functions embedded in validated templates, trading runtime flexibility for predictability and efficiency.

## [Reinforced Mode Regulation Mitigates LLM Mode Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00435) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Reinforced Mode Regulation (RMR), a lightweight inference-time intervention that applies low-rank damping to the Transformer value cache to prevent geometric collapse and mitigate mode collapse in LLM generation. RMR enables stable generation at entropy rates as low as 0.8 nats/step, whereas standard decoding typically collapses near 2.0 nats/step. This work provides a novel dynamical-systems perspective on mode collapse, suggesting that it is not merely a token-level issue but a geometric collapse in the representation space. The lightweight nature of RMR could make it widely applicable to improve diversity and stability in text generation across various LLMs, potentially impacting applications like chatbots and content generation. RMR is implemented as a low-rank damping operator applied to the value cache along dominant self-reinforcing directions, identified via eigenvalue analysis. The method is validated across multiple large language models, showing substantial reduction in mode collapse without degrading text quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Mode collapse is a known problem in generative models where the model produces repetitive or low-diversity outputs. In autoregressive text generation, this manifests as looping or premature convergence. The paper treats the generation process as a dynamical system and argues that mode collapse corresponds to the internal trajectory becoming confined to a low-dimensional subspace, termed geometric collapse. The Transformer's value cache is considered part of the state, and RMR intervenes to prevent this collapse.

## [Nonlinear SVD Theory for Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06938) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proves that most modern neural architectures admit a nonlinear singular value decomposition (NLSVD) with norm-preserving properties, without changing input-output behavior. It introduces a flexible architecture for explicit decomposition at training time and a data-driven algorithm for estimating the representation from trained models. This work extends singular value decomposition theory to nonlinear neural networks, providing a new theoretical framework for understanding latent spaces and network interpretability. It enables direct calibration of distances in latent space to input space, with practical applications in visualization, bias detection, and membership-inference robustness. The factorization represents the network as a left-invertible nonlinear map followed by a final linear layer, where the left-invertible factor is norm-preserving. The paper also establishes mathematical foundations for nonlinear analogues of row and null spaces, and includes empirical case studies on latent-space pullback, bias detection, and membership-inference robustness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Singular value decomposition (SVD) is a fundamental linear algebra tool that decomposes a matrix into singular values and vectors, revealing its structure. In neural networks, SVD has been used for weight initialization and compression, but it applies only to linear maps. This paper builds on the generalized SVD theory of Brown et al. [2025] to extend SVD to nonlinear maps, which is crucial for analyzing modern deep networks that are inherently nonlinear.

## [DySink: Dynamic Frame Sinks for Autoregressive Long Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21028) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DySink introduces a retrieval-based framework that dynamically selects visually relevant historical frames as frame sinks, coupled with an anomaly gate to filter out context prone to sink collapse. Experiments on 50-100 second videos show it achieves the highest temporal quality among evaluated autoregressive baselines. This addresses a critical limitation in autoregressive long video generation, where static early-frame sinks become outdated and can cause content regression. By making long-range context adaptive, DySink could improve the quality and coherence of long-form video generation, benefiting applications like film production and virtual environments. DySink maintains a compact memory bank and uses adaptive retrieval to select dynamic frame sinks, while the anomaly gate filters out retrieved context with excessive inter-head consensus, an attention pattern linked to sink collapse. The method also addresses RoPE-induced phase re-alignment that can homogenize inter-head attention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive video generation typically uses bounded-memory streaming for efficiency, combining local windows for short-term continuity with static early-frame sinks as long-range anchors. However, this fixed allocation can become less adaptive as the visual state diverges, and RoPE-induced phase re-alignment may cause sink collapse. DySink improves upon this by dynamically selecting relevant historical frames as sinks.

## [MemForest: Write-Efficient Agent Memory with Hierarchical Temporal Indexing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23986) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MemForest introduces a memory framework for LLM agents that uses parallel chunk extraction and a hierarchical temporal index called MemTree to enable faster, write-efficient memory updates. It achieves up to 6.0x and 9.5x higher build rates compared to EverMemOS on LongMemEval-S and LoCoMo benchmarks, respectively, while maintaining competitive answer quality. This work addresses a key bottleneck in long-context LLM agents: the latency between new information and its availability for querying. By decoupling memory construction from sequential LLM passes and localizing maintenance, MemForest could significantly improve the scalability and responsiveness of agent memory systems, benefiting applications like conversational AI and autonomous agents. MemTree organizes memory as time-ordered trees, replacing global rewrites with localized dirty-path refresh, which can be done in parallel across nodes and trees. Experiments used Qwen3-4B, Qwen3-30B, and Gemma-4-12B-IT models; with Qwen3-30B, MemForest reached 81.8% pass@1 on LongMemEval-S and 84.09% on LoCoMo categories 1-4, within 0.13 percentage points of EverMemOS.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often need persistent memory to maintain state across interactions, but traditional systems update memory sequentially, causing delays. MemForest reformulates agent memory as a write-efficient temporal data-management problem, using parallel extraction and a hierarchical temporal index to speed up updates. This approach is analogous to database indexing techniques, where hierarchical structures reduce the cost of updates.

## [APPO: Fine-Grained Credit Assignment for Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12384) ⭐️ 8.0/10

APPO (Agentic Procedural Policy Optimization) is a new reinforcement learning method that shifts branching and credit assignment from coarse interaction units to fine-grained decision points in the sequence. It uses a Branching Score combining token uncertainty with policy-induced likelihood gains to select branching locations, and introduces procedure-level advantage scaling to distribute credit across branched rollouts. This method addresses a key limitation in agentic RL by improving credit assignment, which is crucial for training LLM agents on complex multi-turn tasks. Experiments on 13 benchmarks show consistent improvements of nearly 4 points over strong baselines, potentially advancing the field of LLM agents and reinforcement learning. The pilot analysis revealed that influential decision points are broadly distributed throughout the sequence, not just at tool calls, and token entropy alone is not a reliable indicator of impact. APPO filters out spurious high-entropy positions and maintains efficient tool-calls and interpretability.

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning (RL) trains LLM agents to make multi-turn decisions and use tools. Traditional credit assignment methods often use coarse units like tool-call boundaries, which can miss important intermediate decisions. APPO improves this by focusing on fine-grained decision points, using a Branching Score and procedure-level advantage scaling.

## [Implicit Reasoning Outperforms Explicit CoT in LLM-Based Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14142) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper systematically analyzes the limitations of explicit reasoning pipelines in LLM-based generative recommendation and proposes PauseRec, a lightweight implicit reasoning paradigm. PauseRec outperforms standard explicit CoT methods by up to 6.22%, reduces training cost by up to 65% GPU hours, and speeds up inference by up to 71.3%. This work challenges the prevailing assumption that explicit reasoning is necessary for LLM-based generative recommendation, offering a more efficient and effective alternative. It could significantly reduce computational costs and improve recommendation quality, influencing future research and practical deployments in recommender systems. The paper identifies three key limitations of explicit reasoning: weakened world-knowledge verbalization, misalignment between Semantic ID (SID) and natural-language token embedding spaces, and sensitivity to rationale quality. PauseRec avoids costly reasoning trace acquisition and reasoning alignment training, making it exceptionally practical.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly used as backbones for Generative Recommendation (GR), where items are often represented by Semantic IDs (SIDs) that disrupt the natural-language reasoning interface. Existing approaches use expensive multi-stage pipelines to ground SIDs and elicit explicit rationales, but this paper shows that implicit reasoning can be more effective. Implicit reasoning, unlike explicit chain-of-thought, does not require generating intermediate natural-language steps, potentially reducing cost and improving performance.

## [Metanym Game: Contamination-Resistant LLM Benchmark with Spectral Scoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21008) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The Metanym game introduces a self-contained, self-consistent LLM benchmark for structural intelligence that avoids test-set leakage by having contestants generate all content. It also presents a spectral solution using singular value decomposition to evaluate factual accuracy without golden keys, correlating with GPQA Diamond at r = 0.92. This benchmark addresses a critical problem in AI evaluation—test-set contamination—by design, making it more robust over time. The spectral method for factual accuracy could change how LLMs are evaluated, especially in peer-review settings, and the dissociation between generation and judging skills offers new insights into model capabilities. The benchmark uses a council-of-peers mechanism where the strongest models form an official benchmarking council with contestable seats. The spectral analysis derives both generation and judging competence from a single SVD of the ratings matrix, and the factual rating correlates strongly with GPQA Diamond (r = 0.92).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LLM benchmarks often suffer from test-set leakage, where models are trained on benchmark data, inflating scores. The Metanym game avoids this by having models generate their own content, making it contamination-resistant. Spectral analysis, typically used in graph theory, is applied here to decompose the ratings matrix, providing a principled way to estimate latent abilities without ground truth.

## [ECHO: Selective Turn Memory for Traceable Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31650) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ECHO introduces a selective turn-memory framework that compresses each completed environment turn into a source-indexed memory record, enabling traceable context reconstruction for credit assignment in agentic reinforcement learning. On BrowseComp-Plus, ECHO achieves 43.4% held-out accuracy, outperforming GRPO (28.9%) and SUPO (36.1%) while using fewer turns and lower trajectory volume. This addresses a critical mismatch in agentic RL: policies act on reconstructed context while learners lack source-level provenance for credit assignment. By enabling traceable credit routing, ECHO could improve the training efficiency and generalization of LLM-based agents in long-horizon tasks, impacting fields like tool use, web navigation, and multi-step reasoning. ECHO compresses each completed tool-use turn into a compact memory record with a source-turn pointer, reconstructs bounded policy contexts by selecting useful records, and reuses selected source indices to route positive outcome credit to the final trajectory segment, reused evidence turns, memory findings, and memory-selection actions. The trained policy also improves zero-shot generalization across multi-objective QA, code generation, and deep information-seeking benchmarks on both dense and MoE backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon language agents must repeatedly interact with tools and make decisions under bounded context windows, often using context-management methods like deletion, folding, or memory editing. However, collapsing history into compressed states can lose source-level provenance, making it hard to assign credit to the evidence that mattered. ECHO addresses this by maintaining source-indexed memory records that preserve traceability while compressing context.
