## [CPInj Attack Exposes Prompt Injection Risks in Collaborative Prompt Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18622) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Collaborative Prompt Injection (CPInj), a novel attack that injects malicious instructions into the global prompt of Textual Collaborative Prompt Optimization (TCPO), a decentralized extension of TextGrad. The attack successfully degrades downstream task performance and evades existing defenses, while the paper also introduces a defense called Anchored Purification Aggregation (APAgg). This work highlights a critical vulnerability in the emerging area of collaborative prompt optimization, which is increasingly used in decentralized AI systems. The findings underscore the need for robust security measures in such frameworks, as current defenses are insufficient against CPInj, potentially affecting many applications relying on shared prompt optimization. The attack targets the collaborative optimization loop, requiring malicious instructions to survive aggregation, persist through benign optimization, and evade server-side defenses. Experiments across three LLM families and five reasoning tasks show CPInj outperforms existing attacks like CatAttack and GCG, while APAgg only partially mitigates the threat.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Textual Collaborative Prompt Optimization (TCPO) extends TextGrad, a framework that uses textual gradients to optimize prompts for large language models, to a decentralized setting where multiple clients jointly improve prompts without sharing raw data. Prompt injection is a known attack vector where malicious instructions are embedded in inputs to cause unintended model behavior, similar to SQL injection. This paper explores how such attacks can be adapted to the collaborative optimization process, where the global prompt is aggregated from local updates.

## [GEAR: Evidence-Aware Reward Shaping to Curb Repetitive Copying in Long-Context LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19345) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies repetitive copying as a pervasive failure mode in long-context LLM reasoning and proposes GEAR (Grounding Evidence-Aware Reward), a reward shaping method that augments accuracy signals with a grounding reward for key evidence and a penalty for distractor context. Experiments show consistent improvements of up to +4.6 average points over standard RL across multiple model scales and benchmarks. This work addresses a critical limitation in long-context reasoning, where models often copy input text instead of solving problems, which degrades performance and wastes computational resources. By improving grounding, GEAR could enhance the reliability and efficiency of LLMs in complex, long-context tasks, benefiting applications like document analysis and multi-hop reasoning. GEAR combines an accuracy reward with a grounding reward for overlap with key evidence and a distractor penalty for overlap with irrelevant context. The authors also develop an automated pipeline to construct evidence-annotated training data from arbitrary documents, enabling GEAR on natural-language data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context LLMs are increasingly used for complex reasoning tasks that require processing large amounts of text. However, these models often exhibit repetitive copying, where they copy text from the input into their reasoning traces instead of generating novel reasoning steps. This behavior is linked to insufficient grounding, meaning the model fails to focus on task-relevant evidence. Reward shaping is a reinforcement learning technique that provides additional training signals to guide the model toward desired behaviors, and GEAR applies this to encourage evidence grounding.

This paper introduces LENS, a contextualization-based evaluation protocol, and the SCE score to assess narrative unlearning in LLMs, tested on disinformation frames across four multilingual models. This work addresses the critical challenge of suppressing disinformation narratives in LLMs, providing a diagnostic tool that can guide safer model alignment and unlearning practices, with implications for AI safety and trustworthiness. The study evaluates two source-grounded narratives (Russia-Ukraine war and US-Taiwan relations) across four near-12B multilingual models: Lapa LLM, Gemma-12B, Qwen-14B, and TAIDE-Gemma. The SCE score rewards target-narrative suppression while penalizing degraded outputs, and the results show suppression can transfer beyond direct forget prompts, with entity recovery as a side effect.

This paper introduces a three-tier (platform/squad/mission) compositional runtime verification framework that detects mission-level violations in LLM-assisted ISR swarms, which per-platform guardrails miss. It aggregates per-platform verdicts over a verification-aware messaging fabric and uses an evidence-aware algebra to handle lost or delayed evidence. This work addresses a critical safety gap in LLM-assisted autonomous swarms, where individually compliant actions can compose into mission-level violations. It provides a formal method to detect such violations, enhancing trust and safety in contested environments, and could influence future AI safety and swarm robotics standards. The framework decomposes mission policies into per-agent and cross-agent aspects, and fuses verdicts using a two-axis (security × completeness) algebra with provenance. In a simulated ISR mission, an indirect prompt injection causing LLM planners to split a prohibited task across four platforms was detected compositionally, while a best-effort central monitor emitted silent false all-clears.

## [Role-Stratified Conformal Risk Control for LLM Tool Calls](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24343) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces role-stratified per-field conformal risk control, a calibration layer that assigns separate thresholds and risk budgets to each semantic argument role in LLM tool calls, ensuring rare high-risk fields are certified individually rather than being masked by aggregate metrics. This work addresses a critical safety gap in AI agents by providing finite-sample guarantees for individual argument fields, which is essential for preventing harmful actions like setting wrong recipients or executing dangerous commands. It sets a new standard for certifying structured tool calls at the semantic-role level, with implications for safe deployment of LLM agents in real-world applications. The method wraps any per-field detector and pools the rarest roles, while certifying sufficiently sampled roles directly. Evaluations on AgentDojo and InjecAgent with six language models show consistent role-specific budget compliance under model and attack transfer, detector noise, gradual drift, unseen tool suites, and adaptive attacks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Conformal risk control extends conformal prediction to control the expected value of any monotone loss function, providing finite-sample guarantees. In LLM tool calls, different argument fields carry varying risks; existing methods certify the entire call, allowing rare high-risk fields to be averaged away. Role-stratified calibration addresses this by assigning per-role thresholds and budgets.

This paper introduces a benchmark of 14,320 procedurally-generated English prompts across fifteen inference templates to test LLMs' logical inference over probability operators (e.g., probably, might, must). Evaluating 29 models, the study finds that most exhibit systematic answer biases independent of logical form, with only 9 models exceeding random chance. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLMs' reasoning abilities, moving beyond surface-level pattern matching to test principled symbolic reasoning over uncertainty expressions. The study introduces a 'competence floor' metric—the worse of a model's accuracy on Yes-correct and No-correct items—to summarize answer biases.

SE(3)-MeanFlow introduces a few-step generative framework for protein backbones on the SE(3) Lie group, extending MeanFlow from Euclidean space to the Lie algebra so(3) and R^3. It derives closed-form average-velocity identities and an SE(3) alpha-Flow objective, enabling simulation-free training and faster inference. This work addresses a major computational bottleneck in protein diffusion models, which typically require hundreds of network evaluations for ODE integration. By enabling few-step generation, SE(3)-MeanFlow could significantly accelerate high-throughput protein design campaigns, impacting computational biology and AI-driven drug discovery. The method works natively in the Lie algebra so(3) and R^3, deriving closed-form average-velocity identities for rotations and translations. It introduces an SE(3) alpha-Flow objective that removes the Jacobian-vector product from the rotation branch, serving as a warm-up stage before switching to a small-t stabilized MeanFlow loss for pretraining and rectification-based post-training.

The framework uses a state-grounded inner trial loop for adaptive perception, online planning, and safety validation, while the outer evolution loop distills completed trajectories into reusable skill, strategy, and safety experience. ## [OpenClaw and Ollama: A Layered Architecture for Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28629) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a comprehensive layered architecture for Agentic AI, separating inference, orchestration, and execution layers, and validates it with a full-stack implementation using OpenClaw and Ollama. A prototype experiment demonstrates that capabilities like persistent memory and tool use emerge from system-level integration. This work addresses a critical gap in unified frameworks for designing and evaluating full-stack agentic systems, providing a foundational architecture for building scalable, secure, and trustworthy autonomous AI agents. It offers a practical roadmap for developers and researchers working on Agentic AI, potentially accelerating the transition from reactive LLMs to persistent, action-capable systems. The architecture separates Ollama as the LLM inference layer and OpenClaw as the agent runtime orchestration layer, integrating reasoning, tool use, and action execution. The study also examines challenges in scalability, security, privacy, governance, and evaluation, and releases all models, code, and datasets publicly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to systems that go beyond reactive LLMs to persistent, goal-driven agents with memory, planning, and continuous execution. OpenClaw is an agent runtime that orchestrates multiple AI agents, while Ollama is a lightweight tool for running LLMs locally, providing efficient and privacy-preserving inference. The paper builds on these tools to propose a layered architecture that separates concerns for better scalability and security.

## [TAPR: Task-Aware Prompt Rewriter Boosts LLM Performance via RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28657) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces TAPR (Task-Aware Prompt Rewriter), a model trained with reinforcement learning using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to reformulate user prompts into task-optimized versions, improving downstream LLM performance. Experiments on tasks like question answering, summarization, and arithmetic reasoning show consistent gains, with fine-tuned Phi-4-mini-instruct achieving higher accuracy on benchmarks such as Natural Questions and GSM8K. This work addresses the barrier of prompt engineering for non-expert users by automating prompt optimization, which could make LLMs more accessible and effective across various applications. It also demonstrates the effectiveness of GRPO and LLM-as-judge rewards in a new domain, contributing to the growing field of RL-based prompt optimization. TAPR is trained using GRPO, where rewards are derived from LLM-as-judge evaluations of both the reformulated prompt and the corresponding task output. The code is available on GitHub, and the base model for TAPR is Phi-4-mini-instruct, which is fine-tuned to produce clearer and more instructive prompts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) often require carefully crafted prompts to perform well, which can be challenging for non-expert users. Prompt rewriting aims to automatically improve prompts, and reinforcement learning (RL) has been used to train models for this purpose. GRPO is an RL algorithm that compares a group of outputs for a given prompt and rewards those that outperform the group average, while LLM-as-judge uses a language model to evaluate the quality of outputs, providing a scalable alternative to human evaluation.

## [GenCDSR: Hybrid Tokenization and Serial-Parallel Decoding for Cross-Domain Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28659) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GenCDSR, a generative framework for cross-domain sequential recommendation (CDSR) that employs a cross-domain hybrid tokenization mechanism with a multi-tower architecture and a serial-parallel decoding strategy. It achieves an average accuracy improvement of 1.5% and an 85.1% reduction in inference latency compared to state-of-the-art baselines on three public datasets. This work addresses two critical limitations in existing generative recommendation methods: the neglect of cross-domain collaborative correlations during tokenization and inefficient decoding strategies like beam search. By improving efficiency and accuracy, GenCDSR could enable real-time deployment of cross-domain recommendation systems, benefiting e-commerce, content streaming, and other personalized services. The hybrid tokenization mechanism uses hierarchical shared-specific and fine-grained codebooks to capture both cross-domain commonalities and domain-specific distinctions. The serial-parallel decoding strategy leverages the hierarchical SID structure to partially parallelize generation, significantly reducing inference latency while preserving generation consistency. The implementation code and datasets are available online.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Cross-domain sequential recommendation (CDSR) extends traditional recommender systems by modeling users' dynamic interests and sequential patterns across multiple domains, addressing data sparsity issues. Generative recommendation (GR) formulates recommendation as autoregressive generation using semantic identifiers (SIDs), which are discrete token sequences learned from item semantics. However, existing GR methods often ignore cross-domain correlations and rely on inefficient decoding methods, hindering real-time deployment.

## [Ontology-Guided Extraction Layer for Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28662) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper presents a production extraction layer that converts heterogeneous documents into validated knowledge graphs using ontology-guided LLM extraction and deduplication. It reduces catalog overhead by about 94% and improves search recall from roughly 70% to 95% with no false merges. This work addresses real-world challenges in LLM-based knowledge graph construction, such as type vocabulary fracture and entity conflation. It offers a practical, scalable solution that could improve data integration and retrieval in enterprise and intelligence settings. The system uses a locally hosted Qwen3.5-9B model tuned on the ontology, and processes PDF, spreadsheet, Office, and image content via Kafka metadata. It includes a five-stage refinement pipeline with six deduplication algorithms and an embedding resolution subsystem with a conflict guard.

## [ViSAGE: Self-Correcting Entity-Centric Memory for Long-Form Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28678) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ViSAGE is a new multimodal agentic memory framework that constructs self-correcting, entity-centric memories for long-form video understanding. It introduces cross-modal binding for entity identity anchoring, bidirectional memory refinement, and multi-agent cross-verification, achieving 5.9% higher accuracy than the strongest baseline. This work addresses critical challenges in multimodal AI, such as entity confusion and error propagation in long-horizon reasoning, which are essential for embodied agents and advanced video understanding systems. By enabling abstention when evidence is missing, ViSAGE could reduce hallucinated answers and improve reliability in real-world applications. ViSAGE anchors entity identity via cross-modal binding over long temporal ranges, then applies bidirectional memory refinement to propagate delayed identity evidence and retroactively unify historical records. It also uses multi-agent cross-verification under an identity-evidence alignment constraint, allowing the system to abstain rather than provide unsupported answers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal agents operating in long-horizon environments need to build and update multimedia memories for entity-consistent, temporally grounded reasoning. Existing agentic memory approaches often discard fine-grained identity cues under aggressive compression and rely heavily on vector similarity retrieval, which can lead to entity confusion and error propagation. ViSAGE builds on recent work in multimodal memory systems, such as MemVerse and ViLoMem, which emphasize the importance of memory for long-horizon reasoning in AI agents.

## [Multi-Agent Path Planning with STL-GO Constraints via MIP and SMT Encodings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28679) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces two encodings for multi-agent path planning that satisfy spatio-temporal and topological constraints expressed in STL-GO logic, one based on mixed-integer programming (MIP) and another on satisfiability modulo theory (SMT), with soundness guarantees. The authors provide a unified interface for specifying constraints and evaluate both methods on a multi-UAV search-and-rescue benchmark. This work addresses a challenging problem in multi-agent planning with formal spatio-temporal and topological constraints, which is relevant to applications like wildfire fighting and factory inspection. By providing two sound encodings and a unified interface, it enables practical use and comparison of different solving approaches, potentially advancing the state of the art in formal-methods-based planning. The encodings handle multiple, potentially time-varying graphs via STL-GO's graph operators, which is a key technical challenge. The evaluation ablates over team size and graph complexity, highlighting the expressiveness of the encodings under dynamic multi-graph interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: STL-GO (Spatio-Temporal Logic with Graph Operators) is a recent formalism for specifying requirements in multi-agent systems, supporting reasoning about multiple agents and their topologies such as sensing, communication, and task topologies. Multi-agent planning with such constraints is challenging because it requires encoding graph-based, time-varying constraints into solvers like MIP or SMT. This paper builds on STL-GO to provide a planning solution, which is a step forward from previous work that focused on monitoring and expressivity.

<a id="item-145"></a>
## [LSR-Synth Benchmark May Not Measure LLM Priors Effectively](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28684) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.28684) analyzes the LSR-Synth benchmark and finds that most of its tasks can be solved using a fixed vocabulary without relying on language model priors. The study shows that language-model-generated candidates only provide a significant advantage when the fixed vocabulary is deliberately limited. This finding is significant for the AI/ML community because it questions the effectiveness of LSR-Synth in measuring the unique contributions of language model priors in symbolic discovery. It suggests that current benchmarks may overestimate the role of LLMs in scientific equation discovery, impacting how future benchmarks are designed. The study constructs a semantics-free baseline using a fixed vocabulary with documented provenance, and employs semantic blinding, library weakening, and matched operator-family knockouts. Strict out-of-distribution evaluation lowers absolute success rates but does not alter the relationship between fixed vocabulary and LLM-generated candidates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LSR-Synth is a benchmark designed to evaluate scientific equation discovery by introducing novel synthetic terms into established mechanisms, aiming to mitigate memorization of known equations. The benchmark is part of LLM-SRBench, which also includes LSR-Transform for testing reasoning beyond memorized forms. The paper examines whether LSR-Synth can distinguish language model priors from conventional operator search.

## [Amortized Bayesian Experimental Design for Cognitive Parameter Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28894) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an amortized Bayesian experimental design (BED) framework that treats the experimental environment as a design variable for cognitive planning experiments, and demonstrates its effectiveness on the Mouselab-MDP paradigm, closely matching exact Monte Carlo BED while reducing computational cost. This work addresses a critical gap in cognitive modeling by optimizing the design of experiments for parameter inference, potentially improving the efficiency and informativeness of cognitive studies. The amortized approach makes BED computationally feasible for complex paradigms, which could accelerate research in cognitive science and related fields. The framework uses an amortized policy network for posterior inference and design evaluation, and the experiments on Mouselab-MDP reveal trade-offs between expected information gain, posterior recoverability, and information efficiency, showing that no single environment is uniformly optimal across objectives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian inverse planning is a principled method for inferring latent cognitive mechanisms from observed behavior, but its success depends on the experimental environment. Traditional approaches fix the environment, leaving the question of which experiments are most informative. Bayesian experimental design (BED) optimizes the design to maximize information gain, and amortized BED uses a neural network to approximate the design process, making it scalable. Mouselab-MDP is a process-tracing paradigm used to study human planning behavior.

## [MMShopBench: Real-Log Benchmark for Multimodal Shopping Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29002) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MMShopBench is introduced as the first real-log benchmark for multimodal, multi-turn shopping agents, built from 289 manually annotated real-world shopping interactions. It provides ground-truth annotations of purchase intent and mandatory product requirements, and includes an offline shopping sandbox for reproducible experimentation. This benchmark fills a critical gap by evaluating agents on real-world multimodal requests, which are underrepresented in existing text-only or synthetic benchmarks. It enables more accurate assessment and improvement of AI shopping assistants, potentially accelerating their deployment in e-commerce. The benchmark requires agents to jointly infer purchase intent and product requirements from user images and multi-turn dialogue, then retrieve and verify candidate products using image and text search. Fine-tuning an open-source model on the companion training set substantially narrows the performance gap with leading proprietary models, demonstrating the training data's effectiveness.

Existing benchmarks for such agents typically rely on text-only or synthetic requests, failing to capture the complexity of real-world shopping interactions. MMShopBench addresses this by using real, manually annotated shopping logs, providing a more realistic evaluation environment.

## [Steering Vectors for CoT Faithfulness Generalize Broadly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29062) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically studies how activation steering for improving chain-of-thought (CoT) faithfulness generalizes across cue types, datasets, and steering vector construction methods in three models (Gemma-3 4B, Qwen-3.5 9B, Gemma-3 12B). It finds that when steering is effective, its effect generalizes broadly, and the construction method has little impact. This work advances AI safety by clarifying when and how activation steering can improve CoT faithfulness, a key concern for monitoring model reasoning. The finding that steering generalizes across cues and datasets suggests it could be a robust tool for enhancing transparency in LLMs. Steering reliably increased cue acknowledgment only for the largest model (Gemma-3 12B), and effect size was determined primarily by the evaluation setting rather than the vector's training setting. Four construction methods, including one with no specific cue in its optimization target, yielded similar effect sizes, and steering left cue use rate unchanged while reducing hidden cue use.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning involves models generating step-by-step explanations, which can be monitored for safety, but these explanations are not always faithful to the model's actual reasoning. Activation steering is an inference-time technique that modifies model activations to control behavior, often by adding a steering vector computed from contrastive examples. Prior work showed that steering can improve CoT faithfulness, and this paper extends that by testing generalization across various conditions.

## [Counterfactual Explanations Reframed as Generalized Bayes MAP Estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29077) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper shows that distance-minimization-based counterfactual explanations are mathematically equivalent to MAP estimates of a Gibbs posterior with a distance-based prior, introducing the DP-GBCE framework. It also proposes new decision rules (Bayes and CVaR) and a model-mixing extension to handle model multiplicity. This provides a theoretical foundation for counterfactual explanations, linking them to generalized Bayesian inference, which could lead to more robust and principled interpretability methods. The new decision rules and evaluation metrics may improve practical deployment of CEs in high-stakes applications. The paper defines metrics for evaluating both individual CEs and the posterior distribution, and experiments on simulated and Google Trends data quantify trade-offs among decision rules. The CVaR-CE rule is risk-averse, and the model-mixing extension uses Bayesian model weights to combine posterior distributions from multiple models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Counterfactual explanations identify the minimal change to an input to achieve a desired output, typically formulated as a distance-minimization problem. Generalized Bayes inference relaxes the need for a full probabilistic model by allowing arbitrary loss functions, and the MAP estimate is the mode of the posterior distribution. CVaR (Conditional Value at Risk) is a risk measure that considers the tail of the loss distribution, useful for risk-averse decision-making.

## [WILC: Complementarity-Driven Iterative Collaboration for LLM Crowds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29087) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces WILC (Wisdom Integration of LLM Crowds), a framework that dynamically coordinates multiple LLMs through iterative reflection-and-refinement and complementarity-driven model selection. Experiments show WILC outperforms existing methods and matches GPT-5.2 average performance at roughly 7 times lower cost. This work addresses the challenge of combining multiple LLMs in enterprise settings, offering a dynamic alternative to static ensembling. It demonstrates that collective intelligence can exceed any single model, potentially reducing costs while maintaining high performance and supporting data sovereignty. WILC uses a dual-gate mechanism: prospective complementarity fit (PCF) selects the best worker for the current bottleneck, and posterior complementarity gain (PCG) evaluates whether the transition improves the solution. The framework was tested on four benchmarks, showing superior performance over self-refinement, ensemble, and query-routing methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have individual capability limitations, and combining them can unlock collective intelligence. Traditional approaches fix the combination strategy in advance, but WILC adapts dynamically based on the state of the solution. The wisdom-of-crowds paradigm suggests that diverse models can complement each other, and WILC operationalizes this through sequential relay-style collaboration.

## [MirrorCraft: Benchmarking LLM Agents Under Hidden Minecraft Rule Changes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29218) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MirrorCraft introduces a paired benchmark for evaluating LLM-based agents in Minecraft under hidden rule changes, where each Mirror world modifies server-side rules via datapacks while keeping terrain, spawn, and objectives matched to a Vanilla counterpart. The benchmark includes five biomes, six rule suites, three objectives, two model families, and six agent configurations, and uses the Rule Intervention Effect (RIE) to measure performance changes. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM agent evaluation by testing adaptability to rule changes, which is essential for real-world deployment where environments are dynamic. It provides a controlled setting to study how agents leverage gameplay outcomes when familiar rules differ, potentially influencing future agent design and evaluation methodologies. The experiments show that hidden rule changes have strongly different effects across rule suites, and among configurations evaluated without rule descriptions, ReAct achieves the highest pooled Mirror score. Providing exact rules yields only modest gains in average progress and completion across all three objectives, suggesting that explicit rule descriptions have limited benefit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Minecraft is a popular sandbox game often used to test AI agents due to its open-ended nature. Data packs are collections of files that modify game mechanics, such as recipes and loot tables, without changing the core game. Mineflayer is a JavaScript library that allows creating Minecraft bots, providing a high-level API for agent control. Existing benchmarks typically evaluate agents under fixed game mechanics, which does not reflect real-world scenarios where rules may change.

## [MAGA: Multi-Platform GUI Agent Self-Fusion via Structured Action Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29320) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MAGA, a method that re-allocates training signals based on the correctness of structured actions during distillation, suppressing invalid signals and focusing on erroneous actions. Across two model scales, MAGA achieves the highest mean success rate, outperforming the strongest baseline by 2.0% at 8B and nearly matching teacher performance. This work addresses a key challenge in GUI agents: consolidating domain-specific models into a unified cross-environment policy. By focusing distillation on action tokens, MAGA improves multi-platform performance, which is crucial for real-world deployment and user experience across mobile, web, and desktop. MAGA uses a training-only hint to optimize supervision from domain-specific teachers without altering student input. It outperforms weight merging and on-policy distillation baselines, achieving a 2.0% improvement over the strongest baseline at 8B scale and nearly matching teacher performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: GUI agents based on large language models are deployed across mobile, web, and desktop, but are often domain-specific. Weight merging combines experts but can corrupt actions under disagreement, while on-policy distillation (OPD) treats all tokens equally. MAGA addresses this by focusing on action tokens, which are the only interface between the environment and the agent.

<a id="item-153"></a>
## [ModelEquivBench: Certifying Multi-Relational Evaluation of LLM Optimization Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29431) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ModelEquivBench introduces a certifying, multi-relational evaluation system that reports a per-pair semantic profile E0–E6 for LLM-generated optimization models, replacing coarse single verdicts with independently checkable evidence. It was applied to three model snapshots (GPT-5.4, Claude Sonnet 4.6, Qwen3.5-397B-A17B) on 173 base problems, revealing distinct failure stages that a single accuracy score cannot capture. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM-generated optimization models, where current methods reduce equivalence to a binary verdict or execution success rate, which are neither faithful nor independently checkable. By providing a certifying, multi-relational profile, it enables more nuanced and trustworthy evaluation, potentially influencing how LLMs are assessed in operations research and formal verification communities. The system defines seven equivalence levels (E0–E6) covering model construction, representation alignment, feasible-set relations, objective-order equivalence, optimal-value equality, and optimizer-set equivalence. Each decided entry includes replayable traces, exact-rational certificates, or explicit witnesses, while incomplete or unsupported cases yield typed UNKNOWN, N/A, or ABSENT outcomes. In the study, 49, 35, and 25 cells contained executable candidates that were certified negative on at least one relation, and 25, 8, and 18 structural rejections occurred on pairs with verified mapped feasible-set equality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to translate natural-language descriptions of decision problems into solver-ready optimization models. However, evaluating whether a generated model faithfully represents the original problem is challenging; traditional metrics like execution success rate or a single equivalent/not-equivalent label are insufficient because they do not capture the multiple ways two formulations can agree or differ. ModelEquivBench addresses this by providing a certifying, multi-relational evaluation framework that produces evidence-backed semantic profiles, enabling more rigorous and transparent assessment of LLM-generated optimization models.

This paper introduces analytic memory as a new abstraction for multimodal agents, complementing existing retrieval memory, and presents the AdaMM framework that jointly supports both. Experiments show AdaMM improves performance by up to 11.3% on MemEye and 7.3% on MemGallery benchmarks. At inference, a memory-aware planner decomposes queries into retrieval and analytic operations, routing each to appropriate tools.

The paper introduces AMTFV (Agentic Mathematical Tool-Flow Verification), a framework that decouples verification modeling from execution using a Mathematical Tool Flow (MTF) interface. It outperforms baselines on five math datasets, improving accuracy by up to 8.3 percentage points over the strongest baseline. This approach addresses a critical limitation in LLM mathematical reasoning: reliable verification of candidate answers. By enabling exact computation through a decoupled tool flow, it could improve the trustworthiness of LLM outputs in math-heavy applications, benefiting researchers and engineers deploying such models. AMTFV uses an interrupt-execute-resume interface, where a verification agent constructs a workflow and encodes mathematical objects in an MTF request, which a toolbox agent parses and executes via backend tools. Evaluations cover seven model configurations from DeepSeek, GPT, and Gemini, with larger gains on medium and high verification complexity samples.

## [COntExt: Context-Aware Ontology Extension from Operational Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29553) ⭐️ 7.0/10

COntExt is a new framework that automatically suggests ontology extensions by analyzing structured operational metric definitions, breaking the task into parent class prediction, relation type prediction, and data property assignment. It was evaluated on four cybersecurity ontologies, showing that metric-derived context improves suggestions for relation type and data property tasks compared to ontology-context baselines. This work addresses the labor-intensive and manual process of integrating operational metrics with ontologies, offering a lower-cost, automated alternative. It highlights operational metric catalogues as an underexploited source for ontology extension, which could improve semantic interoperability and knowledge engineering in organizations. The framework defines three sub-tasks: parent class prediction, relation type prediction, and data property assignment, and evaluates various algorithms on four cybersecurity ontologies. Results show that metric-derived context outperforms ontology-context baselines for relation type prediction and data property assignment, but not for parent class prediction.

**Background**: Ontologies are formal representations of domain knowledge, but they often lag behind the evolving concepts and properties used in operational metrics. Operational metrics are quantifiable indicators used to monitor systems and processes, and they implicitly encode domain knowledge that can extend ontologies. Traditionally, extending ontologies is manual and labor-intensive, so automated methods like COntExt are valuable.

## [LEMUR: Multi-Objective RL from Human Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29559) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LEMUR introduces a novel framework that combines multi-objective reinforcement learning (MORL) with preference-based reinforcement learning (PbRL), enabling agents to learn optimal policies from human preferences without predefined reward functions. The method jointly learns policies and multiple objective-specific reward models from human feedback, and demonstrates superior performance over baseline methods on benchmark multi-objective tasks. This work bridges a significant gap between MORL and PbRL, addressing real-world scenarios where multiple competing objectives exist and reward functions are difficult to specify. It could enable more practical applications of RL in areas like robotics, autonomous driving, and personalized recommendation, where human preferences are more natural than engineered rewards. LEMUR learns multiple objective-specific reward models from human feedback, allowing the agent to balance competing objectives during learning. The paper evaluates LEMUR on various benchmark multi-objective tasks and reports superior performance over baseline methods, though the abstract is truncated and specific algorithmic details are not fully disclosed.

**Background**: Multi-Objective Reinforcement Learning (MORL) extends standard RL to handle vector-valued rewards, aiming to find Pareto-optimal policies across conflicting objectives. Preference-based Reinforcement Learning (PbRL) learns from qualitative human preferences between trajectories instead of numeric rewards, which is particularly useful when reward engineering is difficult. LEMUR combines these two paradigms to learn from human preferences in multi-objective settings, eliminating the need for explicit reward functions.

## [Four-World Framework Models Interpretive Perspectives in Computational Creativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28644) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a four-world framework to model interpretive perspectives in computational creativity, operationalized through three evaluative personas (formalist, social-historical, iconographic) applied to 1,069 artworks from the SemArt dataset, generating 38,484 persona-based evaluations. The results show systematic divergence in creativity assessment across perspectives, and linear probing of CLIP embeddings reveals distinct orientation vectors for each persona. This work challenges the traditional view of creativity as an objective property, offering a relational perspective that could lead to more interpretively diverse co-creative systems. It bridges computational creativity with digital humanities, potentially impacting AI art evaluation and human-AI collaboration. The study uses a twelve-trait creativity framework organized across four conceptual domains, and the SemArt dataset contains 21,384 fine-art images with textual annotations. ## [Evaluating Federated Pre-Training: Downstream Fine-Tuning vs. Intrinsic Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28658) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically compares downstream fine-tuning on GLUE with next-token prediction as an intrinsic evaluation signal for federated pre-trained models. The authors find that downstream fine-tuning does not reliably preserve the reference ranking, while next-token prediction strongly correlates with pre-training test perplexity. This research addresses a critical gap in federated learning: how to reliably evaluate pre-trained models when data is distributed and private. The findings suggest that relying solely on downstream fine-tuning can lead to misleading conclusions, urging the community to consider intrinsic evaluation signals closer to the pre-training objective. The study uses a 16M parameter transformer model trained on identical client data under both centralized and federated settings. They evaluate full, head-only, and reduced-data fine-tuning variants on GLUE, and compare them with next-token prediction on GLUE text, using a reference ranking from the same pre-training test set.

This paper explores which evaluation protocol better reflects federated pre-training quality.

## [LAWFUL Framework Verifies Physics Law Learning in Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28672) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LAWFUL, a framework that closes gaps in coverage-aware causal consistency and domain-of-validity testing for physics laws over continuous variables, demonstrated on the Mocap2Radar transformer for Doppler frequency prediction. This work advances neural network interpretability by providing rigorous methods to verify whether models truly learn and use physical laws, which is crucial for trustworthy AI in scientific applications. It addresses a growing need for causal consistency and domain-of-validity testing in high-stakes fields like physics and engineering. LAWFUL addresses four interpretability gaps: coverage-aware causal consistency over continuous counterfactuals, domain-of-validity testing, verification of law invariants and forbidden behaviors, and quantification of derived physical quantity flow. The framework is illustrated on the Mocap2Radar transformer, validating the Doppler frequency law f(t) = 2v(t)/λ from data where neither f(t) nor v(t) appears explicitly.

## [MPP-GNN: Adaptive Brain Module Discovery for Alzheimer's fMRI Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28681) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MPP-GNN (Meta Probabilistic Pooling GNN), a graph neural network that adaptively discovers subject-specific brain modules via bilevel optimization, and uses these modules as priors to guide edge refinement for Alzheimer's disease (AD) classification from fMRI data. It achieves the highest AUC on two public AD datasets compared to established baselines. This work addresses a key limitation in existing GNN-based fMRI analysis methods, which assume a fixed number of functional modules across all subjects, ignoring inter-subject variability. By enabling subject-specific module discovery, MPP-GNN could improve the accuracy and interpretability of brain disorder classification, potentially aiding in earlier and more personalized diagnosis of Alzheimer's disease. MPP-GNN employs a coupled bilevel optimization framework that performs hierarchical adaptive graph partitioning to discover subject-specific modules, which are then used as an explicit prior for edge refinement and representation learning. The model shows significant alignment with the Yeo brain atlas's canonical functional networks and reveals a network-level dedifferentiation pattern in AD.

Bilevel optimization is a technique where one optimization problem is nested within another, commonly used for hyperparameter tuning in deep learning. ## [Metaphors Can Steer LLMs Toward Suboptimal Code Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28683) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a phenomenon called metaphorical algorithmic steering, where metaphorical instructions cause code-generation LLMs to transfer procedural patterns from source domains, leading to less efficient algorithms. The authors developed the MASC framework to elicit and detect this effect, achieving high detection rates. This finding highlights a subtle but significant risk in prompt engineering and AI safety: seemingly benign metaphorical language can bias LLMs toward suboptimal code, potentially degrading performance in real-world applications. It underscores the need for careful prompt design and evaluation of code generation models beyond surface-level correctness. The study shows that metaphorical skills induce a hidden-state shift toward lower-efficiency procedural behavior prototypes, indicating the effect operates through procedural pattern transfer rather than surface-level language. The MASC framework iteratively metaphorizes and refines benign skills to elicit low-efficiency code while remaining task-relevant.

## [Reflected UAS Routing: Corrected Stability and Direct CTMC Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28688) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper corrects the deterministic stability analysis for Reflected UAS routing in heterogeneous multi-server queues, showing that the deterministic surrogate is a reflected ODE with a unique boundary equilibrium. It also provides a direct Foster-Lyapunov drift inequality for the CTMC using a weighted-quadratic function, bypassing a previously failed lifting argument. This work fills a known gap in the literature by providing a rigorous stability proof for Reflected UAS routing, which is important for performance evaluation of routing policies in queueing systems. The improved performance over UAS and JSSQ at benchmark parameters suggests practical benefits for load balancing in heterogeneous server environments. The deterministic surrogate is a reflected ODE on the nonnegative orthant, and its boundary equilibrium is characterized by a scalar consistency equation and a convex-potential representation. At the benchmark parameter point, the boundary equilibrium matches the numerical attractor to machine precision, and the default Reflected UAS policy achieves lower mean queue length than UAS and JSSQ across independent seed blocks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Reflected UAS (Utility-based Allocation with Skipping) is a routing policy for multi-server queues where jobs are assigned to servers based on a utility function, with reflection at the boundary to keep queue lengths nonnegative. The stability of such policies is often analyzed using deterministic ODE approximations and Foster-Lyapunov drift conditions for the underlying continuous-time Markov chain (CTMC). This paper addresses a flaw in a previous stability argument and provides a corrected analysis.

## [PINN Guide for Geometric Analysis: Asymptotic Plateau Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a methodological guide for using physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) to construct near-minimal discs in hyperbolic space asymptotic to a prescribed knot at infinity, providing numerical evidence for a conjecture by Joel Fine relating minimal surfaces in H^4 to HOMFLY polynomial coefficients. It details two implementation techniques—forward propagation of second-order jets and compiling the computational graph once—that reduce training step cost by a factor of 40-50. This work bridges machine learning and pure mathematics, offering a practical framework for applying PINNs to geometric analysis problems, which could accelerate research in differential geometry and related fields. The significant speedup in training makes PINN-based methods more feasible for complex geometric problems, potentially opening new avenues for numerical exploration of conjectures. The paper emphasizes encoding the geometry of the problem into the model architecture so that boundary conditions and asymptotics at infinity hold exactly for all learnable parameters, resulting in a single-component loss function. The two implementation techniques—replacing nested reverse-mode automatic differentiation with forward propagation of second-order jets, and compiling the computational graph of the residual once—together reduce training step cost by a factor of roughly 40-50 on identical hardware.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) are a class of deep learning models that embed physical laws, typically described by partial differential equations (PDEs), into the training process as a regularization agent, enabling solutions even with low data availability. The asymptotic Plateau problem in hyperbolic space asks for the existence of an area-minimizing submanifold asymptotic to a given submanifold at infinity. The HOMFLY polynomial is a two-variable knot invariant that generalizes the Alexander and Jones polynomials, used to distinguish knots.

This paper introduces SCMA, a novel flow matching-based framework for CT metal artifact reduction that integrates sample-specific structural guidance and metal-aware degradation modeling. It alternates conditional flow matching updates with projection-consistency correction during inference to improve artifact suppression and anatomical preservation. SCMA addresses key limitations of existing MAR methods, such as residual artifacts, poor generalization, and anatomically inconsistent structures, by leveraging flow matching with structural and physical constraints. This could improve diagnostic accuracy in CT imaging with metal implants, benefiting clinical practice and quantitative analysis. The method uses a linear-interpolation-corrected image as input to the velocity network, with the intermediate state serving as a sample-specific structural condition. Time-varying spatial weights derived from the metal mask and its distance transform emphasize severe degradation near metal regions, and projection-consistency correction constrains predictions using reliable measurements outside metal traces.

This paper introduces a two-axis evaluation framework (Goal Persistence and Relational Attunement) for counselor responses in motivational interviewing, and uses Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to study how penalizing different failure modes affects these dimensions. ## [TextCloak: RL-Driven Unlearnable Text to Thwart Unauthorized LLM Exploitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28862) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TextCloak introduces a reinforcement learning (RL) framework that generates unlearnable text examples to protect data from unauthorized fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs). It uses a generative policy and a novel GRPO-UE optimization to degrade downstream performance while preserving semantic fidelity. This addresses a critical gap in data privacy for LLMs, as existing unlearnable example methods are mostly designed for classification tasks and fail in open-ended generation. TextCloak offers a practical defense against unauthorized data exploitation, which is increasingly important as LLMs are trained on vast web-scraped data. The framework employs a bi-level optimization: the generator is rewarded based on the degradation it induces in fine-tuned surrogate LLMs, using group-relative policy optimization (GRPO-UE). Experiments on six datasets and nine LLMs demonstrate consistent impairment of unauthorized fine-tuning while maintaining utility for legitimate use, with transferability across architectures and robustness to adaptive attacks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Unlearnable examples (UEs) are a defense mechanism that adds carefully designed perturbations to training data, causing models trained on it to perform poorly. Traditional UE methods for text often rely on class-specific cues, limiting their use in generative LLM settings. Reinforcement learning (RL) has been explored for adversarial text perturbation, but applying it to generate UEs for LLMs is novel.

<a id="item-173"></a>
## [RareSense: Rarity-Aware Similarity Search for Anomaly Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28879) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RareSense introduces a rarity-aware similarity framework that mines minimal rare itemsets and derives rare association rules to map sparse transactional data into rare-rule profiles, then compares them using weighted Jaccard similarity. It is shown to outperform classical measures like Jaccard, cosine, and Hamming, as well as IDF-weighted Jaccard, in anomaly retrieval across four benchmark families. This work addresses a known limitation of classical similarity measures in sparse transactional data, where frequent background attributes dominate and obscure rare anomalies. By explicitly modeling rare higher-order co-occurrences, RareSense improves anomaly retrieval, which is critical for cybersecurity and other domains where rare events are of high interest. RareSense's rule weights combine inverse support, confidence, lift, structural complexity, and stability. The induced distance is proven to be a pseudometric on original objects and a metric over equivalence classes defined by identical rule profiles, and IDF-weighted Jaccard is shown to be a restricted singleton case of RareSense.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Similarity search in sparse set-valued data often relies on measures like Jaccard, cosine, and Hamming, which compare objects based on atomic overlap. IDF weighting partially mitigates the dominance of frequent attributes but remains atom-wise and cannot capture higher-order co-occurrences. Rare itemsets and rare association rules are concepts from data mining that focus on infrequent but potentially informative patterns, which RareSense leverages to improve anomaly retrieval.

## [Human-LLM Collaborative Coding Pipeline for K-12 Educator AI Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28889) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a multi-phase human-LLM collaborative coding pipeline that adapts open, axial, and selective coding to develop a hierarchical codebook from 45,000 educator-AI messages. The final codebook contains 72 items across 19 categories and six domains, and was validated through human coding of 2,560 messages. This work addresses the growing challenge of analyzing large-scale qualitative datasets, demonstrating a practical method for integrating LLMs while preserving human interpretive authority. It offers a replicable template for researchers in education and other fields, potentially advancing qualitative research methodologies. The pipeline treats the LLM as a labeling instrument rather than an interpretive agent, with human researchers retaining control over category definitions and merging decisions. The study used set-valued agreement measures for multi-label coding and extended the codebook with five additional codes identified by human coders.

**Background**: Qualitative research often involves coding data to identify themes, with methods like open, axial, and selective coding used in grounded theory. ## [TORUS Benchmark Reveals Unified Audio Models Lack Self-Coherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28896) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces TORUS, the first self-coherence test for audio-native unified models, comprising 48 three-stage tests with 432 six-option questions across speech, sound, and music. Evaluation of five open unified models shows the best achieves only 50.5% accuracy, below the Cascaded Baseline's 63.2%. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating unified audio models by testing whether they can understand their own generations, a capability essential for real-world applications. The results highlight significant weaknesses, particularly in audio editing, guiding future research and development. TORUS includes five task families and compares five open unified models against a Cascaded Baseline combining state-of-the-art specialized models. The chance floor is 16.7%, and models particularly struggle with audio editing tasks, indicating a lack of self-coherence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Unified audio models aim to combine audio understanding, generation, and editing in a single system, but current evaluation methods assess each capability separately. Self-coherence refers to a model's ability to correctly interpret its own generated audio, which is crucial for tasks like iterative editing or quality control. The TORUS benchmark is the first to systematically test this property.

## [DiffAttack: Latent Diffusion-Based Adversarial Faces Evade Face Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28936) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DiffAttack, a novel framework that uses latent diffusion models to generate imperceptible adversarial faces capable of evading face recognition systems. It achieves an average attack success rate of 84.86% across multiple FR models, outperforming existing methods by over 15.28% compared to noise-based approaches and 5.21% over semantic-based methods on FFHQ and CelebA-HQ datasets. This work highlights a growing vulnerability in face recognition systems, particularly in cross-demographic and cross-gender scenarios, which could have significant implications for biometric security. It also demonstrates the potential of generative models like latent diffusion in adversarial attacks, prompting a need for more robust defense mechanisms. DiffAttack leverages latent diffusion models to guide generation toward target identity embeddings, as measured by a face recognition model. The framework was evaluated on standard benchmarks including FFHQ and CelebA-HQ, and it demonstrates superior transferability across different FR models such as FaceNet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Face recognition systems map facial images into a high-dimensional embedding space where identities are distinguished. Adversarial attacks craft imperceptible perturbations to cause misclassification. Latent diffusion models (LDMs) are generative models that perform diffusion in a compressed latent space, enabling high-quality image synthesis. This paper applies LDMs to generate adversarial faces that are both effective and imperceptible.

## [Training-Free Retrieval Method for AI Video Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28955) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a training-free, retrieval-driven method for attributing AI-generated videos to their source generative models, using a generative fingerprint pipeline with orthogonal color transformation, multi-scale quantized residual generation, and temporal-semantic aggregation. On the GenVidBench benchmark, it achieves a Rank-1 accuracy of 20.5% and a mean Average Precision of 16.6%, outperforming existing state-of-the-art methods. This work addresses the critical need for attributing AI-generated videos to their source models, which is essential for forensic investigation and legal regulation as synthetic videos become increasingly realistic and prevalent. By being training-free and retrieval-based, it offers a scalable and generalizable solution that overcomes the limitations of image-focused methods, potentially impacting cybersecurity and content authenticity verification. The method formulates video attribution as an instance retrieval task and uses a generative fingerprint pipeline that progressively captures and integrates artifacts introduced by generative models across video frames. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/renxi-seu/Video_Attribution, and the benchmark GenVidBench contains 100,000 semantic labels along with original prompts and images used during generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: AI-generated videos are becoming increasingly realistic, making it difficult to distinguish them from authentic content, which poses threats to cybersecurity and social governance. Traditional visual attribution methods focus on images and rely on image generation models, lacking generalization to large-scale video data. This paper proposes a training-free, retrieval-based approach that leverages generative fingerprints to identify the source model of a video, addressing the limitations of existing methods.

## [Efficient LLM Adversarial Training via Low-Rank Defense and Circuit-Guided Surrogates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28959) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes two complementary strategies to speed up latent adversarial training (LAT) for large language models: defense-side optimization using representation fine-tuning (ReFT) and attack-side optimization using circuit-guided surrogate models. The method reduces per-step adversarial-training FLOPs by 48.1% on average while requiring only 0.0118% trainable parameters compared to standard LAT with full fine-tuning. Adversarial training is crucial for robust LLMs but is computationally prohibitive at scale. This work makes it more practical, potentially enabling broader adoption of robustification techniques in real-world LLM deployments, which is significant for AI safety and security. The defense-side optimization explores ReFT within LAT and identifies a potential issue when there is a mismatch between the tokens selected for ReFT and those attacked. The attack-side optimization extracts only relevant circuits from the LLM to build a lightweight surrogate model, avoiding full forward-backward passes during attack generation. The paper provides theoretical justifications and numerical evidence for both strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial training involves generating adversarial examples during training to make models robust against attacks. Latent adversarial training (LAT) perturbs latent activations rather than raw inputs, but still incurs high computational cost. Representation fine-tuning (ReFT) is a parameter-efficient method that intervenes on hidden representations, and circuit analysis identifies the minimal subnetwork responsible for specific behaviors.

## [LLM Productivity Link Robust to Stopping-Time Selection Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28968) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper refutes claims that the positive association between LLM adoption and scientific productivity is an artifact of stopping-time selection. Using recalibrated placebos and complementary designs, the authors show the association persists and is not explained by the timing artifact. This finding strengthens the evidence that LLM adoption genuinely boosts productivity, which has implications for research policy and AI adoption strategies. It also provides methodological guidance for future studies on technology adoption and productivity. The authors recalibrated RBB's random placebo to the detector's realized flag rate and found the measured association stays well above this benchmark. They also used four complementary designs: a before-and-after comparison, a conservative control group for difference-in-differences, an intensity-based specification, and a rank-based measurement, all showing a positive association, while pre-ChatGPT placebo data returned null effects.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Stopping-time selection bias occurs when outcomes are evaluated at a data-dependent time, such as the first month an abstract is flagged, which can create spurious trends. Event-study designs and difference-in-differences are common causal inference methods used to estimate treatment effects in observational data. This paper addresses a methodological critique by RBB and provides robust evidence for a real productivity effect.

The paper introduces RAID, a novel AI-generated image detection method that uses bit-reversed images, gradient-based patch selection, and a convolutional classifier. It also provides theoretical validation and two new challenging datasets, achieving superior performance on over 40 benchmarks. This work addresses the robustness and generalization shortcomings of existing AI image detectors, which is crucial as generative models become more advanced. The method's speed and accuracy improvements could enable more reliable detection in real-world applications, helping mitigate the spread of misinformation. The approach is nearly 100 times faster than existing counterparts and outperforms them on over 40 benchmarks, including cross-generator, cross-dataset, and zero-shot settings. The code is publicly available on GitHub.

## [PARALLEL: Prefrontal-Aligned Reinforcement Approach for Efficient Language Model Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28982) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PARALLEL, a novel method that assigns sample-dependent update intensity using a reinforcement-inspired controller, improving efficiency in language model adaptation. It retains 94.1-99.2% of full-adaptation performance while using updates more efficiently than selective baselines. This approach could significantly reduce computational costs and energy consumption in fine-tuning large language models, making adaptation more sustainable and practical for real-world deployment. It also offers a new perspective on integrating cognitive control principles into machine learning, potentially influencing future model adaptation strategies. PARALLEL represents goal-related and uncertainty-related information as separate controller signals and combines them with the current model representation. Experiments on XSum and CNN/DailyMail show it retains 96.9-98.6% of ROUGE-1 and ROUGE-2 scores and 98.8-98.9% of ROUGE-L scores achieved by full adaptation, with higher ARC accuracy at the same cumulative adaptation time or GPU energy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Language model adaptation typically applies uniform updates across all training samples, which can be inefficient. PARALLEL draws inspiration from the prefrontal cortex's complementary roles in goal-related and uncertainty-related control, using a reinforcement-inspired controller to decide when and how strongly to update each sample. This approach aims to prioritize beneficial updates while limiting unnecessary parameter changes, leading to more stable and efficient adaptation.

## [Adjudicated Captioning: Multi-Agent Framework Boosts Zero-Shot Image Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28986) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Adjudicated Captioning, an inference-time multi-agent framework that adds multiple grounding checkpoints and a learned reranker to the IFCap captioner, achieving CIDEr 117.6 on COCO Karpathy, up from 108.0 for IFCap, without retraining the captioner. This framework addresses the stagnation in zero-shot image captioning by restoring visual grounding feedback during decoding, potentially setting a new state-of-the-art and inspiring further research in inference-time multi-agent approaches for vision-language tasks. The framework includes a stronger frozen retrieval encoder, a cross-attention verifier that re-ranks top-9 retrievals to top-5, and a learned reranker combining TriFuse (MLP) and MemAttend (memory-attended transformer), trained via Borda-consensus distillation without paired labels. The training-free fixed-fusion baseline reaches 115.8 CIDEr, indicating most gains come from architectural changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Zero-shot image captioning (ZIC) generates captions without paired image-caption supervision, relying on text-only corpora and frozen pretrained image-text scorers. Existing retrieval-augmented methods score alignment only once at retrieval, then decode with language-model probability alone, lacking visual grounding feedback. This framework introduces multiple checkpoints to provide grounding feedback, improving performance without retraining the captioner.

## [Sparsity-Biased Guidance Improves scDiffusion for scRNA-seq Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29043) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces sparsity-biased classifier-free guidance (SB-CFG), a training-free sampling modification for scDiffusion that replaces the unconditional branch with a deliberately under-informative sparse reference. It consistently improves marker gene expression fidelity, cell-type consistency, and sparsity preservation across five public scRNA-seq datasets. This work addresses a key limitation of standard classifier-free guidance in conditional scRNA-seq generation, where the unconditional branch retains gene-specific structure that weakens guidance. By amplifying the contrast between conditional and unconditional predictions, SB-CFG enables more biologically meaningful synthetic data, which is crucial for downstream analyses and model development in computational biology. SB-CFG is a drop-in, training-free modification: when the sparsity-biased module is removed, sampling reduces to standard CFG. The sparse reference removes gene identity while preserving only coarse sparsity statistics, and the method was validated on five publicly available scRNA-seq datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) measures gene expression at single-cell resolution, and generating synthetic scRNA-seq data is important for benchmarking and privacy. Diffusion models, such as scDiffusion, generate data by iteratively denoising random noise, and classifier-free guidance (CFG) improves conditional generation by combining conditional and unconditional predictions. However, the unconditional branch in standard CFG is trained to approximate the true marginal distribution, which may retain gene-specific structure and limit guidance effectiveness. SB-CFG addresses this by using a deliberately 'bad' sparse reference to amplify guidance.

## [Benchmarking Frontier LLMs for Police Crash Coding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29064) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study benchmarked six frontier LLMs on coding police crash narratives against official Arkansas fatal-crash database fields, using a zero-shot prompt. GPT-5.5 High achieved the highest agreement among LLMs, but simple baselines like always-majority and keyword-rule performed comparably or better. This research provides a rigorous benchmark for evaluating LLM-based crash coding, a task with significant public safety implications. It highlights that LLMs may not yet outperform simple baselines, emphasizing the need for attribute-specific evaluation and human review before deployment. The study linked 5,587 narratives with 5,889 crash records, yielding 4,194 matched crashes, and evaluated six attributes: crash manner, non-motorist relation, intersection type, work-zone relation, roadway surface condition, and light condition. Performance metrics included agreement, macro-averaged F1, Cohen's kappa, coverage, and selective agreement, with repeated-measures analyses and GEE models.

## [Legal Deception Detection Benchmark: Fine-tuned Models vs. Few-shot LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29066) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a comprehensive survey and unified empirical evaluation of NLP-based Automatic Deception Detection (ADD) in the legal domain, comparing six fine-tuned transformer models and seven LLMs across seven datasets under four prompting strategies. The results show that fine-tuned models excel in data-rich general domains, while few-shot LLMs remain competitive in low-resource legal settings, and Chain-of-Thought prompting often underperforms direct classification. This research highlights the domain sensitivity of deception detection models and the limitations of Chain-of-Thought prompting in high-stakes legal contexts, providing valuable insights for NLP researchers and legal technology developers. It underscores the need for domain adaptation and interpretable systems to ensure reliable performance in legal proceedings, law enforcement, and online security. The evaluation covers two legal and five general-domain datasets, comparing six fine-tuned transformer models and seven LLMs under four prompting strategies. Notably, Chain-of-Thought prompting often underperforms direct classification, suggesting that reasoning steps may not always benefit deception detection tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Deception detection is critical in legal proceedings, law enforcement, and online security, but human judgment is limited in accuracy and scalability. NLP offers a data-driven alternative, with methods evolving from feature-based machine learning to large language model approaches. Fine-tuning adapts pre-trained models to specific tasks, while few-shot learning enables LLMs to perform new tasks with minimal examples. Chain-of-Thought prompting instructs models to generate intermediate reasoning steps, which can improve complex reasoning but may not always be beneficial for classification tasks.

## [DoubleHelix: Iterative Cross-Modal Fusion for AVSR with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DoubleHelix introduces a novel iterative cross-modal fusion framework for audio-visual speech recognition (AVSR), comprising ReverseParallelHelix, QualitySensor, and HelixReplication components. On the LRS3 benchmark, it achieves 0.68% WER on clean audio, a 5.6% relative improvement over previous best results under matched backbone settings. This work addresses a key limitation in AVSR by reformulating fusion as an iterative process rather than a single-step operation, potentially improving robustness in noisy environments. The significant WER improvement on LRS3 suggests DoubleHelix could advance state-of-the-art multimodal speech recognition and inspire further research in structured fusion with LLMs. The framework includes ReverseParallelHelix for multi-turn structured interaction with learned alignment constraints, QualitySensor for degradation-aware gating, and HelixReplication for consistency-guided feature enhancement. Under babble-noise conditions, it achieves 11.6% WER at SNR -5dB, and ablation studies validate each component's contribution, including asymmetric pathway weighting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Audio-visual speech recognition (AVSR) combines audio and visual modalities to improve recognition accuracy, especially in noisy environments. Traditional approaches often treat cross-modal fusion as a single-step operation, lacking iterative refinement. DoubleHelix leverages large language models (LLMs) to enable structured, iterative cross-modal interaction, with components designed to handle varying audio quality and enhance features based on consistency.

## [MGLP: Multi-Granularity Position Embedding for Link Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29115) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MGLP, a novel method for link prediction that uses an Adaptive Granular-Ball Graph Refinement mechanism to refine graphs into homophilic subdomains at optimal granularity levels, and a Multi-granularity Hierarchical Distance encoding mechanism to capture hierarchical correlations. This approach generates multi-granularity position embeddings that outperform baseline algorithms in link prediction tasks. This work addresses a key limitation in existing position embedding methods, which rely on single-granularity landmarks and ignore the hierarchical nature of homophilic structures. By introducing multi-granularity position embeddings, MGLP could improve the accuracy of link prediction in complex graphs, benefiting applications such as social network analysis, recommendation systems, and biological network modeling. MGLP introduces an Adaptive Granular-Ball Graph Refinement mechanism that adaptively splits the graph into granular-balls of varying sizes and optimal granularity, treating central nodes as landmarks to form a Hierarchical Central Graph. The method also employs a Multi-granularity Hierarchical Distance encoding mechanism to capture both homophilic structures and their hierarchical correlations, enhancing node discriminative power. The code is available at an anonymous repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Link prediction aims to identify potential or future connections in a graph, and position information is crucial for distinguishing homogeneous nodes. Traditional methods derive node positions as distances to single-granularity landmarks, which are centers of homophilic regions, but they neglect the multi-granularity nature of such structures. Granular-ball graph refinement is a technique that adaptively aggregates feature-coherent regions into supernodes, and it has been used in graph coarsening for scalable GNN training. MGLP extends this concept to position embedding for link prediction.

## [InferQ: Database-Oriented Benchmark for Quantum Circuit Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29134) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InferQ is a new benchmark that generates quantum circuit simulation tasks as SQL workloads, releasing a dataset of 202,975 circuits with a web-based viewer. It extracts static, graph, SQL, and dynamic features for workload characterization. This benchmark bridges quantum computing and database systems, enabling systematic database research such as query optimization and physical design for SQL-based quantum simulation. It also shows that RDBMSs can outperform Qiskit Aer in memory usage on over 50% of circuits, and machine learning can predict when SQL is preferable, opening new optimization avenues. InferQ generates general, compositional circuits by assembling subcircuits from templates, and compiles simulation into join-and-aggregate tensor contractions. Experiments across PostgreSQL, SQLite, DuckDB, and Umbra show RDBMSs achieve better peak memory usage than Qiskit Aer on more than 50% of circuits, with ML models predicting SQL preference with up to 95.3% accuracy for runtime and 97.4% for memory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum circuit simulation is computationally intensive, and recent research has explored using relational database management systems (RDBMSs) to execute simulations by compiling them into SQL workloads, primarily join-and-aggregate tensor contractions. However, prior work focused on a narrow set of structured circuits, limiting systematic database research. InferQ addresses this by providing a large, diverse benchmark with feature extraction, enabling workload characterization and optimization.
