## [HERO: History-Enriched Rollout Training for Stable Long-Horizon Neural Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29135) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces HERO (History-Enriched Rollout Training), a novel training strategy that augments conventional absolute trajectory supervision with relative supervision derived from the model's optimization history. This approach ranks candidate rollouts and uses the strongest failure trajectory as a reference to reweight gradients, improving long-horizon accuracy on nine PDE benchmarks. This work addresses a critical limitation in autoregressive neural operators for PDEs: error accumulation during long-horizon rollout. By providing a new supervision signal that targets historical failure modes, HERO could enable more reliable and stable long-term predictions, benefiting fields like weather forecasting and fluid dynamics. HERO uses a margin-based objective with a fixed reference trajectory selected from a periodically refreshed lagged operator, current model, and perturbed input. It ranks rollouts by rollout error, spectral discrepancy, energy drift, and error growth, and provides theoretical analysis of the bounded reweighting. Experiments show consistent improvements in long-horizon accuracy, stable rollout length, and out-of-distribution robustness at no inference-time cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Neural operators are machine learning models that learn mappings between function spaces, often used to solve partial differential equations (PDEs) efficiently. Autoregressive rollout involves feeding the model's own predictions back as input for subsequent time steps, which can lead to error accumulation over long horizons. Existing training strategies like rollout training aim to reduce the train-test mismatch but still rely on absolute supervision, which may not capture long-term failure behaviors.

## [ActFovea: Runtime Safeguarding for VLA Policies via Spatiotemporal Visual-Action Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29169) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ActFovea is a plug-and-play framework that detects and mitigates runtime disturbances in vision-language-action (VLA) policies without retraining. In closed-loop evaluations on LIBERO suites, it boosts success under localized visual overlays from 49.3% to 90.3%, closing 93.7% of the gap to clean performance. This addresses a critical robustness issue in VLA-based robotic manipulation, offering a practical safeguard that can be applied to existing policies. It could enhance the reliability of robots in real-world settings where visual disturbances, action drift, or delays are common. ActFovea uses robot kinematics, proprioceptive states, and recent actions to construct action-conditioned foveated regions, and verifies recovery actions before acceptance. It also invokes a bounded safe-failure procedure when recovery is impossible, and improves success under action drift and visual delay by 7.0 and 9.8 percentage points respectively.

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that integrate vision, language, and action to generate robot policies. They are vulnerable to runtime disturbances that break temporal alignment among observations, states, and actions. Foveated regions, inspired by human vision, focus processing on relevant areas, which ActFovea adapts for action-conditioned safeguarding.

## [SERUM: Multi-Pass VLM Framework Extracts User Behavior Models from Screen Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29181) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SERUM is a new multi-pass framework that uses hierarchical VLM annotation to extract finite-state behavioral models of user actions and intents directly from unstructured egocentric screen recordings, without manual annotation. It was accepted to COLM 2026 and is publicly available with demo, code, and results. This work addresses a key challenge in building proactive AI assistants: deriving structured user models from raw screen activity. By enabling scalable, interpretable behavior modeling from egocentric video, SERUM could advance user modeling and human-computer interaction, potentially improving personalized and context-aware assistance. SERUM processes screen recordings through a sliding window, alternating between activity-recognition and intent-inference passes, using accumulated prior context to reduce hallucination and temporal conflation. Synonymous states are merged via sentence embeddings and human-calibrated thresholds, and the resulting label sequences are evaluated by fitting first-order Markov models, showing lower perplexity and higher predictive accuracy than frequency baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic assistants need structured models of user intent and workflow to provide proactive, personalized interactions, but building these from raw screen activity is difficult. Traditional approaches often rely on logs or predefined taxonomies, which are not always available or flexible. SERUM leverages vision-language models (VLMs) to interpret egocentric video, a form of first-person recording, and uses a multi-pass approach to iteratively refine labels, converging to a stable state vocabulary called 'schematic equilibrium'.

## [SAF Framework Stabilizes RLVR and On-Policy Distillation Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29209) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SAF, a Stable Advantage Fusion framework that resolves magnitude and temporal mismatches when combining RLVR and on-policy distillation, preventing entropy collapse. It uses a four-stage pipeline with sparsify-then-compress and warm-up-then-anneal mechanisms applied to the OPD advantage. This work addresses a critical challenge in combining RLVR and OPD, which are complementary but suffer from entropy collapse when naively fused. By providing a lightweight solution that improves performance across multiple benchmarks, SAF could enhance reasoning and code generation capabilities of LLMs, benefiting the RL community and practitioners. SAF is evaluated with GRPO on seven mathematical reasoning and code generation benchmarks using Qwen3-1.7B/4B/8B models. It consistently outperforms fixed-coefficient GRPO+OPD fusion, improving aggregate scores by 0.51-2.70% across all six model-domain settings while achieving more stable training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method that uses rule-based checkers to provide rewards, while On-Policy Distillation (OPD) trains a student on its own rollouts with dense token-level supervision from a teacher. Combining them is promising but can cause entropy collapse due to miscalibrated advantages. SAF addresses these issues with a lightweight pipeline.

## [Linear Proposal Geometry Uncovered in SOMA and Differential Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29228) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an operator-selection factorization that separates objective-independent variation from boundary repair and fitness-dependent selection, revealing that the canonical SOMA proposal is affine in the search space and exactly linear in an augmented migrant-leader state. It also derives closed-form expressions for proposal statistics under Bernoulli perturbation masks and finite-population moments for DE/rand/1/bin. This theoretical analysis provides a deeper understanding of the proposal geometry of SOMA and DE, which could lead to improved algorithm design and new variants. The proposed geometry-controlled and rotation-aware SOMA variants show substantial improvements over canonical SOMA and are competitive with established DE methods on the BBOB benchmark, indicating practical impact. The paper derives closed-form expressions for proposal mean, covariance, expected squared step length, expected squared distance from the leader, active dimensionality, and coordinate coverage under Bernoulli perturbation masks. For DE/rand/1/bin, it characterizes the additional covariance and coordinate dependence induced by forced-coordinate binomial crossover, and validates the analytical identities through exact enumeration and Monte Carlo experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: SOMA (Self-Organizing Migrating Algorithm) and Differential Evolution (DE) are population-based stochastic optimization algorithms. They are typically analyzed as complete procedural systems, but this paper separates the proposal generation from selection and repair to reveal simpler linear structures. The BBOB benchmark is a standard test suite for comparing optimization algorithms.

## [InMyStyle: Small LoRA Models Suffice for Per-User Style Rewriting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29238) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces InMyStyle, a privacy-first system that adapts small language models (0.5B to 7B parameters) via LoRA adapters to rewrite AI-edited text in a user's style without needing an instruction prompt at inference. Experiments on 219 evaluation pairs show that the automatic composite score plateaus at 0.69 across all model sizes, suggesting that small models are sufficient for this task. This finding challenges the assumption that larger models are always necessary for style rewriting, potentially enabling more efficient and privacy-preserving personalization of AI-generated content. It could benefit users who want AI tools to match their personal writing style without sending data to large cloud models. The system uses multiple local helper LLMs to construct paired training examples from a user's documents, and applies length-aware generation budgets and automatic chunking to handle inputs of varying lengths. A secondary evaluation with 400 ratings from five LLM judges showed that InMyStyle outputs have a mean perceived AI-ness score over 20% lower than the helper-AI generated inputs, with perceived AI-ness decreasing as model size decreases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adapts pre-trained models to specific tasks with minimal computational overhead. Style rewriting aims to transform AI-generated text to match an individual's writing style, which is useful for personalizing AI assistants. Traditional approaches often rely on large models and instruction prompts, but InMyStyle explores a privacy-first, small-model alternative.

## [RecHarness: Bandit-Routed Agentic Framework for Recommender Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29241) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RecHarness introduces a novel framework that separates direction selection (via a bandit router) from hypothesis generation (via an LLM) to automate recommender model optimization. In a 7-day online A/B test on a large-scale short-video advertising platform, it improved ADVV by 2.084%, Revenue by 0.534%, and Exposure by 0.559%. This work addresses a key bottleneck in recommender system development—the heavy reliance on manual engineering iteration. By enabling stable, long-horizon automated optimization, it could significantly reduce human effort and improve model performance across various recommendation tasks, with potential industry-wide impact. RecHarness uses a jump-basin mechanism to activate a structural-jump arm when local edits stagnate, sustaining long-horizon exploration. It outperforms LLM-reasoning search in stability and trial-budget efficiency across multiple tasks, datasets, and model backbones. Code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Recommender systems are crucial for personalization in platforms like e-commerce and video streaming, but optimizing them typically requires engineers to manually tweak architecture, objectives, and training strategies. LLM-based agents can automate this trial-and-error process, but letting the LLM both choose directions and generate hypotheses often leads to unstable search under limited budgets. Bandit algorithms, which balance exploration and exploitation, can help select promising directions based on historical feedback, while LLMs excel at generating concrete code changes. RecHarness combines these strengths to achieve more reliable automated optimization.

## [LLM Persuasion Unaffected by Perceived National Origin](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29334) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A preregistered experiment with 403 US adults found that labeling a chatbot as American or Chinese did not affect its persuasive power, despite lower initial trust in the Chinese-labeled model. This challenges assumptions that perceived foreign origin could limit AI influence, suggesting that transparency about AI origin may be insufficient to counter foreign influence operations via conversational AI. Participants debated with GPT-4o, introduced as 'DiscoveryAI' (American) or 'ZhengheAI' (Chinese). The study used equivalence tests and Bayes factors to support null effects, and found that collective narcissism acted as a general barrier to attitude change.

This study systematically analyzes cross-lingual transfer among five Turkic languages (Turkish, Azerbaijani, Uzbek, Kazakh, Kyrgyz) using pairwise transfer matrices with mT5, finding that transfer strength correlates with linguistic proximity and that Latinization improves BLEU and chrF in script-mismatched settings. This provides valuable empirical insights for low-resource machine translation within a language family, showing that leveraging closely related languages can improve performance. The study uses pairwise transfer matrices where each model is fine-tuned on one transfer source and evaluated on a different transfer target while the translation target remains the same. Transfer is strongest for Turkish-Azerbaijani and Kazakh-Kyrgyz pairs, and transfer direction matters; Latinization's effect is not uniform across metrics.

## [CrossRAG: Retrieval-Augmented Framework for Multivariate Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29459) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CrossRAG, a novel retrieval-augmented forecasting framework that integrates Shape-Aware Memory (SAM) with RevIN normalization, Future-Consistent Contrastive (FCC) learning, and Cross-Attention Temporal Fusion (CATF) to improve multivariate time series forecasting. Experiments on seven public benchmarks show that CrossRAG consistently outperforms both parametric-only baselines and existing retrieval-augmented forecasting methods. This work addresses key challenges in applying retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) to time series forecasting, such as magnitude variations and history-future mismatch, which are common in heterogeneous IoT sensor data. By improving long-term forecasting accuracy, CrossRAG could enhance resource scheduling and predictive maintenance in IoT systems, and it opens new avenues for integrating external historical patterns into time series foundation models. CrossRAG uses Shape-Aware Memory (SAM) with RevIN normalization to achieve magnitude-robust shape-level retrieval, and Future-Consistent Contrastive (FCC) learning to distinguish informative references from hard negatives that have similar history but divergent futures. The framework also employs Cross-Attention Temporal Fusion (CATF) to fuse retrieved historical-future reference pairs into the backbone's representations at the representation level.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Multivariate time series forecasting is crucial for applications like resource scheduling and predictive maintenance in IoT environments. Recent time series foundation models rely on static parametric knowledge and lack dynamic access to external historical patterns during inference. Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) offers a way to incorporate external knowledge, but its application to time series is challenged by magnitude variations and the mismatch between historical similarity and future consistency. RevIN is a normalization technique that helps handle distribution shift in time series data.

## [DreamQAS: Model-Based RL for Efficient Quantum Architecture Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29491) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DreamQAS introduces a model-based reinforcement learning framework that learns only the expensive post-VQE feedback, achieving lower energy errors on molecular tasks compared to existing RL-QAS methods. It uses a recurrent randomized-prior ensemble to predict oracle-free scores and supports multi-step imagined policy learning over legal circuits. This work addresses a significant bottleneck in VQE efficiency by reducing the number of real VQE calls, which is crucial for practical quantum computing. It demonstrates that learning decision-useful feedback rather than exact energy prediction can lead to more efficient quantum architecture search, potentially accelerating progress in quantum chemistry and optimization. Under a common 15,000-episode budget, DreamQAS achieved the lowest mean frozen-policy energy error on four of five molecular tasks and the second-lowest on one. It used 1.6x to 2.0x fewer real VQE calls on four tasks and 10.6x fewer on BeH2-8q, with a mean counterfactual action-ranking utility increase of 0.346 (95% CI [0.185, 0.507]).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Variational quantum eigensolver (VQE) is a hybrid quantum-classical algorithm used to find the ground state energy of a quantum system, central to quantum chemistry. Quantum architecture search (QAS) automates the design of quantum circuits, often using reinforcement learning (RL) to explore the circuit space. Model-based RL learns a model of the environment to improve sample efficiency, which is particularly valuable when real evaluations are costly, as in VQE.

## [ARB Benchmark Reveals AI Detectors Fail on LLM-Rewritten Human Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29539) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ARB, a benchmark dataset with 1,800 human source texts and four LLMs, creating matched human, direct LLM, LLM-rewritten human, and same-generator rewritten LLM texts. Evaluations show that detectors like FastDetectGPT and Binoculars drop from over 90% recall on direct LLM text to under 31% on LLM-rewritten human text at a 1% false-positive rate. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in AI-text detection evaluation by simulating realistic rewriting scenarios, revealing that current detectors are unreliable for detecting LLM-assisted authorship. The findings have significant implications for academic integrity, content moderation, and the development of more robust detection methods. The dataset includes 600 texts each from XSum, WritingPrompts, and OpenWebText, rewritten by Llama-3.2-3B, Qwen2.5-7B, Mistral-7B, and Gemma-2-9B. Five detectors were evaluated at a strict 1% false-positive operating point, with BERT-Defense and RoBERTa-Defense consistently below 3% recall across all regimes.

**Background**: AI-text detectors are designed to distinguish human-written text from machine-generated text, often using zero-shot methods like Fast-DetectGPT and Binoculars, which rely on conditional probability curvature or dual-model scoring. Previous work has shown that paraphrasing can degrade detector performance, but standard benchmarks typically only compare human text against direct LLM output, not LLM-rewritten human text. This benchmark fills that gap by providing matched variants to test real-world scenarios where users might use an LLM to revise their own writing.

## [MOT-SR: Multi-Objective Tool-Augmented Symbolic Regression with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29561) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MOT-SR is a new framework that integrates external analytical tools with two collaborative LLM modules (Meta Strategy Generator and Equation Generator) to perform symbolic regression, jointly optimizing accuracy, complexity, and generalization via a dynamic Pareto front. It outperforms existing SR methods on 40 standard tasks and achieves the lowest trajectory-level integration error on EMRI orbital modeling. This work addresses key limitations in LLM-based symbolic regression by incorporating data analysis for variable dependencies and multi-objective evaluation, which can improve the efficiency and reliability of scientific equation discovery. It demonstrates potential for long-horizon scientific dynamics modeling, such as gravitational-wave astronomy, where small errors can accumulate over time. MOT-SR maintains a dynamic Pareto front to balance accuracy, complexity, and generalization, and uses a closed-loop system to refine strategies and equations. The framework was validated on 40 standard tasks and an EMRI orbital modeling problem, achieving the lowest trajectory-level integration error on held-out configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Symbolic regression (SR) searches for mathematical expressions that fit data, balancing accuracy and simplicity, and is NP-hard. Recent LLM-based approaches have shown promise but often lack data analysis and rely on single-objective evaluation. Multi-objective optimization deals with conflicting objectives, and Pareto optimality is key. Tool-augmented LLMs combine language models with external tools to enhance capabilities.

## [TraceViT: Grounded Trace Supervision Boosts Visual Abstract Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29586) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TraceViT, a looped visual reasoner, is trained with semantically monotonic transformation chains and soft trace alignment, achieving 67.8% pass@2 on ARC-AGI-1 and 24.3% on ARC-AGI-2. This method grounds each iteration with task references and object workspaces, improving intermediate refinement supervision. This work addresses a key limitation in looped visual reasoners by providing supervision for intermediate steps, not just the final output. It demonstrates significant performance gains on the challenging ARC benchmark, potentially advancing research in abstract reasoning and few-shot generalization. The method uses programmatic task implementations to generate transformation chains, which are then aligned with the loop iterations via soft trace alignment that only enforces ordering. Ablations show that trace supervision is only beneficial when combined with grounding, highlighting the importance of the grounding mechanism.

**Background**: The Abstraction and Reasoning Corpus (ARC) is a benchmark designed to test AI systems' ability to infer unseen transformations from few examples, requiring human-like abstraction and generalization. Looped visual reasoners iteratively refine predictions, but conventional training only supervises the final output, leaving intermediate steps unconstrained. TraceViT introduces a novel training paradigm that provides grounded supervision for each iteration, using transformation chains derived from programmatic task solutions.

This paper introduces the EPC score, a model-agnostic metric that extends the Explainability-Performance Coefficient to quantify explanation quality by balancing feature sparsity and preserved model performance. It is validated across tabular, text, and image modalities and against human-based explanations. The EPC score addresses a critical open challenge in XAI: objectively evaluating explanation fidelity without ground truth. By aligning with human judgments, it provides a more trustworthy metric for high-risk domains, potentially improving how AI explanations are assessed and trusted. The EPC score explicitly balances feature selection sparsity and model performance, and its validation reveals operational dependencies among network activations, data dimensionality, and explainer performance. Higher EPC scores strongly align with human lexical sentiment judgments and spatial visual annotations.

## [CENDRe: New Concept Extraction Method for Time-Series CNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29621) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CENDRe is a novel concept extraction method for CNNs that automatically discovers and localizes temporal and spectral patterns in time-series data, addressing three limitations of existing methods: time-domain-only analysis, predefined concept numbers, and misaligned localizations. This method enhances interpretability of CNNs in critical domains like fault diagnosis, where understanding both temporal and frequency features is crucial. It provides more accurate and automatic concept discovery, potentially improving trust and reliability in AI systems. CENDRe uses two-stage clustering with silhouette-guided aggregation to automatically select the number of concepts. It localizes concepts via gradients of a presence score propagated through a differentiable invertible mapping like Fourier transform, yielding frequency-domain localizations. On synthetic benchmarks, it achieves comparable representation correctness and significantly higher importance correctness than state-of-the-art methods.

**Background**: Concept extraction (CE) methods identify human-understandable patterns in a model's latent space to explain predictions. Existing time-series CE methods often operate only in the time domain, require a predefined number of concepts, and produce localizations that may not align with the actual regions used by the model. CENDRe overcomes these by incorporating frequency features and automatic concept selection.

This paper introduces the theoretical foundation for 'Socratic Tests,' an automated, multimodal conversational assessment method that integrates Dynamic Assessment, Bloom's Taxonomy, and SOLO Taxonomy to map students' cognitive boundaries and grade adaptively. This framework could transform educational assessment by moving away from static, deficit-based grading toward a dynamic, mastery-oriented approach, potentially improving diagnostic feedback and reducing test anxiety. It also highlights the growing role of AI in personalized education, which could impact educational technology and assessment practices significantly. The Socratic Test formalizes the use of graduated scaffolding to quantify the Zone of Proximal Development (ZPD) and details a non-compensatory, additive grading architecture that prioritizes mastery over penalty. It also emphasizes human-AI alignment to ensure measurement reliability, though the paper lacks empirical validation.

A scoping review of 223 AI-in-wargames papers, retrieved through May 1, 2026, found that only 20 (~9%) give language models both open-ended control over player actions and adjudication. This distinction is critical for researchers using LLM-based social simulations, as the model's role affects the validity of the simulation as a behavioral signal. It highlights a common oversight in simulation fidelity, urging clearer reporting of model control in future studies. The review covers papers retrieved through May 1, 2026, and focuses on open-ended wargames where models handle unusual actions and ambiguous consequences. The authors argue that fidelity depends not only on plausible agent behavior but also on whether LLMs can reliably act as adjudicators or world models.

RAPiD introduces a reward-guided consistency distillation framework that compresses a pretrained DiffusionPlanner into a few-step consistency student, reducing complete-pipeline inference latency from 100.91 ms to 18.41 ms (5.5x speedup) while maintaining comparable performance on nuPlan and achieving the highest aggregate score among learning-based methods on interPlan. This work addresses the critical latency bottleneck of diffusion-based trajectory planners, enabling real-time closed-loop deployment in autonomous driving. By combining consistency distillation with reward-guided safety-aware training, it demonstrates a practical path to compress expensive diffusion models without sacrificing multi-modal trajectory quality or safety. The student model is trained using deterministic DDIM teacher steps and a low-noise data anchor to stay grounded in expert demonstrations. An Implicit Q-Learning critic, trained on a balanced mixture of log-replay and DiffusionPlanner rollouts with a modified PDM-style reward, performs best-of-K trajectory selection during deployment.

**Background**: Diffusion models can generate high-quality multi-modal trajectories but require iterative denoising, which is too slow for real-time driving. Consistency distillation (introduced by Song et al., 2023) compresses a diffusion model into a few-step sampler by learning a mapping that directly outputs the final denoised result. Implicit Q-Learning (IQL) is an offline reinforcement learning method that learns a policy without querying values for unseen actions, useful for safety-aware trajectory selection.

## [DDC Framework Cuts LLM Inference Tokens 10x While Boosting Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15100) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced Dual-Dimensional Consistency (DDC), a unified framework that couples Confidence-Weighted Bayesian consensus with Trend-Aware Stratified Pruning to jointly optimize sampling budget and reasoning quality in LLM inference-time scaling. Evaluations on five benchmarks show over 10x reduction in token consumption while maintaining or exceeding baseline accuracy. This addresses a critical inefficiency in inference-time scaling, where current methods treat sampling width and depth as orthogonal, leading to hallucination reinforcement or premature truncation of valid reasoning. By balancing budget and quality, DDC could enable more cost-effective deployment of LLMs for complex reasoning tasks, benefiting both researchers and practitioners. The framework uses Confidence-Weighted Bayesian protocol to filter hallucinations and Trend-Aware Stratified Pruning to avoid premature termination of complex reasoning chains. It was evaluated across five benchmarks with various LLMs, demonstrating consistent improvements in token efficiency without sacrificing accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Inference-time scaling refers to techniques that allocate additional compute during generation to improve LLM reasoning, such as sampling multiple paths or performing deeper search. Current methods often separate width (number of samples) and depth (length of reasoning), leading to inefficiencies. DDC unifies these dimensions by adaptively terminating low-quality paths and focusing resources on high-quality ones.

<a id="item-213"></a>
## [LLM Multi-Agent System Boosts IPMSM Design Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09037) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new LLM-based multi-agent framework for interior permanent magnet synchronous motor (IPMSM) design optimization was introduced, featuring a Design agent with retrieval-augmented generation, a Training agent that repairs design spaces, and an Optimization agent using an uncertainty-aware FEA-AI hybrid model. The system achieved up to 44% lower iron loss in single-objective optimization and 22.5% higher hypervolume in multi-objective optimization compared to FEA-only search. This work addresses critical bottlenecks in simulation-driven engineering design, such as expertise-dependent setup, high FEA costs, and unreliable AI surrogates, by combining domain-specialized LLM agents with uncertainty-aware hybrid evaluation. It demonstrates a scalable paradigm that could accelerate design automation across various engineering fields, reducing computational costs while maintaining accuracy. The Design agent improved answer accuracy on motor design problems from below 50% to 67-80% using RAG. The Training agent raised the success ratio of geometry sampling from 28% to 84% by reasoning over solver failure history. The hybrid model reduced computation time by 52-55% while retaining 90-92% of FEA-only hypervolume, and a controller agent adaptively updated the uncertainty threshold, achieving 5.8% lower single-objective iron loss than a fixed threshold.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Interior permanent magnet synchronous motors (IPMSMs) are widely used in high-performance industrial drives, and their design optimization often relies on finite element analysis (FEA), which is accurate but computationally expensive. AI surrogates can reduce cost but may be unreliable in unexplored design regions. Multi-agent LLM systems leverage specialized agents to handle different tasks, improving accuracy and robustness compared to single-agent models.

<a id="item-214"></a>
## [Role-Agent: Bootstrapping LLM Agents via Dual-Role Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10917) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Role-Agent is a new framework that uses a single LLM to act as both the agent and the environment, enabling bootstrapped co-evolution. It introduces two components, World-In-Agent (WIA) and Agent-In-World (AIW), which provide process rewards and reshape training data, respectively. This framework addresses key limitations in LLM agent learning, such as inefficient feedback and static environments, potentially improving generalization and performance. It offers a novel approach that could influence future agent training methodologies. In WIA, the LLM predicts future states after each action, and the alignment with actual states serves as a process reward. In AIW, the LLM analyzes failure modes and retrieves similar tasks to reshape the training distribution. Experiments show an average performance gain of over 4% over strong baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often struggle with learning due to limited feedback and static environments. Role-Agent leverages the concept of co-evolution, where the agent and environment evolve together, to overcome these challenges. This approach is related to world models and self-evolving agents, which are active areas of AI research.

## [ReSum: RL Framework for Self-Summarized Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13316) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReSum introduces a novel RLVR framework that enables LLMs to self-summarize their reasoning trajectories, improving coherence and reducing errors. Experiments show an average performance improvement of 4% while reducing rollout length by 18.6%. This addresses a known issue in RLVR where models produce overly long reasoning rollouts, which can degrade coherence and exhaust context budgets. By enabling self-summarization, ReSum could lead to more efficient and reliable LLM reasoning, benefiting applications in math, coding, and other long-horizon tasks. ReSum uses a summarization-aware adaptive rollout mechanism that creates contrastive branches by masking or injecting a summarization phrase. It also designs a summarization-aware advantage for finer-grained comparison between trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a technique that uses formal verifiers to reward correct outputs, improving LLM reasoning. However, RLVR often encourages long rollouts. Self-summarization is an intrinsic control mechanism where the model consolidates intermediate reasoning, reducing entropy and recovering from bad prefixes.

## [CogWM: Cognitive World Model for Evaluating Conversational Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29495) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Cognitive World Model (CogWM), an LLM-based framework that models users' beliefs, desires, intentions, and emotions (BDI/E) to evaluate conversational agents beyond task completion. Trained on 150K user-turn samples with Qwen3-14B, CogWM enables explicit cognitive trajectory tracking and outperforms existing user simulation baselines. This work addresses a critical gap in conversational AI evaluation by shifting focus from surface-level outcomes to the cognitive processes underlying user interactions. It provides a new lens for diagnosing agent strengths and weaknesses, potentially influencing how future conversational agents are designed and optimized. CogWM jointly models users' BDI/E states and corresponding responses, enabling progressive comparison of agents through cognitive trajectories. Experiments with six state-of-the-art LLMs reveal distinct agent patterns and complementary relationships between cognitive evolution and behavioral outcomes.

CogWM leverages this framework to provide a more holistic evaluation of conversational agents.

## [Factorized Transition Effects Enable Better Latent Action Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30544) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a two-stage framework that first pretrains Observed Transition Factorization (OTF) to discover reusable local transition primitives via a compositional codebook, then aggregates them into state-conditioned latent actions. The framework is instantiated as OTF-LAM-Pixel and OTF-LAM-Dino, with the latter operating in a frozen DINOv2 representation space. This work addresses the inherent ambiguity in unsupervised action recovery from multi-object or distractor-rich scenes, which is a common challenge for latent action models. By improving action alignment and supervision utilization, it could enhance downstream policy learning in reinforcement learning and robotics. The proposed OTF-LAM variants demonstrate that learned transition primitives transfer across visual appearance and morphology, and the resulting latent actions show substantially stronger action alignment than existing LAMs. The framework also makes more effective use of downstream action supervision and achieves competitive or superior downstream policy performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Latent Action Models (LAMs) aim to learn action-relevant changes from unlabeled videos by compressing frame differences into latent variables. However, in multi-object scenes, these differences can be caused by distractors, camera dynamics, or background changes, making the recovery of true actions ambiguous without supervision. This paper argues that the appropriate unsupervised target is a state-conditioned compositional summary of transition effects, rather than the true action itself.

## [NeurOWL: LLM-Based Neural-Symbolic Framework for Incomplete OWL Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15776) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NeurOWL is an end-to-end neuro-symbolic framework that jointly performs subsumption verification and abduction for incomplete OWL ontologies, using LLMs and ontology embeddings to provide logically sound explanations. It removes the need for a predefined candidate set of missing axioms, generalizing traditional ontology abduction. This work addresses a significant challenge in OWL ontology reasoning with incomplete ontologies, which is common in real-world domains like healthcare and bioinformatics. By unifying verification and abduction, NeurOWL could improve the practicality and reliability of ontology-based systems, potentially aiding in knowledge discovery and maintenance. NeurOWL leverages both formally defined semantics and textual semantics through Large Language Models and ontology embeddings. It is evaluated on real-world ontologies across multiple domains, demonstrating strong and robust performance.

Abduction is a form of reasoning that seeks explanations for observations, and in ontology context, it involves hypothesizing missing axioms.

## [CodeRescue: Budget-Calibrated Recovery Routing for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19338) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces CodeRescue, a method that uses supervised learning and Conformal Risk Control (CRC) to decide whether coding agents should retry with cheap models or escalate to expensive ones after execution failures, ensuring cost control. The approach is validated on five coding benchmarks, showing improved efficiency over fixed actions and binary cascade baselines. This work addresses a practical and timely problem in AI coding agents: budget-aware recovery routing after execution failures. By providing a calibration layer that adapts to changing budgets without retraining, it enables more cost-effective deployment of coding agents, potentially reducing operational costs while maintaining high solve rates. The method combines a supervised router trained from execution rollouts with a Conformal Risk Control layer that selects a deployment-time cost penalty, providing marginal expected-cost control under exchangeability. In the main GPT-5.4-nano/GPT-5.4 setting, one CRC-calibrated frontier point exceeds always-escalate solve rate while using only 35% of its mean recovery cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents operate in executable environments where failed attempts produce actionable feedback. Existing cost-aware systems often use a cascade strategy: try a cheap model first, then escalate hard cases to a stronger, more expensive model. However, in coding, execution feedback can make further cheap-model recovery worthwhile, raising the question of when to spend more cheap compute versus escalate. Conformal Risk Control is a framework that extends conformal prediction to control the expected value of any monotone loss function, providing finite-sample guarantees.

## [AttriMem: Attribution-Guided Process Feedback for Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21106) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AttriMem introduces an attribution-guided process-feedback framework for learning LLM agent memory-construction policies with reinforcement learning. It augments the global outcome reward with local rewards derived from token-level contributions to the final answer, addressing the credit-assignment bottleneck in existing RL-based methods. This work addresses a significant challenge in LLM agent memory construction, offering a more fine-grained credit assignment than heuristic or outcome-level RL methods. It could improve the adaptability and performance of LLM agents in long-horizon tasks, benefiting applications like dialogue question answering and multi-turn tool use. AttriMem uses token-level attribution, likely via ContextCite, to estimate each intermediate memory action's causal contribution to the final answer, converting these scores into local rewards. Experiments on long-horizon dialogue question answering show it outperforms retrieval-based, heuristic, and RL-based baselines, and generalizes across benchmarks and answer models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents need effective memory to manage interactions, but constructing memory policies is challenging. Heuristic methods rely on subjective rules, while RL-based methods use coarse outcome-level rewards that fail to identify which memory contents support the final answer. AttriMem addresses this by providing process-level feedback through token-level attribution, enabling more precise credit assignment.

## [Reason-Mediated Behavioral Models for Auditing LLM Social Simulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24649) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a reason-mediated behavioral model to audit LLM social simulators, arguing that matching human outcomes is insufficient and that simulating the underlying reasoning patterns is crucial. The study uses a 94-person sunscreen concept test to demonstrate that human rationale-derived reasons improve prediction of purchase intent, while LLM-simulated reasons are often brittle and echo the concept board rather than recovering the respondent's reasoning. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety and evaluation: current evaluations of LLM social simulators often only check outcome similarity, which can mask flawed reasoning. By focusing on reasoning patterns, this framework provides a more robust audit method, potentially improving the reliability of LLMs used in social science research and synthetic data generation. The model maps open-ended rationales into signed reason states Z, where positive signs support adoption and negative signs block it. The audit holds respondent descriptors D, category context K, and concept treatment X fixed, and tests whether human rationale-derived reasons help predict behavior Y and whether an LLM can simulate the same reason state without seeing the human rationale or outcome.

## [Dual-Force: Offline RL Diversity via Van der Waals Objective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.04426) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Dual-Force is a new offline reinforcement learning algorithm that maximizes behavioral diversity under imitation constraints using a Van der Waals force objective computed from successor features, eliminating the need for a skill discriminator. It also introduces Functional Reward Encoding (FRE) to stabilize training under non-stationary intrinsic rewards and enable zero-shot skill recall. This work addresses instability issues in existing offline diversity maximization methods, which often rely on mutual-information objectives and skill discriminators. By providing a more stable and flexible approach, Dual-Force could improve the robustness of learned policies in real-world applications like robotics, where distribution shift is a concern. Dual-Force uses an off-policy estimator of the Van der Waals force objective, which is computed from successor features, and conditions the value function and policy on a pre-trained Functional Reward Encoding (FRE). The FRE code allows zero-shot recall of any encountered skill via its latent representation, removing the need to pre-specify a fixed number of skills. Experiments on two Solo12 simulation benchmarks (locomotion and obstacle navigation) show that Dual-Force recovers diverse high-performing behaviors while matching a target expert state occupancy and improves robustness in adversarial obstacle variations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Offline reinforcement learning (RL) learns policies from fixed datasets without additional environment interaction. Diversity maximization under imitation constraints aims to transform demonstration data into a set of distinct behavioral policies, improving robustness to distribution shift. Van der Waals forces are intermolecular forces that can be repulsive or attractive; in this context, they are used as an objective to encourage diversity. Successor features are an RL abstraction that separates environment dynamics from reward structure, enabling efficient transfer. Functional Reward Encoding (FRE) is a method to learn functional representations of tasks from state-reward samples, facilitating zero-shot RL.

The paper introduces DiRe, a force-directed dimensionality reduction framework that combines an initial embedding with graph-based layout optimization to preserve global structure and homological features. It evaluates embeddings using local distortion, context preservation, and persistent homology measures, offering a complementary tradeoff to UMAP and t-SNE. DiRe addresses a known limitation of popular methods like UMAP and t-SNE, which often prioritize local structure at the expense of global geometry. This could impact data visualization and topological data analysis by enabling embeddings whose large-scale structure can be quantified via Betti curves and persistence diagrams. DiRe is designed to be practical on modern hardware, combining an initial embedding with graph-based layout optimization. It evaluates results using local distortion, context preservation, and persistent homology measures, and is positioned as a framework for embeddings whose large-scale geometry can be quantified.

Force-directed graph layout algorithms, originally used for graph drawing, have been adapted for dimensionality reduction to balance local and global structure.

This paper introduces a fully data-driven approach using long short-term memory (LSTM) neural networks to generate original one-dimensional motion that captures the unique motor signatures of specific individuals. The model is validated on real human data from participants performing spontaneous oscillatory motion, and statistical analyses show it reproduces the velocity distribution and amplitude envelopes of the trained individual while remaining distinct from others. By enabling realistic and individualized motion generation, it has significant implications for cognitive architectures, virtual avatars in extended reality, and robots operating in human group activities such as rehabilitation, sports, and manufacturing. The paper first demonstrates that motion amplitude provides a useful characterization of individual motor signatures, complementing existing velocity-based descriptors. The proposed LSTM-based architecture is trained on real human data and validated through thorough statistical analyses, ensuring the generated motion matches the target individual's velocity distribution and amplitude envelopes while remaining distinct from other individuals.

## [StaQ: Finite-Memory Policy Mirror Descent for Discrete Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13862) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces StaQ, a Policy Mirror Descent (PMD)-like algorithm for discrete action spaces that retains only the last M Q-functions in memory. It proves that for a sufficiently large finite M, the algorithm approximates exact PMD without introducing policy update errors, and demonstrates improved performance on MinAtar benchmarks. Exact PMD is theoretically appealing but intractable in practice due to the need to sum all past Q-functions. StaQ offers a practical, theoretically grounded approximation that retains PMD's error-averaging benefits, potentially improving the stability and performance of reinforcement learning algorithms in discrete action settings. The algorithm uses an efficient GPU implementation and shows on medium-scale benchmarks like MinAtar that increasing M improves performance up to a threshold, after which it approaches exact PMD. This suggests that an infinite sum of Q-functions is unnecessary, and finite memory suffices for practical PMD implementation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Policy Mirror Descent (PMD) is a family of RL algorithms that regularize policy updates with KL divergence to penalize large deviations between successive policies. It averages out policy evaluation errors but requires summing all past Q-functions, which is generally intractable. Common approximations like natural policy gradient or actor-critic methods may introduce errors. Q-functions estimate the expected return of taking an action in a given state, and natural policy gradient is a second-order optimization method that provides coordinate-free updates.

## [Patch-Based 3D VAE for Turbulent Flow Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22082) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a patch-based 3D variational autoencoder (3D-VAE) that reconstructs a local 16^3 high-resolution block from a larger coarse neighborhood, enabling efficient super-resolution of turbulent channel flow. The model achieves a mean absolute error of 0.055, outperforming tricubic (0.075) and Lanczos (0.076) interpolation, and reduces spectral error by about threefold. This work addresses a key scalability challenge in 3D super-resolution for turbulence, where model complexity typically grows with domain size. By making the parameter count dependent only on patch size, it enables practical application to large-scale simulations, potentially benefiting computational fluid dynamics and related fields. The model is trained on a single DNS snapshot of turbulent channel flow at Re_tau ≈ 1000 from the Johns Hopkins Turbulence Database and evaluated on a held-out snapshot. It also demonstrates transfer to coarse finite-element simulations, but has limitations including attenuation of smallest resolved scales, periodic artefacts from patch stride, and under-prediction of extreme velocity values. A conditional 3D-GAN trained on the same data failed to converge under Wasserstein training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Direct numerical simulation (DNS) resolves all scales of turbulence but becomes computationally prohibitive at high Reynolds numbers. Super-resolution (SR) offers a practical alternative by reconstructing fine-scale structures from coarse fields. Most existing SR methods are 2D and do not extend well to 3D due to increased complexity. The proposed patch-based 3D-VAE overcomes this by applying the learned operator convolutionally across the domain.

## [RePaCA: Reasoning LLMs for Static Patch Correctness Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22580) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RePaCA is a novel static APCA technique that uses reasoning large language models (LLMs) with chain-of-thought prompting to classify patches as correct or overfitting. It achieves state-of-the-art performance with 83.1% accuracy and 84.8% F1-score on a Defects4J-derived test set. This work addresses the critical problem of overfitting patches in automated program repair (APR), which often pass tests but fail to fix the underlying bug. By improving static patch correctness assessment, RePaCA enhances the reliability and trustworthiness of APR tools, potentially reducing manual review effort and advancing the field. The model is finetuned using Reinforcement Learning with the Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm to enhance reasoning for APCA. It demonstrates superior generalization when trained on different datasets, outperforming the leading technique, and provides enhanced explainability through its reasoning process.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Automated Program Repair (APR) aims to automatically fix software bugs, but generated patches often overfit to the test suite, passing tests without truly fixing the bug. Automated Patch Correctness Assessment (APCA) addresses this by identifying overfitting patches, and static approaches use only the original and fixed code snippets. Traditional static techniques struggle with reliability, flexibility, and transparency, which RePaCA aims to overcome using reasoning LLMs.

## [LLMs Expose Social Biases Against Homelessness Across Online and Offline Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13187) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the first multi-domain corpus of social biases against people experiencing homelessness (PEH), annotated with a 16-category taxonomy, and benchmarks six prompted LLMs for bias detection. The corpus includes a 1,698-item gold-standard set and 48,389 GPT-4.1-labeled texts from Reddit, X, news, and city council transcripts across ten U.S. cities (2015-2025). This work addresses a critical social issue by providing a systematic tool to monitor and understand stigma against homelessness, which can inform policy and AI fairness efforts. The cross-domain approach and the audit protocol offer a novel way to track biases in both online discourse and offline governance, potentially influencing how municipalities address homelessness. The study found that all six LLMs exhibit moderate F1 scores but significant miscalibration: they over-tag 'not in my backyard' (NIMBY) by +11.5 percentage points and under-detect factual claims by -30.5 percentage points. Error analysis reveals that models often mistake housing-related vocabulary and question forms as opposition proxies, leading to false NIMBY labels on pro-service text.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Homelessness is a persistent social challenge, with over 876,000 people experiencing homelessness recorded in the U.S. in 2025. Social bias shapes public perception and policy, making it crucial to detect and track such biases in textual media and council discourse. The corpus and audit protocol aim to support municipal PEH stigma monitoring without treating teacher labels as ground truth.

## [Adaptive Policy Backbone: PEFT for Meta-Transfer RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22310) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Adaptive Policy Backbone (APB), a meta-transfer RL method that inserts lightweight linear layers around a shared backbone to enable parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT). APB improves sample efficiency over standard RL and adapts to out-of-distribution (OOD) tasks where existing meta-RL baselines typically fail. This work addresses the significant challenge of out-of-distribution generalization in reinforcement learning, which is crucial for deploying RL in real-world scenarios where training and deployment conditions differ. By leveraging PEFT, APB offers a parameter-efficient way to adapt policies, potentially reducing computational costs and improving sample efficiency, which could benefit applications in robotics, autonomous driving, and other domains. APB inserts lightweight linear layers before and after a shared backbone, preserving prior knowledge during adaptation while enabling PEFT. The method demonstrates improved sample efficiency over standard RL and outperforms existing meta-RL baselines on out-of-distribution tasks, as shown in the paper's experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) often requires extensive interaction data to learn optimal policies, limiting practical deployment. Meta-transfer RL aims to leverage prior knowledge from related tasks to accelerate learning on new tasks, but existing methods often struggle with out-of-distribution (OOD) tasks where the task distribution shifts. Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT), popularized in large language models, reduces the number of trainable parameters during adaptation, and APB applies this concept to RL by using a shared backbone with lightweight linear adapters.

## [Monotone and Separating Set Functions: Theory and Neural Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.23634) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Monotone and Separating (MAS) set functions that preserve the subset relation, establishes dimension bounds for finite ground sets, and shows that MAS functions do not exist for infinite ground sets, proposing a relaxed 'weakly MAS' model with Hölder continuity. This work provides a theoretical foundation for designing set-to-vector functions with an inductive bias for set containment, which is crucial for applications like set containment prediction. The proposed weakly MAS model offers a practical solution for infinite ground sets, potentially improving performance in set-related machine learning tasks. The paper establishes lower and upper bounds on the vector dimension needed for MAS functions, depending on the cardinality of multisets and the ground set. For infinite ground sets, it proves the non-existence of MAS functions and introduces a 'weakly MAS' model that is Hölder continuous, along with universal approximation results for monotone set functions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: In mathematics, a monotonic function preserves or reverses the order between ordered sets. A separating set of functions distinguishes distinct points. This paper combines these concepts to define MAS set functions, which are relevant to set containment problems. The study extends to infinite ground sets, where exact MAS functions are impossible, leading to a relaxed property.

## [Game-Theoretic Mechanism Against Dishonest LLM Providers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.00847) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the first formal economic model for a user-provider ecosystem where users delegate queries to multiple LLM providers, and proves the existence of an approximate incentive-compatible mechanism with an additive approximation ratio of O(T^{1-ε} log T) for any ε in (0, 1/2). It also establishes an impossibility result showing that no mechanism can guarantee asymptotically better expected user utility. This work addresses a critical vulnerability in LLM APIs: dishonest providers may substitute models or inflate tokens, undermining user trust and market efficiency. By providing a theoretically grounded, incentive-compatible mechanism, it offers a path toward more reliable and trustworthy LLM services, which is essential as LLM APIs become ubiquitous in industry. The mechanism is designed for a continuous strategy space and guarantees quasi-linear second-best user utility. Simulation experiments with real-world API settings demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: LLM APIs are typically billed per token, and users rely on providers to deliver the advertised model. However, providers may secretly substitute a cheaper model or inflate token counts to increase revenue, a problem known as model substitution and token inflation. Mechanism design, a branch of game theory, aims to design rules that incentivize honest behavior even when participants are self-interested.

## [KRPO: Label-Free Prompt Optimization for Open-Domain Triplet Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15037) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces KRPO (Knowledge Restoration-driven Prompt Optimization), a label-free framework that adapts LLMs to open-domain relational triplet extraction (ORTE) by restoring extracted triplets into textual statements and using semantic consistency with source inputs as intrinsic feedback to iteratively optimize prompts. It also includes a Memory-augmented Relation Canonicalizer to align free-form relations with a dynamically updated schema memory. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in adapting LLMs to open-domain contexts without labeled data, which is common in real-world knowledge graph construction. By enabling label-free prompt optimization and improving relation consistency, KRPO could significantly enhance the practicality and scalability of LLM-based information extraction systems. KRPO achieves the best average F1 score across three ORTE benchmarks with multiple LLM backbones. The framework uses knowledge restoration-based self-evaluation to accumulate experience from previous errors and iteratively derive more effective prompts, contrasting with static prompt methods like EDC.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Open-domain Relational Triplet Extraction (ORTE) aims to mine structured knowledge without predefined relation schemas, a task that has evolved from rule-based OpenIE to LLM-driven approaches. Automatic prompt optimization (APO) typically requires labeled validation data, but collecting such labels is costly. Label-free methods like KRPO use intrinsic feedback from the LLM itself to guide optimization, which is a growing area of research.

## [HOLO Plugin Exploits LLM Holographic Characteristic for Faster Short-Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22546) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper identifies a 'Holographic Characteristic' in LLMs, where target-side keywords are captured early in generation, and proposes a plugin called HOLO that leverages this to extract keywords and generate short text in parallel, achieving comparable performance to baselines. This work introduces a novel observation about LLM generation that can be exploited for efficiency gains, offering a practical plugin for faster short-text generation without requiring a draft model. It contributes to ongoing efforts in optimizing LLM inference, potentially benefiting applications like chatbots and content generation. HOLO extracts target-side keywords within a limited number of generation steps and complements the sentence using parallel lexically constrained text generation. Experiments were conducted on language models of varying architectures and scales, showing comparable performance in both automatic and human-like evaluation metrics.

Lexically constrained text generation aims to control output by incorporating pre-specified keywords, and parallel decoding methods can speed up generation. The Holographic Characteristic suggests that early generation steps contain rich target-side semantic information, which HOLO exploits.

## [Curvature-Aware MDL Framework for Layer-Adaptive LLM Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00910) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a curvature-aware, MDL-inspired framework for allocating capacity and pruning layers in large language models under hardware budgets. It formulates two convex programs that yield unique globally optimal solutions, computable in O(K log(1/epsilon)) time via bisection. This framework provides a principled alternative to heuristic score-to-decision methods, potentially improving efficiency and performance of LLM deployment under resource constraints. It addresses a significant problem in model compression and could impact practitioners working on LLM optimization. The method uses layer gain zeta_k^2 = g_k^T H_kk^{-1} g_k, which incorporates inverse local curvature, and normalizes gains into scores q_k. Experiments on Mistral-7B and Gemma-7B show clear allocation gains in some settings and competitive, though mixed, pruning performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models have non-uniform layer-wise capacity, with some layers contributing more to loss reduction than others. Existing layer-scoring methods provide sensitivity estimates but lack a principled rule for converting them into allocation or pruning decisions under a global budget. The minimum description length (MDL) principle is a model selection criterion that favors models minimizing the total description length, formalizing Occam's razor. Curvature-aware compression methods use second-order information to guide pruning, which can be more effective than gradient-only approaches.

## [Learning Netlist Representations from Imperfect LLM-Generated RTL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09161) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a novel framework that uses imperfect LLM-generated RTL code as training data for netlist representation learning, exploiting structural patterns in synthesized netlists despite functional errors. The approach forms an end-to-end pipeline from automated code generation to downstream tasks, and evaluations show it matches or surpasses methods trained on scarce high-quality data. This work addresses the critical data bottleneck in circuit representation learning by providing a cost-effective way to generate large-scale training data without relying on expensive, IP-protected real designs. It could significantly advance ML for EDA, enabling better scalability to realistic designs and improving tasks like sub-circuit boundary identification and component classification. The framework is evaluated on circuit functional understanding tasks, including sub-circuit boundary identification and component classification, across benchmarks of increasing scales, extending from operator-level to IP-level tasks. The key insight is that even functionally incorrect LLM-generated RTL, when synthesized, preserves structural patterns indicative of intended functionality.

Register-Transfer-Level (RTL) is a design abstraction in digital circuit design, and LLMs can generate RTL code at scale, though often with functional errors. This paper leverages the structural information in synthesized netlists from imperfect RTL to overcome the data scarcity problem.

## [OPERA: Dynamic Data Pruning Boosts Dense Retrieval Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17205) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OPERA, a data pruning framework for dense retrieval models, featuring a two-stage dynamic pruning strategy that adaptively modulates sampling probabilities at query and document levels during training. Experiments across eight datasets show dynamic pruning improves NDCG@10 by +1.0 points and Recall@20 by +0.4 points over standard fine-tuning, while achieving comparable performance in less than 50% of the training time. This work addresses a critical efficiency bottleneck in domain-specific fine-tuning of dense retrievers, which are widely used in search and retrieval-augmented generation systems. By reducing training time while improving effectiveness, OPERA could enable faster adaptation of retrieval models to new domains, benefiting both researchers and practitioners. The study first examines static pruning, which retains only high-similarity query-document pairs, revealing a quality-coverage tradeoff: NDCG improves while Recall degrades due to reduced query diversity. The proposed dynamic pruning strategy resolves this tradeoff by maintaining access to the full training set while prioritizing high-quality examples, and its benefits are confirmed on Qwen3-Embedding, an LLM-based dense retriever, showing architecture-agnostic gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Dense retrieval models encode queries and documents into dense vectors and use similarity search to retrieve relevant items. They are often fine-tuned on domain-specific data, but not all training pairs are equally informative, and pruning can reduce training cost. However, naive pruning may hurt recall by reducing query diversity. NDCG and Recall are standard metrics for evaluating ranking and retrieval effectiveness.

The framework uses Channel-Group SHAP to estimate contributions of grouped input channels to pixel-level predictions. The ADAGE framework employs Channel-Group SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) to estimate the contributions of grouped input channels to pixel-level predictions. Experiments on two satellite-based flood mapping tasks demonstrate that the framework can quantitatively assess alignment and help domain experts identify misaligned explanations through alignment scores.

## [TimeRFT: Reinforcement Fine-Tuning Boosts Time Series Foundation Model Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00015) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TimeRFT introduces a reinforcement learning-based fine-tuning paradigm for time series foundation models (TSFMs), featuring a quality-aware temporal reward mechanism and a difficulty-aware data selection strategy. It consistently outperforms supervised fine-tuning (SFT) methods across diverse real-world forecasting benchmarks under different data regimes. This work addresses critical challenges in adapting TSFMs to downstream tasks, namely temporal distribution shift and limited data availability, which are common in real-world forecasting. By improving generalization, TimeRFT could enhance the practical utility of TSFMs across industries like finance, retail, and energy, where accurate forecasting under shifting conditions is vital. TimeRFT's reward mechanism provides fine-grained credit assignment by evaluating the contribution of each prediction step to overall forecasting performance. The difficulty-aware data selection prioritizes informative samples with generalizable patterns, and experiments show improved accuracy and robustness against unforeseen distribution shifts compared to SFT.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Time series foundation models (TSFMs) are pre-trained models that forecast, classify, and detect anomalies in time series data without needing a separate model for each dataset or industry. They use transformer architectures and large-scale datasets to generalize across domains. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) is a common adaptation method, but it can overfit due to distribution shifts and limited data. Reinforcement learning fine-tuning, as proposed by TimeRFT, offers an alternative that optimizes for long-term forecasting quality.

## [Predict-then-Diffuse: Adaptive Response Length for Compute-Budgeted Diffusion LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04215) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Predict-then-Diffuse, a model-agnostic framework that estimates the optimal response length before generation using an Adaptive Response Length Predictor (AdaRLP), enabling compute-budgeted inference in diffusion LLMs. This approach reduces computational waste on padding tokens and avoids costly re-computations from truncation, as validated on multiple datasets. Diffusion LLMs offer parallel token generation but suffer from fixed response length constraints, leading to inefficiency. This framework addresses a critical bottleneck, potentially improving deployment efficiency and reducing latency for real-world applications, making diffusion LLMs more practical. The framework includes a data-driven safety mechanism that slightly increases the predicted length to mitigate underestimation and avoid re-runs. Experimental results show significant FLOP reductions compared to default D-LLM inference while maintaining output quality and robustness to skewed data distributions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion LLMs generate text by iteratively denoising a sequence of tokens in parallel, unlike autoregressive models that generate one token at a time. However, they require a fixed response length before generation, which can lead to wasted computation on padding or truncation errors. Response length prediction is a known technique to optimize inference efficiency, and this work adapts it specifically for diffusion LLMs.

## [Gen4Regen: Using Image Generators to Create Synthetic Data for Forest Regeneration Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05627) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Gen4Regen, a framework that uses the Nano Banana Pro image generator to create synthetic training data with pixel-aligned semantic masks for fine-grained semantic segmentation of plants in forest regeneration zones. It also introduces WilDReF-Q-V2, an expanded natural forest dataset with 13,977 unlabelled and 50 hand-labelled real images. This work addresses the critical bottleneck of data scarcity and class imbalance in forest regeneration mapping, which is essential for sustainable forest management. By demonstrating that AI-generated data can significantly boost segmentation performance, it opens new avenues for applying generative models to niche environmental monitoring tasks where expert labels are scarce. The Gen4Regen dataset contains 2,101 pairs of synthetic images and semantic masks. Unified training with real and synthetic data improved the F1 score by over 15 percentage points compared to purely supervised baselines, and some underrepresented classes saw per-class F1 gains of over 30 percentage points.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Forest regeneration mapping traditionally relies on labor-intensive ground surveys, while UAVs offer scalable data collection but are bottlenecked by the lack of expert-annotated imagery. Semantic segmentation is a computer vision task that assigns a class label to each pixel, which is crucial for identifying plant species in high-resolution aerial imagery. Nano Banana Pro is a large-scale image generation model based on Google's Gemini 3 Pro Image, capable of generating high-fidelity images and corresponding masks from prompts.

## [Spiking Neural Networks Detect AI-Generated Videos via Temporal Smoothness Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05895) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MAST, a detector that uses spiking neural networks (SNNs) to identify AI-generated videos by exploiting temporal smoothness gaps at pixel and semantic levels. On the GenVideo benchmark, MAST achieves 93.14% mean accuracy across 10 unseen generators under strict cross-generator evaluation. This work addresses the growing challenge of detecting photorealistic AI-generated videos, which pose risks of misinformation and fraud. By demonstrating that SNNs can match or surpass traditional ANN-based detectors while being event-driven and sparse, it opens new avenues for efficient and robust deepfake detection in real-world applications. MAST processes multi-channel temporal residuals with a spike-driven temporal branch alongside a frozen semantic encoder for cross-generator generalization. The method leverages the observation that fake clips elicit firing predominantly at object and motion boundaries in SNNs, unlike real clips.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: AI-generated videos are often photorealistic at the single-frame level, making inter-frame dynamics a key detection cue. Traditional detectors use generic temporal backbones, fixed video-level descriptors, or detection metrics, but they degrade under cross-generator evaluation. Spiking neural networks (SNNs) are event-driven and sparsely activated, making them naturally suited to capture sparse, asynchronous temporal artifacts. GenVidBench is a benchmark for AI-generated video detection, and GenVideo is a related evaluation set.

## [MANTA: Multi-Turn Adversarial Benchmark for LLM Animal Welfare Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16301) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MANTA, a benchmark of 1,088 five-turn conversations that tests LLMs' value stability and moral sensitivity in animal welfare reasoning under adversarial pressure. It evaluates seven frontier models and reveals that multi-turn evaluation changes model rankings compared to single-turn benchmarks. MANTA addresses underexplored failure modes in LLM safety evaluation: alignment degradation under sustained adversarial pressure and moral sensitivity in everyday queries. This could lead to more robust AI ethics evaluations and safer deployment in contexts where animal welfare is implicitly relevant. The benchmark scores conversations on Animal Welfare Value Stability (AWVS) and Animal Welfare Moral Sensitivity (AWMS), using a five-type pressure taxonomy (Social, Cultural, Economic, Pragmatic, Epistemic). Results show that 4 of 7 models change rank relative to Turn 1, and welfare robustness depends jointly on animal species and pressure type.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Existing benchmarks like AnimalHarmBench evaluate animal welfare reasoning through single-turn, explicitly framed questions, missing multi-turn dynamics. MANTA builds on this by simulating realistic multi-turn interactions and adversarial pressure, providing a more comprehensive assessment. The benchmark is released with dataset, pressure plans, judge prompts, and analysis code.

## [Quantization Can Break Recourse; New Metrics and Training Method Fix It](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17160) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces counterfactual sensitivity under quantization, proposing two metrics (Validity Drop and Counterfactual Recourse Gap) and a quantization-aware training method called Counterfactual-Faithful Quantization (CFQ) that preserves recourse behavior in quantized models. This work highlights a critical but overlooked issue: quantization can degrade algorithmic recourse even when accuracy is preserved, undermining the reliability and fairness of deployed decision systems. By providing metrics and a mitigation method, it enables practitioners to build quantized models that maintain both efficiency and actionable recourse, which is essential for high-stakes applications like credit and criminal justice. The proposed CFQ method jointly learns quantizer parameters and mixed-precision bit allocation while preserving target predictions at teacher-generated recourse points. It is compatible with standard LSQ/PACT-style quantizers and can also be used as a training-free calibration procedure for post-training quantization. Experiments on Adult, German Credit, and COMPAS show that CFQ reduces Validity Drop and Counterfactual Recourse Gap compared to accuracy-centric baselines; for example, on Adult, VD/CRG drop from 0.121/0.162 to 0.061/0.071.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Model quantization is a widely used technique to reduce memory, latency, and deployment cost by lowering the precision of weights and activations (e.g., from FP32 to INT8). Algorithmic recourse refers to providing individuals with actionable recommendations to reverse unfavorable decisions made by automated systems, often based on counterfactual explanations. This paper addresses the intersection of these two areas, showing that quantization can break recourse even when accuracy is preserved.
