## [AI4BayesCode: LLM-driven validated modular stateful Bayesian samplers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18476) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AI4BayesCode is an extensible LLM-driven system that translates natural-language Bayesian model descriptions into runnable, validated MCMC samplers. It introduces a novel recursively stateful coding paradigm and a modular design with pre- and post-generation validation. This addresses a major bottleneck in Bayesian workflows by automating sampler generation, reducing the need for manual coding of complex algorithms. It could make Bayesian inference more accessible to non-experts and improve reliability through validation and modular composability. The system decomposes models into modular sampling blocks, each mapped to built-in components, and uses a recursively stateful paradigm to compose blocks coherently. A benchmark suite is provided, and experiments show it can implement a wide range of models from natural language alone; software is available at ai4bayescode.com.

**Background**: Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) is a widely used technique for sampling from complex probability distributions, but writing correct samplers is often difficult and error-prone. Existing probabilistic programming systems have limitations in model support, extensibility, and composability. AI4BayesCode leverages large language models to automate this process, aiming to improve reliability and usability.

## [LoRA Fine-Tuning of 3B SLM for Telecom Support with Energy Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a systematic comparative study of 16 LoRA configurations for fine-tuning Qwen2.5-3B, a small language model, for telecommunications customer support. It introduces a combinatorial synthetic data generation method using a glossary of 52 terms to create ~30,000 training examples, and evaluates configurations with both quantitative metrics and energy consumption analysis. This work addresses the need for domain-specific, energy-efficient fine-tuning of small language models in telecommunications, where data sovereignty and regulatory constraints limit the use of external LLMs. The finding that validation loss does not correlate with qualitative performance highlights the importance of holistic evaluation, which could influence how practitioners select fine-tuning configurations for conversational AI. The best validation loss (0.5024) ranked only 6th-7th in qualitative evaluation, while the worst loss (0.6807) ranked first according to both LLM judges (GPT-5.2 and Claude 4.5 Sonnet). The study also provides an energy-performance trade-off analysis, contributing insights into sustainable LLM deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) techniques like LoRA allow adapting large pre-trained models to specific tasks by updating only a small fraction of parameters, reducing computational costs. Small language models (SLMs) are streamlined for speed and efficiency, making them suitable for domain-specific applications. In telecommunications, handling sensitive customer data requires on-premise or private deployment, making PEFT and SLMs attractive.

MSFAN consistently outperforms state-of-the-art models on the classification task, reaching 85.2% accuracy. The dataset includes pure polymers, multilayer films, commercial blends, and biopolymers, and the model uses feature gating for signal recalibration and multi-scale convolutions to capture diverse frequency patterns.

## [Decidable Criterion for Creative Integration via Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13977) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a formal, decidable criterion for creative integration (CI), defining it as a strict compression of description length that resolves a real conflict, validated by four falsifiable tests. The criterion is operationalized through four binary, conjunctive gates and a taxonomy of pseudo-integration. This provides a measurable, objective way to distinguish genuine creative integration from mere re-description, which could impact AI creativity evaluation and cognitive science research. It moves the discussion from philosophical debate to empirical testability, offering a citable primitive for future work. The criterion requires that the compression ratio C = L_pre/L_post > 1, with the reduction located in the conflict itself, and is validated by four tests: independent computational check, discrimination against hard negatives, out-of-sample prediction, and description-language robustness. The paper claims only the sign of C-1, not generation, and states as a conjecture whether all creativity is so constituted.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: The paper builds on the lineage that treats creativity and intelligence as compression, such as the minimum description length (MDL) principle. Decidability in logic refers to the existence of an effective method to determine the truth of a statement, and falsifiability is a key aspect of scientific testability. The criterion aims to operationalize the vague notion of 'integrative' solutions.

SqLinear introduces Square Partition, a geometry-adaptive algorithm that partitions traffic sensors into balanced, non-overlapping, compact regions, and a Hierarchical Linear Interaction (HLI) module that replaces attention with linear interactions. Experiments show it reduces MAE by 2.30% on average and up to 6.78% under extreme scalability, while cutting training runtime by 13.27%–30.84%. This addresses a critical scalability bottleneck in deploying traffic forecasting models to real-world urban networks with thousands of sensors. By achieving linear complexity without sacrificing accuracy, SqLinear could enable more efficient and practical intelligent transportation systems. Square Partition provides provable guarantees on partition utilization and split balance, unlike previous heuristic-based methods. The HLI module propagates global inter-region dependencies and refines them at the node level with linear computational complexity, avoiding costly attention mechanisms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Traffic forecasting is essential for intelligent transportation systems, but neural network models often struggle with scalability when applied to large sensor networks. Previous attempts used spatial database partitioning, but relied on handcrafted heuristics that led to imbalanced partitions and degraded accuracy. SqLinear's balanced square partitioning and linear interaction design aim to overcome these limitations.

## [DRIFT: A New Framework for LLM Self-Improvement via Difficulty Routing and Rhythm Gating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30345) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DRIFT is an online self-evolution policy optimization framework for large language models that introduces Difficulty Routing and Rhythm Gating to dynamically allocate self-distillation and reinforcement learning signals. It achieves state-of-the-art results, outperforming GRPO by 9.5% and SDPO by 7.5% on average across five benchmarks. This framework addresses a critical challenge in LLM self-improvement: the lack of explicit mechanisms to track problem-level learning progress and adapt optimization strategies. By improving training efficiency and reasoning performance, DRIFT could enable more stable and effective self-improvement for LLMs without external supervision, benefiting the broader AI community. DRIFT uses a success buffer and a two-stage curriculum learning strategy to preserve high-quality historical experience and guide the model from reliable behavior acquisition to stable policy evolution. On the ToolUse benchmark, DRIFT achieves 79.2% accuracy, improving over GRPO by 13.5% and SDPO by 10.7%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often struggle with stable self-improvement on complex reasoning tasks without external expert supervision. Existing methods like self-distillation and reinforcement learning (e.g., GRPO, SDPO) lack explicit mechanisms to track problem-level learning progress, leading to over-optimization on easy problems and weak supervision on hard ones. DRIFT addresses this by jointly using Difficulty Routing to allocate training signals at the problem level and Rhythm Gating to refine policy updates at the token level.

## [BeatEdit: First Explicit Editing Framework for Symbolic Music Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11124) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BeatEdit introduces the first symbolic music generation framework based on explicit edit operations, leveraging the BEAT encoding to enable selective modification of drafts. It comprises three mechanisms—per-token sequence tagging, iterative refinement, and tag-then-fill—sharing a single pre-trained backbone and achieving high precision with inference under 100 ms. This work addresses a significant gap in symbolic music generation, which has traditionally focused on generating complete sequences from scratch. By enabling explicit editing, BeatEdit could improve human-AI collaboration in music creation and inspire further research into edit-based generative models for other structured domains. The BEAT encoding, originally designed for autoregressive generation, provides structural properties amenable to editing, such as beat-grid anchoring. Cross-encoding evaluation reveals that encoding design significantly influences editing effectiveness, with notable encoding-method interaction effects. Code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Symbolic music generation typically uses event-based encodings that represent notes as sequences of tokens, but these lack the structural properties needed for explicit editing. The BEAT encoding, which anchors tokens to musical beats, preserves musical priors like pitch- and time-shift invariance, making it suitable for edit-based approaches. Edit-based methods have been successful in text transformation but were unexplored for music until now.

## [AuEmoChat: Authentic Emotion Understanding and Rendering for Conversational Speech Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15755) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AuEmoChat introduces a novel CSS framework with AuEmoCodec, which learns a discrete authentic emotion token space via finite scalar quantization, and AuEmoToMe, an emotion-guided token merging algorithm to reduce redundancy in multimodal dialogue history. It also proposes Authentic Emotion Flow Matching for speech rendering, achieving state-of-the-art performance on the NCSSD-EmCap dataset. This work addresses key limitations in conversational speech synthesis by moving beyond predefined emotion categories and handling redundant multimodal tokens, enabling more authentic and context-aware emotional speech. It could influence future research in emotion modeling and multimodal speech synthesis, benefiting applications like virtual assistants and interactive AI systems. AuEmoCodec uses finite scalar quantization (FSQ) to build a discrete emotion codebook, providing finer-grained emotion representation than seven basic categories. AuEmoToMe merges redundant tokens in dialogue history while preserving emotion-relevant context, and the framework integrates these into an autoregressive text-speech model with Authentic Emotion Flow Matching for rendering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Conversational Speech Synthesis (CSS) aims to generate speech with human-like emotional expression and contextual consistency in user-agent interactions. Traditional methods rely on limited predefined emotion labels, which fail to capture the nuance of authentic emotions, and multimodal dialogue history often contains redundant tokens that hinder context understanding. Finite scalar quantization (FSQ) is a technique that replaces vector quantization in VQ-VAEs with a simpler scheme, leading to smaller and more expressive discrete representations. Token merging (ToMe) is a method that speeds up transformer-based networks by merging redundant tokens, which AuEmoToMe adapts for emotion-guided merging.

## [EduPanel: Three-Agent LLM Judge for Teaching Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18529) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EduPanel is a novel rubric-grounded, learner-conditioned LLM judge that decomposes teaching video evaluation across three specialized agents, achieving reliability comparable to a median human expert. In expert studies, its feedback improved scoring accuracy (MAE from 0.87 to 0.73) while experts could still detect unreliable outputs (AUC = 0.77). This addresses the growing need for scalable, interpretable evaluation of teaching videos, a major educational medium. By demonstrating human-comparable reliability and supporting human oversight, EduPanel could serve as an effective assistant for educational evaluation, potentially transforming how teaching quality is assessed at scale. EduPanel is learner-conditioned, meaning evaluations are tailored to the intended learner rather than being universal. The system uses a rubric-grounded approach and decomposes evaluation across specialized agents to produce interpretable assessments for different aspects of teaching quality.

## [ElasticTTT: Preventing Prior Collapse in Test-Time Video Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21529) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ElasticTTT introduces a novel framework that addresses prior collapse in test-time tuning for video editing by preserving the generative distribution through three techniques: Target Distribution Regularization, Contrastive CFG, and Asynchronous Noise Schedule. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on one-shot video editing tasks. This work addresses a fundamental mismatch between generative models and single-point optimization in test-time tuning, which can lead to prior collapse and degraded editing quality. By preserving the generative prior, ElasticTTT could improve the reliability and versatility of diffusion-based video editing, benefiting applications in content creation and post-production. The framework includes Target Distribution Regularization to avoid sharp memorization minima, Contrastive CFG to guide inference away from source biases, and Asynchronous Noise Schedule to preserve unedited regions. The paper provides theoretical analysis and extensive evaluations supporting the effectiveness of these techniques.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time tuning (TTT) adapts pretrained diffusion models for specific tasks like video editing, but standard TTT optimizes a single point, conflicting with the distribution-mapping nature of generative models. This mismatch can cause prior collapse, where the model ignores text conditions and spatial latents, collapsing outputs to the source video or entangling features. Prior collapse is related to mode collapse in GANs and model collapse in generative AI, where models lose diversity or degrade when trained on generated data. Contrastive CFG is a technique that improves classifier-free guidance by using contrastive loss to balance alignment and concept removal, avoiding artifacts from naive negative guidance.

## [DualityCert: Verifier-Gated LLM Repair of Broken Seiberg Duality Claims](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23614) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DualityCert introduces a symbolic verifier that checks 't Hooft anomaly matching, superpotential R-charge consistency, central-charge matching, and a bounded chiral-ring proxy for candidate Seiberg-duality claims in 4D N=1 quiver gauge theories. Using this verifier as a repair environment, language-model agents improved repair success by up to 8.3 percentage points on a benchmark of 145 broken claims. This work demonstrates a novel verifier-gated approach that combines symbolic verification with language models to repair theoretical physics claims, potentially accelerating AI-assisted research in quantum field theory. The framework's success on a preregistered benchmark suggests it could be extended to other domains where formal verification is possible. The verifier provides a consistency certificate, not a proof of duality. On the benchmark, verifier-gated retry improved success by +8.3 pp on deepseek-chat and +7.1 pp on qwen-plus (Holm-adjusted p<0.002). The stop-first strategy portfolio underperformed independent verifier-filtered resampling by 10.3 pp on deepseek-chat but outperformed it by 14.7 pp on qwen-plus, showing model-dependent policy effectiveness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Seiberg duality, conjectured by Nathan Seiberg in 1994, is an S-duality relating two different supersymmetric QCD theories that flow to the same infrared fixed point. Quiver gauge theories describe D-branes on orbifolds and are common in string theory. 't Hooft anomaly matching is a consistency condition for gauge theories, and language models are increasingly used for scientific discovery, but ensuring correctness remains challenging.

## [LEX-EC: Black-Box Audit Framework for LLM Personality Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24435) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LEX-EC, a reusable black-box audit framework that combines prevalence and agreement diagnostics with controlled lexical ablation to assess personality signal in LLMs. It demonstrates that different text genres yield varying levels of trait-associated signal, with free-form essays showing the broadest but still weak signal. This framework addresses the interpretability gap in LLM personality classification, offering practical diagnostics for black-box settings. It could help researchers and practitioners better understand and audit how LLMs infer personality traits, leading to more transparent and reliable AI systems. LEX-EC evaluates classification prevalence, item-level association, chance-corrected agreement, persistence under lexical restriction, and prompt sensitivity. The study found that masking topical and demographic content weakened some associations while leaving others detectable from function words, affective terms, and cognitive-style vocabulary.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) can assign personality labels from text, but their interpretability remains an open problem. Lexical ablation is a technique that systematically removes or masks specific words or tokens to assess their contribution to model predictions. Prevalence and agreement diagnostics are methods used in auditing to measure how often a label is assigned and how consistent the model is across different inputs or runs.

## [A2TTA: Test-Time Adaptation for Evolving Traffic Sensor Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25875) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces A2TTA, a novel test-time adaptation framework that handles topology expansion and multi-scale temporal shifts in evolving traffic sensor networks. It transforms topology-induced errors into an expandable output calibration problem and separates temporal adaptation into persistent global correction and agile context-specific specialization. This work addresses a practical yet under-explored problem in smart city traffic forecasting, where sensor networks continuously evolve. By enabling efficient adaptation of pretrained models during deployment, A2TTA could improve the robustness and accuracy of traffic prediction systems in real-world dynamic environments. A2TTA was evaluated on ten real-world traffic networks, consistently improving forecasting performance across different backbones, datasets, and prediction horizons. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/lixus7/A2TTA.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time adaptation (TTA) is a machine learning technique that adapts pre-trained models to new data distributions at inference time without retraining, which is crucial for handling domain shifts. Traffic forecasting often uses graph neural networks (GNNs) to model sensor networks as graphs, but traditional methods assume fixed sensor graphs, which is unrealistic as real-world networks evolve. A2TTA specifically tackles the challenges of topology expansion and multi-scale temporal shifts in such evolving networks.

The paper introduces PALATE, a scalable benchmark for role-playing agents that uses person-aligned LLM-simulated users instead of fixed dialogue histories. It trains per-user simulators and constructs personalized rubrics, showing higher agreement with human judgments than general rubrics. This addresses a critical gap in evaluating role-playing agents, as fixed histories and rubrics fail to capture real multi-turn interactions and individual user satisfaction. PALATE provides more interpretable and user-centric evaluations, potentially influencing future LLM evaluation methodologies. PALATE includes a pool of 300 character profiles and trains five per-user simulators for the main evaluation. It evaluates 16 candidates along three tracks: generic turn quality, long-horizon session capability, and per-user experience, producing evaluations for specific user-RPA pairs.

## [EOMR: Joint Feature Subset and Subspace Learning for Nonlinear Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28080) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Entropy-Optimal Manifold Regression (EOMR), an extension of EOMC that simultaneously identifies relevant feature subsets and subspaces in nonlinear regression problems. It demonstrates robust performance with linear scaling in iteration and memory complexity on chaotic and plasma dynamics benchmarks. EOMR offers a new approach to feature selection and subspace learning that outperforms state-of-the-art ML and AI tools by orders of magnitude in prediction error and model complexity on challenging scientific problems. This could significantly impact machine learning applications in chaotic and plasma dynamics, where traditional methods struggle. EOMR is compared against gradient boosted random forests, deep neural networks, and TabPFN v.03 on Lorenz-96 (F=8, F=12) and Hasegawa-Wakatani data. For the Hasegawa-Wakatani example, EOMR distills an 8-parameter linear autoregressive model for the leading EOF dynamics.

**Background**: EOMC is a metricized, entropy-regularized extension of PCA clustering that achieves linear-in-T scaling and robustly recovers nonlinear, nonstationary manifolds. The Lorenz-96 model is a classic chaotic dynamical system used to test prediction methods, while the Hasegawa-Wakatani model describes electrostatic resistive drift wave turbulence in tokamak edge plasma.

## [Lift4D: Single-Video 4D Reconstruction of Dynamic Objects](https://lift4d.github.io/) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Lift4D is a new test-time optimization framework that reconstructs full 360° geometry, textures, and non-rigid deformations of dynamic objects from a single video, including unseen viewpoints. It adapts an existing single-view 3D reconstruction model to produce temporally consistent per-frame predictions via causal latent conditioning, providing a coherent initialization for a deformable 3D Gaussian Splatting representation. This advancement could significantly impact computer vision and graphics by enabling high-fidelity 4D reconstruction from readily available monocular video, with applications in AR/VR, film production, and robotics. It addresses limitations of previous methods that required multi-view inputs or struggled with temporal consistency and unseen viewpoints. The framework is presented at SIGGRAPH Asia 2026, with an arXiv paper (2606.23688) and open-source code available on GitHub. It uses a deformable 3D Gaussian Splatting representation, which enables efficient and high-quality rendering of dynamic scenes.

telegram · ai_newz · Aug 4, 18:29

**Background**: 4D reconstruction aims to capture the 3D geometry and appearance of dynamic objects over time, which is challenging due to non-rigid deformations and occlusions. Traditional methods often require multi-view camera setups or assume static scenes. Recent advances in neural rendering and 3D Gaussian Splatting have enabled more flexible reconstruction from monocular video, but maintaining temporal consistency and handling unseen viewpoints remains difficult. Lift4D addresses these issues by leveraging a single-view 3D estimation model with causal latent conditioning to provide a strong prior for the deformable representation.

## [Evidence-Grounded Constraint Checking in Construction Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29058) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces an evidence-grounded pipeline for constraint checking in construction documents, which normalizes extracted facts, executes four-state rules deterministically, retains source spans, and escalates unresolved cases. The evaluation shows that reallocating a four-image budget from page overviews to one overview and three overlapping tiles improves decision accuracy by 10.6 percentage points in a repeated test. This work addresses the challenge of evidence allocation in document AI, specifically for construction document review, where decisions depend on relations among text, geometry, pages, and revisions. The findings highlight a resolution-breadth trade-off rather than a universal advantage for region-focused evidence, which could inform the design of more effective document review systems. The pipeline was evaluated on 160 reference-based tasks from 29 construction projects using a repeated four-system test and a disjoint two-system breadth extension. In the repeated test, the improvement was statistically significant (95% CI: 4.3 to 18.0; exact p = 0.031), but the effect did not persist in the broader block, where Region-RAG changed accuracy by -4.1 points (95% CI: -10.2 to 1.9; p = 0.209). Exact finding-set recovery remains low, false passes remain common, and repeated-run agreement is poorly calibrated.

**Background**: Professional-document review is a constraint-checking problem where decisions depend on relations among text, geometry, pages, and document revisions. Evidence-grounded pipelines aim to improve accuracy by allocating visual evidence (e.g., PDF images) to support rule-based checks. ## [Scaffolding Critical GenAI Engagement for Ethnic Minority Students](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28630) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This design-based research study, involving 78 ethnic minority preparatory students in China, demonstrates that pedagogical scaffolding—including a human-in-the-loop workflow and teacher modeling with contrasting cases—can shift students from passive GenAI consumption to critical co-creation. The intervention led to a significant increase in prompt self-efficacy and a realignment of strategic planning toward critical evaluation and peer co-construction. This research addresses a critical challenge in AI education: preventing cognitive laziness and fostering epistemic agency among marginalized student groups. It provides evidence that technical training alone is insufficient, offering a model for educators to design targeted interventions that promote critical human-AI collaboration. The study used epistemic network analysis to analyze collaborative discourse, thematic analysis for student reflections, and paired-samples t-tests for prompt self-efficacy changes. Results revealed a 'strategic repurposing' phenomenon, where students initially used strategy talk for efficient copying but later realigned it to scaffold critical evaluation and peer co-construction.

## [HenTwin: A Digital Twin Framework for Poultry Health Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28652) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HenTwin introduces a five-layer IoT-based digital twin framework for longitudinal biological state monitoring in laying hens, using a four-dimensional state vector and a discrete-time transition model estimated from 25 weeks of multimodal data collected from 150 Lohmann LSL-Lite hens. This work formalizes state-aware digital twin inference in precision livestock farming, potentially improving early-life monitoring and management of poultry health. It demonstrates the feasibility of applying state-space modeling and IoT to agricultural settings, which could lead to more efficient and data-driven farming practices. The state vector integrates body surface temperature, acoustic energy entropy, band energy ratio, and optical-flow-based motion, with temperature-humidity index as an exogenous input. Perturbation analysis shows a sustained +2.0 THI increase leads to a stable long-run acoustic entropy elevation of 0.54 nats, and Pettitt change-point detection identifies coordinated transitions at Weeks 12-14.

## [GRU, LSTM, Transformer Compared for ADS Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28665) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper evaluates GRU, LSTM, and Transformer encoder models for classifying Level 2 automated driving systems (Comma Openpilot, Tesla Autopilot, Cadillac Super Cruise) and manual driving using vehicle telematics data. All models achieve high macro F1-scores on clean data (0.92, 0.90, 0.93 respectively), but temporal jitter severely degrades performance to 0.44-0.50. This work addresses the critical need for independent monitoring of which ADS is active, which is important for safety, regulatory compliance, insurance, and anomaly detection. The robustness evaluation framework highlights vulnerabilities to temporal jitter, guiding future improvements in real-world telematics-based classification systems. The paper introduces a modular robustness evaluation framework simulating five corruption families at five severity levels (L1-L5), including additive white Gaussian noise with cumulative drift, correlated cross-channel noise, temporal jitter, burst loss, delayed transitions, spurious toggles, and cross-feature inconsistencies. Threat-matched training yields macro F1-scores of 0.904-0.916 with only a modest clean-data penalty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Automated driving systems (ADSs) are becoming common, and future software-defined vehicles may run multiple ADSs, both native and aftermarket. Sequence-based deep learning models like GRU, LSTM, and Transformers are widely used for classifying time-series data, such as vehicle telematics, which includes continuous signals and binary event signals. Robustness to data corruption is crucial for real-world deployment, where sensor noise and communication errors are common.

## [SEDR-Seq2P: Lightweight Dilated Residual Network for Industrial NILM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28693) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces SEDR-Seq2P, a lightweight Seq2Point extension with dilated residual blocks and squeeze-and-excitation attention, for multi-task industrial NILM. It reduces MAE by ~7%, improves R² by ~1%, and increases match rate by ~0.8% over Seq2Point, while cutting inference latency by ~58% compared to WaveNet. This work addresses the gap between accuracy and computational cost in industrial NILM, offering a scalable solution for real-time energy disaggregation in factories. It demonstrates that lightweight architectures can achieve competitive accuracy, potentially enabling broader deployment of NILM systems in industrial settings. The model is evaluated on the IMDELD dataset under a unified protocol, benchmarking against Seq2Seq, Seq2SubSeq, Seq2Point, GRU, and WaveNet. The architecture uses dilated residual blocks and squeeze-and-excitation attention to balance accuracy and latency, achieving a favorable trade-off for industrial deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Non-intrusive load monitoring (NILM), also known as energy disaggregation, is the task of separating aggregate energy consumption into individual appliance loads. Traditional Seq2Point models map a window of aggregate power to a single point of appliance power, but they often struggle with industrial settings due to noise and concurrent machine operation. Dilated residual networks use dilated convolutions to expand receptive fields without increasing parameters, and squeeze-and-excitation attention recalibrates channel-wise features, both of which are leveraged in SEDR-Seq2P.

## [AI Interactive Narrative System to Foster Geopolitical Reflexivity in Tech Workers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28904) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a speculative HCI design for an AI-enabled interactive narrative system aimed at encouraging geopolitical reflexivity among tech workers at geopolitically relevant technology companies. The system uses narrative interaction, archetype assignment, and socially scaffolded workshop reflection to help users critically examine their assumptions and values. This work bridges responsible innovation and geopolitical awareness, addressing a gap in existing Responsible AI approaches that rarely engage with geopolitical narratives. It could influence how tech companies integrate geopolitical considerations into their responsible technology initiatives, potentially shaping the ethical discourse in AI development. The system is designed as a speculative proposal, not a fully implemented tool, and relies on archetype assignment and workshop reflection to scaffold reflection. It draws on scholarship in International Relations, Science and Technology Studies, and reflective HCI, and aims to avoid prescriptive or moralising approaches.

## [Metasignal: Python Package for Metacognitive Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29093) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Metasignal is a newly released open-source Python package that implements 17 metacognitive measures for signal detection theory, along with reference variables like d', c, and mean confidence. It provides a single function to compute all measures from trial-level data, along with a CLI, bootstrap CIs, permutation tests, and optional hierarchical Bayesian models. This package consolidates a wide range of metacognitive measures into a single, accessible tool, which could significantly lower the barrier for researchers in psychology and neuroscience to conduct metacognition research. By standardizing these measures, it may promote consistency and comparability across studies, potentially accelerating progress in understanding decision-making and confidence. The 17 measures include three meta-d' family estimates (meta-d', M-ratio, M-difference), four nonparametric Type-2 measures (AUC2, Gamma, Phi, delta confidence) with their eight SDT-normalized forms, and two model-based measures (meta-noise, meta-uncertainty). Currently, it supports only binary (two-alternative) discrimination tasks, where stimulus and response are coded with exactly two categories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Signal detection theory (SDT) is a framework for measuring the ability to differentiate between signal and noise, commonly used in psychology and neuroscience to study perception and decision-making. Metacognition refers to the ability to monitor and evaluate one's own cognitive processes, often assessed through confidence ratings and measures like meta-d', which quantify the sensitivity of confidence judgments. This package aims to unify various metacognitive measures, making it easier for researchers to apply and compare them.

## [MBDiff: Multi-view Behavior-aware Diffusion Model for Utility Data Imputation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29177) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces MBDiff, a multi-view behavior-aware diffusion model for probabilistic imputation of missing utility data, incorporating a multi-view user behavior extraction module and a behavior-aware conditional diffusion model. Experimental results show MBDiff outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, improving 7.04% on electricity and 29.1% on water usage datasets for block missingness imputation. This work addresses a practical problem in utility data management, where missing values can impact billing accuracy and demand forecasting. By leveraging user behavior information, MBDiff offers a more accurate imputation approach that could benefit utility providers and improve operational efficiency. MBDiff consists of two key components: a multi-view user behavior extraction module that captures global, local, and instance-level views, and a behavior-aware conditional diffusion model with a reference selection module and a conditional attentional denoising network. The model was implemented and evaluated in collaboration with a major municipal utility provider in Florida.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Utility data, such as electricity, water, and gas consumption, is collected by sensors and often contains missing values due to device failures or transmission issues. Traditional imputation methods often rely on aggregated data and overlook user behavior, which can provide valuable insights. Diffusion models are a class of generative models that iteratively denoise data to generate samples, and they have been applied to various tasks including data imputation.

## [OsteoCAD: Cloud-Edge Framework for Bone Tumor Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29266) ⭐️ 6.0/10

OsteoCAD is a new human-in-the-loop cloud-edge framework that enables deep learning-based bone tumor segmentation without requiring local GPU resources or advanced technical expertise. It was validated in a real-world case study in Mexico, demonstrating its feasibility for clinical deployment. This framework addresses key barriers to AI adoption in healthcare, such as limited computational resources and lack of specialized expertise. By democratizing access to deep learning tools, it could enable more healthcare organizations to leverage AI for medical imaging, potentially improving diagnostic accuracy and efficiency. The framework provides end-to-end deep learning capabilities, including dataset creation, preprocessing, model training, and inference, through an integrated user-friendly interface. It securely connects to remote GPU infrastructures to mitigate local hardware constraints, and supports human-in-the-loop dataset scaling and exportable artifacts in standard formats.

## [DualDiT: Joint OCT Image and Segmentation Mask Generation with Diffusion Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29337) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces DualDiT, a conditional dual-output Diffusion Transformer that jointly generates OCT B-scans and segmentation masks of the upper retinal cell layers in ex vivo mouse retina. It outperforms DDPM and LDM baselines in generative quality (FID 56.14, sFID 114.35) and improves downstream segmentation performance when used for data augmentation. This work addresses the critical shortage of annotated medical imaging data, particularly in OCT of mouse eyes where manual segmentation is labor-intensive. By demonstrating that transformer-based diffusion models can effectively learn joint image-mask distributions, it opens new avenues for data augmentation in annotation-scarce medical imaging domains. DualDiT encodes both modalities into a shared latent space using a pretrained VAE, concatenates their latent representations, and performs conditional diffusion over the joint tensor. Expert evaluation showed that 46% of synthetic samples were misclassified as real, and adding generated data improved Dice and IoU scores on a held-out test set.

**Background**: Optical coherence tomography (OCT) is a non-invasive imaging technique that provides cross-sectional images of biological tissues, commonly used in ophthalmology. Segmentation of retinal layers in OCT is crucial for diagnosing and monitoring diseases, but manual annotation is time-consuming and requires expertise, leading to scarce labeled datasets. Diffusion models are a class of generative models that have shown promise in medical image synthesis, but most joint image-mask generation approaches rely on U-Net-based denoisers rather than transformers.

## [SATViz: Real-Time Visualization of Clausal Proofs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.05838) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SATViz is a new tool that visualizes CNF formulas and animates clausal proofs in real time using variable interaction graphs and force-directed layouts. It highlights variables in recently learned clauses and can adjust edge weights to create new layouts. This tool provides a novel way to observe the community structure of SAT instances and its relation to solver performance, which could aid in understanding instance hardness and improving clause quality heuristics. It offers a practical visualization aid for researchers and developers in the SAT community. SATViz uses the variable interaction graph (VIG) and a force-directed layout algorithm to visualize CNF formulas. It animates clause proofs by continuously highlighting variables in a moving window of recently learned clauses, and can recompute layouts with adjusted edge weights.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: SAT (Boolean satisfiability) is a fundamental problem in computer science, and SAT solvers are used in many applications. The community structure of SAT instances, often measured by modularity, has been linked to instance hardness and solver performance. Variable interaction graphs represent variables as nodes and edges when they appear together in a clause, and force-directed layouts arrange graphs aesthetically to reveal structure.

## [SSPP: Real-Time Human-Centered Path Planning for En-Route ATC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00064) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces Solution Space Path Planning (SSPP), a novel real-time, interpretable conflict-free path planning algorithm for en-route air traffic control. It integrates three intent-based conflict detection methods and offers two variants, SSPPV and SSPPE, with SSPPV achieving an average computation time of 3.69 ms in the Dutch Delta sector. This work addresses the gap between algorithmic research and operational adoption in tactical air traffic control by emphasizing interpretability and real-time performance. It demonstrates how human-centered design can facilitate the integration of such algorithms into future ATC systems, potentially improving safety and efficiency. SSPP extends the solution-space display concept to path planning, using vertex-based (SSPPV) and edge-based (SSPPE) search nodes. Empirical results show SSPPV with zone-based conflict detection performs best, being approximately 3.77 times faster than SSPPE while offering competitive effectiveness, making it suitable for interactive 'what-if' probing.

**Background**: Air traffic control (ATC) involves managing aircraft to maintain safe separation. Traditional conflict detection and resolution methods often rely on geometric or probabilistic approaches, but operational adoption of advanced algorithms has been limited. Solution-space displays visualize all feasible vector commands that satisfy constraints, aiding controllers in decision-making. This paper builds on that concept to create a fast, interpretable path planning algorithm.

## [Robust BAM via Subspace Rotation and Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11902) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces the Bidirectional Subspace Rotation Algorithm (B-SRA), a gradient-free training method for Bidirectional Associative Memory (BAM), along with two regularization strategies based on orthogonal weight matrices (OWM) and gradient-pattern alignment (GPA). These methods significantly improve BAM's robustness against noise and adversarial attacks compared to standard Bidirectional Backpropagation (B-BP). This work addresses a critical weakness in BAM—its sensitivity to noise and adversarial perturbations—by providing a more robust training paradigm. The findings could influence the design of resilient neural architectures, particularly in applications requiring reliable associative memory, such as pattern recognition and content-addressable storage. The authors identify two principles—orthogonal weight matrices (OWM) and gradient-pattern alignment (GPA)—as central to BAM robustness. They evaluate BAM under various attack scenarios and memory capacities (50, 100, and 200 associative pairs), finding that the SAME configuration (integrating both OWM and GPA) achieves the strongest resilience.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 4, 04:00

**Background**: Bidirectional Associative Memory (BAM) is a type of recurrent neural network introduced by Bart Kosko in 1988, designed for hetero-associative memory recall, where a pattern can retrieve a different pattern of potentially different size. Traditional training uses Bidirectional Backpropagation (B-BP), which is gradient-based and often yields models sensitive to noise and adversarial attacks. The Subspace Rotation Algorithm (SRA) is a gradient-free method based on Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) that has been previously applied to Restricted Hopfield Networks, and this paper extends it to the bidirectional case.
